13334
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not insult my Companions. Do not insult my Companions. By the One in Whose hand is my soul, if one of you were to spend in charity an amount of gold equal to Mount Uḥud, he would not attain the reward of even one mudd given by one of them, nor even half of it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2540).

13335
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
A man asked the Prophet ﷺ:
“Which people are the best?”
He replied:
“The generation in which I live, then the second, then the third.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2536).
Among the beliefs of Ahl al-Sunnah wa’l-Jamāʿah is that all of the Companions are upright and trustworthy.
This means that they did not deliberately lie about the Messenger of Allah ﷺ; it does not mean that they were incapable of forgetfulness or error.
All of them are among the people of Paradise, and if any slips occurred from any of them, they hastened to repentance and seeking forgiveness.

13336
ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr narrated:
A delegation from Banū Tamīm came to the Prophet ﷺ.
Abū Bakr said:
“Appoint al-Qaʿqāʿ ibn Maʿbad as their leader.”
ʿUmar said:
“Rather, appoint al-Aqraʿ ibn Ḥābis.”
Abū Bakr said:
“You intended nothing except to oppose me.”
ʿUmar replied:
“I did not intend to oppose you.”
They continued disputing until their voices became raised.
Then concerning this, Allah revealed:
“O you who believe, do not put yourselves forward before Allah and His Messenger…”
until the completion of the verse.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4847).

13337
Ibn Abī Mulaykah narrated:
The two best men, Abū Bakr and ʿUmar, nearly brought ruin upon themselves when they raised their voices in the presence of the Prophet ﷺ after a delegation of Banū Tamīm came to him.
One of them suggested al-Aqraʿ ibn Ḥābis, the brother of Banū Mujāshiʿ, while the other suggested another man.
Nāfiʿ said:
“I do not remember his name.”
Abū Bakr said to ʿUmar:
“You only intended to oppose me.”
ʿUmar replied:
“I did not intend to oppose you.”
Their voices rose during the disagreement.
Then Allah revealed:
“O you who believe, do not raise your voices above the voice of the Prophet…”
Ibn al-Zubayr said:
“After this verse, ʿUmar would speak so softly to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that the Prophet ﷺ sometimes had to ask him to repeat himself.”
He did not mention the same concerning his father, Abū Bakr.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4845).

13338
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
When this verse was revealed:
“O you who believe, do not raise your voices above the voice of the Prophet…”
to the end of the verse, Thābit ibn Qays remained inside his house and said:
“I am among the people of the Fire.”
He stayed away from the Prophet ﷺ.
The Prophet ﷺ asked Saʿd ibn Muʿādh:
“Abū ʿAmr, what is the matter with Thābit? Is he ill?”
Saʿd replied:
“He is my neighbor, and I do not know of him having any illness.”
Saʿd went to him and told him what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had said.
Thābit replied:
“This verse has been revealed, and you know that I am among those with the loudest voices in the presence of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Therefore I am among the people of the Fire.”
Saʿd reported this to the Prophet ﷺ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Rather, he is among the people of Paradise.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (119) and al-Bukhārī (4846).

13339
Anas narrated:
When the verse was revealed:
“O you who believe, do not raise your voices above the voice of the Prophet…”
until:
“…while you do not realize it,”
Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās was a man with a naturally loud voice.
He said:
“I am the one who used to raise my voice in the presence of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. My deeds have become worthless. I am among the people of the Fire.”
He remained with his family in sadness.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ noticed his absence, so some of the people went to him and said:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has noticed your absence. What is wrong with you?”
He replied:
“I am the one who raises his voice above the voice of the Prophet ﷺ and speaks loudly. My deeds have become worthless, and I am among the people of the Fire.”
They returned to the Prophet ﷺ and informed him of what Thābit had said.
He replied:
“No. Rather, he is among the people of Paradise.”
Anas said:
“After that, we would see him walking among us while knowing that he was one of the people of Paradise.”
When the Day of al-Yamāmah came and there was some retreat among the Muslims, Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās came forward after having applied perfume used in preparation for death and put on his shroud.
He said:
“How evil is what you are accustoming your opponents to!”
Then he fought them until he was killed.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (12399) and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7168).
Al-Bukhārī narrated in the Book of Prayer (470), through his chain from al-Sāʾib ibn Yazīd, who said:
I was standing in the mosque when a man threw a small pebble at me.
I looked and saw that it was ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb.
He said:
“Go and bring those two men to me.”
So I brought them to him.
He asked:
“Who are you two?” or, “Where are you from?”
They replied:
“We are from al-Ṭāʾif.”
He said:
“Had you been from the people of this city, I would have punished you severely. You are raising your voices in the Mosque of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ!”

13340
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
When the verse was revealed:
“Indeed, those who lower their voices in the presence of the Messenger of Allah…”
Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq said:
“By the One who revealed the Book to you, O Messenger of Allah, I will speak to you only as one speaking privately and softly until I meet Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/461) and al-Bayhaqī in Shuʿab al-Īmān (1431).

13341
Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated concerning the words of Allah:
“Indeed, most of those who call you from behind the chambers do not understand.”
A man stood and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, my praise beautifies a person, and my criticism disgraces him.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“That belongs only to Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3267), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11451), and Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (21/345).

13342
ʿUbayd ibn Rifāʿah al-Zuraqī narrated from his father:
On the Day of Uḥud, after the polytheists had withdrawn, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Form straight rows so that I may praise my Lord, Mighty and Majestic.”
They formed rows behind him, and he said:
“O Allah, all praise belongs to You. O Allah, none can withhold what You expand, none can bring near what You distance, none can distance what You bring near, none can give what You withhold, and none can withhold what You give.
O Allah, spread upon us Your blessings, Your mercy, Your bounty, and Your provision. O Allah, I ask You for lasting bliss that neither changes nor disappears. O Allah, I ask You for bliss on the day of poverty and security on the day of war.
O Allah, I seek refuge in You from the evil of what You have given us and from the evil of what You have withheld from us. O Allah, make faith beloved to us and beautify it within our hearts, and make disbelief, sinfulness, and disobedience hateful to us, and make us among the rightly guided.
O Allah, cause us to die as Muslims, keep us alive as Muslims, and join us with the righteous, neither disgraced nor put to trial.
O Allah, fight the disbelievers who turn people away from Your path and reject Your messengers, and send upon them Your punishment and chastisement. O Allah, fight the disbelievers who were given the Book, O God of Truth.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (15492), al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (699), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1800), and al-Ḥākim (1/506–507).

13343
Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb delivered a sermon at al-Jābiyah and said:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ once stood among us just as I am standing among you and said:
‘…Whoever is pleased by his good deed and saddened by his bad deed is a believer.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (114), Ibn Ḥibbān (7254), and al-Ḥākim (1/113).

13344
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
It was said to the Prophet ﷺ:
“If only you would go to ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy.”
So the Prophet ﷺ went to him riding a donkey, and the Muslims accompanied him. The ground was saline and marshy.
When the Prophet ﷺ reached him, ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy said:
“Keep away from me. By Allah, the stench of your donkey has offended me.”
A man from the Anṣār replied:
“By Allah, the donkey of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ smells better than you.”
A man from ʿAbdullāh’s people became angry on his behalf, and the supporters of each man became angry for him. They began striking one another with palm branches, their hands, and sandals.
Anas said:
“It reached us that concerning them the verse was revealed:
‘If two groups of the believers fight one another, then make peace between them.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2691) and Muslim (1799).
Regarding the words:
“If two groups of the believers fight one another, then make peace between them,”
this verse and what follows indicate that wrongful rebellion does not remove the name of faith from a person, because Allah still called them believing brothers despite the presence of transgression.
The bāghī is one who rebels against the just ruler.
Al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib acted upon this principle and brought reconciliation between two great groups of Muslims, as stated in the authentic hadith:

13345
Abū Mūsā narrated:
I heard al-Ḥasan say:
By Allah, al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī confronted Muʿāwiyah with battalions resembling mountains.
ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ said:
“I see battalions that will not retreat until they kill their counterparts.”
Muʿāwiyah—who, by Allah, was the better of the two men—said to him:
“ʿAmr, if these kill those and those kill these, who will take care of the affairs of the people? Who will take care of their women? Who will take care of their dependents and property?”
He sent two men from Quraysh, from Banū ʿAbd Shams, to al-Ḥasan: ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Samurah and ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Kurayz.
He said:
“Go to this man, make him an offer, speak to him, and ask him to accept.”
They went to al-Ḥasan, entered upon him, spoke to him, and made their request.
Al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī said:
“We are Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, and we have acquired some of this wealth, while this Ummah has been spilling one another’s blood.”
They said:
“He offers you such-and-such and asks this of you.”
Al-Ḥasan asked:
“Who will guarantee this for me?”
They replied:
“We will guarantee it for you.”
Whatever he asked of them, they said:
“We guarantee it for you.”
So he made peace with Muʿāwiyah.
Al-Ḥasan then said:
I heard Abū Bakrah say:
I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the pulpit with al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī beside him. He would turn toward the people at one moment and toward al-Ḥasan at another, saying:
“This son of mine is a leader, and perhaps Allah will bring reconciliation through him between two great groups of Muslims.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2704).
Regarding the words:
“If one of them transgresses against the other, then fight the one that transgresses until it returns to the command of Allah”:

13346
Anas narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Support your brother whether he is an oppressor or oppressed.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, we understand supporting him when he is oppressed, but how do we support him when he is an oppressor?”
He replied:
“You restrain his hands from oppression.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2444).
Regarding the words:
“And act justly. Indeed, Allah loves those who act justly”:

13347
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Indeed, those who act justly will be with Allah upon pulpits of light, at the right hand of the Most Merciful, Mighty and Majestic—and both of His hands are right—those who are just in their judgments, toward their families, and in whatever they are entrusted with.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1827).
Regarding the words:
“The believers are but brothers,”
this brotherhood entails rights, responsibilities, and duties that every believer should fulfill in the best possible manner.

13348
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The Muslim is the brother of the Muslim. He does not wrong him nor abandon him. Whoever attends to the need of his brother, Allah will attend to his need. Whoever relieves a Muslim of a hardship, Allah will relieve him of one of the hardships of the Day of Resurrection. And whoever conceals the faults of a Muslim, Allah will conceal his faults on the Day of Resurrection.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2442) and Muslim (2580).

13349
Abū al-Dardāʾ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is no Muslim servant who supplicates for his brother in his absence except that the angel says: ‘And for you the same.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2732).

13350
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever relieves a believer of one of the hardships of this world, Allah will relieve him of one of the hardships of the Day of Resurrection. Whoever makes things easy for someone in difficulty, Allah will make things easy for him in this world and the Hereafter. Whoever conceals the faults of a Muslim, Allah will conceal his faults in this world and the Hereafter. Allah continues to help the servant for as long as the servant continues to help his brother…”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2699).

13351
Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The believer in relation to another believer is like a building, each part strengthening the other.”
He then interlaced his fingers.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2446) and Muslim (2585).

13352
Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The example of the believers in their mutual love, mercy, and compassion is like that of a single body. When one limb suffers, the rest of the body responds with sleeplessness and fever.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6011) and Muslim (2586).

13353
Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A believer among the people of faith is like the head in relation to the body. The believer feels pain for the people of faith just as the body feels pain because of what afflicts the head.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22877), whose wording is given here, al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (6/160–161), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (4693).

13354
Abū Jubayrah ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk narrated:
This verse was revealed concerning us.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Madinah, every man among us had two or three names.
When the Prophet ﷺ called a man by one of those names, we would say:
“O Messenger of Allah, he becomes angry when he is called by that name.”
Then Allah revealed:
“Do not call one another by offensive nicknames. Evil indeed is the name of wickedness after faith, and whoever does not repent—it is they who are the wrongdoers.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4962), al-Tirmidhī (3268), Ibn Mājah (3741), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (5709) and al-Ḥākim (2/463).

13355
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Beware of suspicion, for suspicion is the falsest of speech. Do not spy, do not seek out one another’s faults, do not compete against one another in a blameworthy way, do not envy one another, do not hate one another, and do not turn your backs on one another. Rather, be servants of Allah as brothers.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Ḥusn al-Khuluq (15), al-Bukhārī (6066), and Muslim (2563).

13356
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not envy one another, do not artificially inflate prices against one another, do not hate one another, do not turn your backs on one another, and do not enter into a sale after another has already concluded a sale. Rather, be servants of Allah as brothers.
The Muslim is the brother of the Muslim: he does not wrong him, abandon him, or look down upon him. God-consciousness is here.”
He pointed to his chest three times.
Then he said:
“It is sufficient evil for a person to look down upon his Muslim brother. Everything belonging to a Muslim is inviolable to another Muslim: his blood, his wealth, and his honor.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2564).

13357
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not hate one another, do not envy one another, do not turn your backs on one another, and be servants of Allah as brothers. It is not lawful for a Muslim to abandon his brother for more than three nights.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Ḥusn al-Khuluq (14), al-Bukhārī (6076), and Muslim (2559).

13358
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do you know what backbiting is?”
They replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“It is mentioning your brother in a way that he dislikes.”
Someone asked:
“What if what I say about my brother is actually true?”
He replied:
“If what you say is true, then you have backbitten him; and if it is not true, then you have slandered him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2589).

13359
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
I imitated a man in the presence of the Prophet ﷺ.
He said:
“I would not like to imitate a person, even if I were given such-and-such in return.”
She said:
I then said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Ṣafiyyah is a woman who is…”
and she gestured with her hand, apparently meaning that she was short.
He said:
“You have uttered a word which, if it were mixed with the water of the sea, would corrupt it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4875), al-Tirmidhī (2502, 2503), and Aḥmad (25560).

13360
Abū Barzah al-Aslamī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“O people who have believed with their tongues while faith has not entered their hearts, do not backbite the Muslims and do not pursue their private faults. Whoever pursues their faults, Allah will pursue his faults; and whoever Allah pursues for his faults, He will disgrace him even within his own home.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4880), Aḥmad (19776), Abū Yaʿlā (7423), and al-Bayhaqī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (10/247).

13361
Al-Barāʾ narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ delivered a sermon to us in such a loud voice that even the young women in their homes—or, he said, in their private chambers—could hear him.
He said:
“O people who have believed with their tongues, do not backbite the Muslims and do not pursue their faults. Whoever pursues the fault of his brother, Allah will pursue his fault; and whoever Allah pursues for his fault, He will disgrace him inside his own home.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (1675).

13362
Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ascended the pulpit and called out in a loud voice:
“O people who have submitted with their tongues while faith has not reached their hearts, do not harm the Muslims, do not shame them, and do not pursue their private faults. Whoever pursues the fault of his Muslim brother, Allah will pursue his fault; and whoever Allah pursues for his fault, He will disgrace him even inside his own dwelling.”
Ibn ʿUmar once looked at the House—or the Kaʿbah—and said:
“How magnificent you are, and how great your sanctity! Yet the believer is even more sacred to Allah than you.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2032) and Ibn Ḥibbān (5763).

13363
Jābir narrated:
A foul-smelling wind arose during the time of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Some of the hypocrites have been backbiting some of the Muslims, and this wind has been sent because of that.”
In another narration from him:
We were with the Prophet ﷺ when the stench of a decaying carcass arose.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do you know what this smell is? This is the smell of those who backbite the believers.”
Authentic: The first narration was narrated by ʿAbd ibn Ḥumayd (1028), al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (733), and al-Kharāʾiṭī in Masāwiʾ al-Akhlāq (187).
The second narration: Narrated by Aḥmad (14784) and al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (732).

13364
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When I was taken up through the heavens, I passed by people who had fingernails of copper with which they were scratching their faces and chests.
I asked: ‘Who are these, O Jibrīl?’
He replied: ‘These are the people who used to eat the flesh of others and attack their honor.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4878) and Aḥmad (13340).

13365
Anas narrated:
The Arabs used to serve one another while traveling. Abū Bakr and ʿUmar had a man with them who served them.
He fell asleep. When they woke up, he had not prepared any food for them.
One of them said:
“This man of yours certainly sleeps a great deal.”
They woke him and said:
“Go to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and tell him: ‘Abū Bakr and ʿUmar send you their salām, and they ask you for something to eat with their bread.’”
The man went and conveyed the message.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Tell them that they have already eaten something with their bread.”
They became alarmed and came to the Prophet ﷺ.
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we sent asking you for something to eat with our bread, and you said that we had already eaten something with it. What did we eat?”
He replied:
“You ate the flesh of your brother. Indeed, I can see his flesh between your teeth.”
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, seek forgiveness for us.”
He replied:
“Let him seek forgiveness for you.”
Good: Narrated by al-Kharāʾiṭī in Masāwiʾ al-Akhlāq (186) and al-Ḍiyāʾ al-Maqdisī in al-Mukhtārah (1696, 1697).

13366
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said regarding the words:
“And We made you peoples and tribes”:
“The shuʿūb are the great tribal groupings, while the qabāʾil are their subdivisions.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3489).
According to this meaning, the words of Allah are understood:
“We divided them into twelve tribal communities.” [Sūrat al-Aʿrāf 7:160]
and likewise:
“We appointed from among them twelve leaders.” [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:12]
For the people of Banū Isrāʾīl constituted a larger people composed of various subdivisions.
It has also been said that shuʿūb are peoples who are not traced to a particular ancestor but are instead associated with cities and towns, whereas tribes trace their lineage to their forefathers.

13367
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Learn enough about your genealogies to enable you to maintain your family ties, for maintaining family ties brings love among relatives, increase in wealth, and extension of one’s lifespan.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1979), Aḥmad (8868), and al-Ḥākim (4/161).

13368
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah does not look at your appearances or your wealth; rather, He looks at your hearts and your deeds.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2564:34).

13369
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ was asked:
“Who is the most honorable of people?”
He replied:
“The one among them who has the greatest God-consciousness.”
They said:
“That is not what we are asking you about.”
He said:
“Then Yūsuf: the Prophet of Allah, son of a Prophet of Allah, son of a Prophet of Allah, son of the intimate friend of Allah.”
They said:
“That is not what we are asking you about.”
He then said:
“Are you asking me about the noble lineages of the Arabs? The best of them in the pre-Islamic period are the best of them in Islam, if they gain understanding of the religion.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3353) and Muslim (2378).

13370
Abū Naḍrah narrated from someone who heard the sermon of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during the middle of the Days of Tashrīq.
He said:
“O people, indeed your Lord is One and your father is one. There is no superiority of an Arab over a non-Arab, nor of a non-Arab over an Arab; nor of a red person over a black person, nor of a black person over a red person, except through God-consciousness. Have I conveyed the message?”
They replied:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has conveyed it.”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (23489). Its chain is authentic, and its detailed discussion is given in the Book of Ḥajj.

13371
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“These lineages of yours are not a cause for insulting anyone. You are all the children of Adam, like a measure that has not even been completely filled. No one has superiority over another except through religion or righteous deeds. It is enough disgrace for a man that he be obscene, foul-mouthed, miserly, and cowardly.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17313).
Regarding the expression:
“Like a measure that has not been completely filled,”
it means that all of you, in being descended from the same father, stand on the same level and none reaches absolute perfection over another merely through lineage. It is like an amount being measured that does not even completely fill the measuring vessel.

13372
Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gave gifts to a group of men while Saʿd was sitting with him, but he left out a man who was the most admirable of them to me.
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, why did you leave out so-and-so? By Allah, I consider him to be a believer.”
He said:
“Or a Muslim?”
I remained silent for a short while, but what I knew about the man overcame me, so I repeated:
“Why did you leave out so-and-so? By Allah, I consider him to be a believer.”
He again said:
“Or a Muslim?”
Then what I knew about him again overcame me, and I repeated my statement. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ repeated his reply and then said:
“O Saʿd, I may give to one man even though another is more beloved to me than he is, out of fear that Allah might cast him face-first into the Fire.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (27) and Muslim (150).

13373
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
A delegation from Banū Asad came to the Prophet ﷺ. They spoke eloquently and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, all of Muḍar fought against you, but we did not fight you. We are not fewer than them in number, nor weaker than them in strength, and we maintained ties of kinship with you.”
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ heard their words, he said to Abū Bakr and ʿUmar:
“Do they really speak in this manner?”
It was said:
“O Messenger of Allah, their understanding is indeed limited, and Satan certainly speaks upon their tongues.”
Another narration adds:
Then this verse was revealed:
“They regard their acceptance of Islam as a favor to you. Say: ‘Do not regard your Islam as a favor to me. Rather, Allah has favored you by guiding you to faith, if you are truthful.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11455), Abū Yaʿlā (2363), and al-Bazzār (5141).

13374
Jābir ibn Samurah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ used to recite in the dawn prayer: “Qāf. By the Glorious Qur’an,” and thereafter his prayer was relatively light.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (458).

13375
Quṭbah ibn Mālik narrated:
I prayed while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ led us in prayer. He recited:
“Qāf. By the Glorious Qur’an,”
until he reached:
“And lofty date-palms…”
He said:
“I kept repeating the phrase to myself, but I did not know exactly what he had said.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (457).

13376
Umm Hishām bint Ḥārithah ibn al-Nuʿmān narrated:
“Our oven and the oven of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ were one and the same for two years, or for one year and part of another year. I learned ‘Qāf. By the Glorious Qur’an’ only from the tongue of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, because he used to recite it every Friday from the pulpit when addressing the people.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (873:52).

13377
ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUtbah ibn Masʿūd narrated:
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb asked Abū Wāqid al-Laythī:
“What did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recite in the prayers of al-Aḍḥā and al-Fiṭr?”
He replied:
“He used to recite ‘Qāf. By the Glorious Qur’an’ and ‘The Hour has drawn near, and the moon has split.’” [Sūrat al-Qamar 54:1]
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik (8) and Muslim (891).

13378
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah said: ‘The son of Adam has denied Me, though he had no right to do so, and he has insulted Me, though he had no right to do so.
As for his denial of Me, it is his statement: “He will not bring me back as He created me the first time.” Yet the first creation is no easier for Me than recreating it.
As for his insulting Me, it is his statement that Allah has taken a son. But I am the One, the Self-Sufficient Master. I neither beget nor was I begotten, and there is none comparable to Me.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Tafsīr (4974), from Abū al-Yamān, from Shuʿayb, from Abū al-Zinād, from al-Aʿraj, from Abū Hurayrah.

13379
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah has pardoned my Ummah for what their souls whisper to them, so long as they do not act upon it or speak of it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5269) and Muslim (127).

13380
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A servant may utter a word pleasing to Allah without giving it much thought, and Allah raises him many degrees because of it. And a servant may utter a word that angers Allah without giving it much thought, and because of it he falls into Hell.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6478).

13381
ʿAlqamah ibn Waqqāṣ narrated:
A man of status passed by him, and ʿAlqamah said to him:
“You have ties of kinship and you have a recognized position. I have seen you enter upon these rulers and speak in their presence whatever Allah wills for you to speak. I heard Bilāl ibn al-Ḥārith al-Muzanī, the Companion of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, say that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
‘One of you may utter a word pleasing to Allah, never imagining that it will reach the level that it reaches, yet because of it Allah records His pleasure with him until the Day of Resurrection.
And one of you may utter a word that angers Allah, never imagining that it will reach the level that it reaches, yet because of it Allah records His anger against him until the day he meets Him.’”
ʿAlqamah then said:
“So consider carefully—may you be mindful—what you say and what you speak. Many a statement I have refrained from making because of what I heard from Bilāl ibn al-Ḥārith.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2319), Ibn Mājah (3969), Aḥmad (15852), Ibn Ḥibbān (280), and al-Ḥākim (1/45).

13382
Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever guarantees for me what is between his two jaws and what is between his two legs, I guarantee Paradise for him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6474).

13383
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day should speak good or remain silent. Whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day must not harm his neighbor. And whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day should honor his guest.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6475) and Muslim (47). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13384
Abū Shurayḥ al-Khuzāʿī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day should treat his neighbor well. Whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day should honor his guest. And whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day should speak good or remain silent.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (48) and al-Bukhārī (6476).

13385
ʿĀʾishah used to say:
There was a small water vessel—or a container containing water—in front of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ; ʿUmar, the narrator, was uncertain which of the two it was.
The Prophet ﷺ would put his hands into the water, wipe his face with them, and say:
“There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah. Indeed, death has agonies.”
Then he raised his hand and began saying:
“With the Highest Companion.”
He continued saying this until his soul was taken and his hand fell.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6510).

13386
Abū Saʿīd narrated that the Prophet of Allah ﷺ said:
“A neck will emerge from the Fire and speak, saying: ‘Today I have been assigned to three: every arrogant tyrant, whoever set up another deity alongside Allah, and whoever killed a person without just cause.’
It will then wrap itself around them and cast them into the depths of Hell.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (11354), Ibn Abī Shaybah (35278), Abū Yaʿlā (1146), and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (3500).

13387
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Paradise and the Fire disputed with one another.
The Fire said: ‘I have been favored with the arrogant and the tyrannical.’
Paradise said: ‘Why is it that none enter me except the weak and those considered insignificant among the people?’
Allah, Blessed and Exalted, said to Paradise: ‘You are My mercy; through you I show mercy to whomever I will among My servants.’
And He said to the Fire: ‘You are only My punishment; through you I punish whomever I will among My servants.’
Each of them will have its full measure. As for the Fire, it will not become full until He places His Foot upon it, whereupon it will say: ‘Enough! Enough! Enough!’ Then it will become full, and its parts will draw together. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, wrongs none of His creation.
As for Paradise, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will create a creation for it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4850) and Muslim (2848:36).

13388
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Paradise and the Fire brought their dispute before their Lord.
Paradise said: ‘My Lord, why is it that only the weak and those considered insignificant among the people enter me?’
The Fire said that it had been favored with the arrogant.
Allah, the Exalted, said to Paradise: ‘You are My mercy.’
And He said to the Fire: ‘You are My punishment; through you I afflict whomever I will. Each of you will have its full measure.’
As for Paradise, Allah does not wrong any of His creation. And He creates for the Fire whomever He wills; they are cast into it, and it says, ‘Is there any more?’ three times, until He places His Foot upon it. Then it becomes full, its parts draw together, and it says: ‘Enough! Enough! Enough!’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7449) and Muslim (2846).

13389
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Prophet of Allah ﷺ said:
“Hell will continue saying, ‘Is there any more?’ until the Lord of Might, Blessed and Exalted, places His Foot upon it. Then it will say: ‘Enough, enough, by Your might!’ and its parts will draw together.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6661) and Muslim (2848).

13390
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Paradise and the Fire disputed.
The Fire said: ‘Within me are the tyrants and the arrogant.’
Paradise said: ‘Within me are the weak and poor among the people.’
Then Allah judged between them, saying: ‘You, Paradise, are My mercy; through you I show mercy to whomever I will. And you, Fire, are My punishment; through you I punish whomever I will. It is incumbent upon Me to fill each of you.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2847) and Aḥmad (11754). The wording is that of Aḥmad.

13391
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“If a believer desires a child in Paradise, its conception, birth, and growth to the age he desires will all take place within a single hour, exactly as he wishes.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2563), Ibn Mājah (4338), Aḥmad (11063), and Ibn Ḥibbān (7404).
This hadith indicates that pregnancy and childbirth can occur in Paradise for whoever among the people of Paradise wishes for that, and the general meanings of the Qur’anic texts support what is stated in this hadith.
Regarding the words:
“And with Us is even more,”
this is similar to the words of Allah:
“For those who do good is the best reward and even more.” [Sūrat Yūnus 10:26]
The “more” refers to looking upon the noble Face of Allah, as stated in the authentic hadith:

13392
Ṣuhayb narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“When the people of Paradise enter Paradise, Allah, Blessed and Exalted, will say: ‘Do you desire anything more that I may give you?’
They will say: ‘Have You not brightened our faces? Have You not admitted us into Paradise and saved us from the Fire?’
Then He will remove the veil, and they will not have been given anything more beloved to them than looking upon their Lord, Mighty and Majestic.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (181).
Muslim also narrated it through another route with the addition:
Then the Prophet ﷺ recited:
“For those who do good is the best reward and even more.” [Sūrat Yūnus 10:26]

13393
Jarīr ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
One night we were sitting with the Prophet ﷺ. He looked at the full moon on the fourteenth night of the month and said:
“You will certainly see your Lord just as you see this moon, and you will not crowd or struggle in order to see Him. So if you are able not to be overcome and prevented from performing a prayer before sunrise and a prayer before sunset, then do so.”
Then he recited:
“Glorify your Lord with praise before sunrise and before sunset.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4851) and Muslim (633).
Regarding the words:
“Glorify your Lord with praise,”
the meaning is: perform prayer in praise of Him.
Regarding:
“Before sunrise,”
this refers to the dawn prayer.
Regarding:
“And before sunset,”
this refers to the prayers occurring after sunrise and before sunset—namely, Ẓuhr and ʿAṣr.
Regarding:
“And during part of the night, glorify Him,”
this refers to the Maghrib and ʿIshāʾ prayers.
Regarding:
“And after the prostrations,”
this means to glorify Allah after the prescribed prayers. This is stated in the authentic hadith:

13394
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever says ‘Subḥān Allah’ thirty-three times after every prayer, ‘al-ḥamdu lillāh’ thirty-three times, and ‘Allāhu akbar’ thirty-three times—that makes ninety-nine—and completes the hundred by saying:
‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah alone, without partner. To Him belongs all dominion and all praise, and He has power over all things,’
his sins will be forgiven even if they are like the foam of the sea.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (597).

13395
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The poor came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“The wealthy have taken away the highest ranks and everlasting bliss. They pray as we pray and fast as we fast, but they have surplus wealth with which they perform Ḥajj and ʿUmrah, strive in Allah’s cause, and give charity.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Shall I not tell you of something which, if you practice it, you will catch up with those who have surpassed you, no one after you will catch up with you, and you will be the best among those in whose midst you live, except someone who does the same?
Say ‘Subḥān Allah,’ ‘al-ḥamdu lillāh,’ and ‘Allāhu akbar’ thirty-three times after every prayer.”
We then differed among ourselves.
Some of us said:
“We should say ‘Subḥān Allah’ thirty-three times, ‘al-ḥamdu lillāh’ thirty-three times, and ‘Allāhu akbar’ thirty-four times.”
I returned to the Prophet ﷺ, and he said:
“Say ‘Subḥān Allah,’ ‘al-ḥamdu lillāh,’ and ‘Allāhu akbar,’ until each of them has been said thirty-three times.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (843) and Muslim (595).

13396
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said:
“He was commanded to glorify Allah after all the prayers,”
meaning the words of Allah:
“And after the prostrations.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4852).

13397
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I am the leader of the children of Adam on the Day of Resurrection, the first whose grave will split open, the first to intercede, and the first whose intercession will be accepted.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2278).
Regarding the words:
“That is a gathering easy for Us,”
bringing people out of their graves, restoring them to life after their death, recreating them, and gathering them for reckoning and recompense is easy and effortless for Allah.
Allah says:
“Our command is but one, like the blink of an eye.” [Sūrat al-Qamar 54:50]
And Allah says:
“Your creation and your resurrection are only like that of a single soul.” [Sūrat Luqmān 31:28]
That takes place through His command:
“Be.”
As Allah says:
“His command, when He intends a thing, is only that He says to it, ‘Be,’ and it is.” [Sūrat Yā Sīn 36:82]

13398
ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Madinah, the people rushed toward him, and it was announced:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has arrived! The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has arrived! The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has arrived!”
So I went among the people to look at him.
When I clearly saw the face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, I knew that his face was not the face of a liar.
The first words I heard him say were:
“O people, spread salām, feed others, and pray while the people are asleep, and you will enter Paradise in peace.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2485) and Ibn Mājah (1334).

13399
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah, Blessed and Exalted, descends every night to the lowest heaven when the final third of the night remains and says: ‘Who is calling upon Me so that I may answer him? Who is asking Me so that I may give him? Who is seeking My forgiveness so that I may forgive him?’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Kitāb al-Qurʾān (30), al-Bukhārī (1145), and Muslim (758).
The hadiths concerning this meaning are numerous, and they have been mentioned in the Book of Faith and the Book of Supplications and Remembrances.

13400
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The truly needy person is not this one who goes around asking people and is turned away with one or two morsels or one or two dates.”
They asked:
“Then who is the truly needy person, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“The one who does not possess enough to satisfy his needs, yet people do not realize his condition and therefore do not give charity to him, and he does not stand up and ask people.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in the Book of Ṣifat al-Nabī ﷺ (7), al-Bukhārī (1479), and Muslim (1039).

13401
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I was granted victory through the east wind, and ʿĀd were destroyed by the west wind.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (900).
The word al-dabūr refers to a wind that blows from the west. Its opposite is al-qabūl, namely the east wind known as al-ṣabā.

13402
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah, the Exalted, says: ‘O son of Adam, devote yourself to My worship and I will fill your heart with richness and remove your poverty. If you do not, I will fill your hands with preoccupation and will not remove your poverty.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2466), who graded it good, Ibn Mājah (4107), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (393), and al-Ḥākim (2/443).

13403
Umm Salamah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
I complained to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that I was unwell.
He said:
“Perform ṭawāf behind the people while riding.”
She said:
So I performed ṭawāf riding my camel while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was praying beside the House and reciting:
“By the Mount, and by a Book inscribed.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Kitāb al-Ḥajj (123), al-Bukhārī (464), and Muslim (1276).

13404
Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim narrated:
“I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recite Sūrat al-Ṭūr in the Maghrib prayer.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Kitāb al-Ṣalāh (25), al-Bukhārī (765), and Muslim (463).

13405
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said in the lengthy hadith of the Night Journey:
“Then I was taken up to the seventh heaven. Jibrīl asked for it to be opened. It was asked: ‘Who is this?’ He replied: ‘Jibrīl.’ It was asked: ‘Who is with you?’ He replied: ‘Muḥammad ﷺ.’ It was asked: ‘Has he been sent for?’ He replied: ‘Yes, he has been sent for.’
Then it was opened for us, and there I saw Ibrāhīm ﷺ reclining with his back against al-Bayt al-Maʿmūr. Seventy thousand angels enter it every day, never returning to it again…”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (162).

13406
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The House in heaven is called al-Ḍirāḥ. It is directly corresponding to the structure of this Sacred House, such that if it were to fall, it would fall directly upon it. Seventy thousand angels enter it every day, and they never return to it again.”
Good: Narrated by al-Azraqī in Akhbār Makkah (1/49).

13407
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will raise the rank of a righteous servant in Paradise. He will say: ‘My Lord, how did I attain this?’ Allah will say: ‘Through your child’s seeking forgiveness for you.’”
In another wording:
“Through your child’s supplication for you.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (3660), Aḥmad (10610), and al-Bazzār (9024).

13408
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When Quraysh gathered in Dār al-Nadwah to discuss what they should do about the Prophet ﷺ, one of them said:
“Confine him in chains and wait for the calamities of time to overtake him until he perishes, just as the poets before him—such as Zuhayr and al-Nābighah—perished. He is only one of them.”
Then Allah, the Exalted, revealed concerning their statement:
“Or do they say, ‘He is a poet for whom we await a misfortune of time’?”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (11/134–135).

13409
Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim narrated:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ reciting Sūrat al-Ṭūr in the Maghrib prayer.
When he reached the verses:
“Or were they created from nothing, or are they themselves the creators? Or did they create the heavens and the earth? Rather, they have no certainty. Or do they possess the treasures of your Lord, or are they the controllers?”
my heart nearly flew from my chest.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4854) and Muslim (463).

13410
ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The two rakʿahs before Fajr are better than the world and everything in it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (725).

13411
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
“The Prophet ﷺ was not more persistent in observing any voluntary prayer than he was in observing the two rakʿahs before Fajr.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1169) and Muslim (724:94).

13412
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated:
I used to write down everything I heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ because I wanted to preserve it.
Quraysh forbade me and said:
“Do you write down everything you hear from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, even though the Messenger of Allah ﷺ is a human being who speaks both when angry and when pleased?”
So I stopped writing.
I mentioned this to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He pointed with his finger to his mouth and said:
“Write. By the One in Whose hand is my soul, nothing comes out of this except the truth.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3646), Aḥmad (6510), and al-Ḥākim (1/105–106).

13413
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, you joke with us.”
He replied:
“Indeed, I say nothing except the truth.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (1990), in al-Shamāʾil (237), and by Aḥmad (8723).

13414
Abū Umāmah narrated that he heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Through the intercession of a man who is not a prophet, people equal in number to the two great tribal groups—or one of the two great tribal groups—Rabīʿah and Muḍar, will certainly enter Paradise.”
A man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, is Rabīʿah not part of Muḍar?”
He replied:
“I only say what I say.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (22215) and al-Ājurrī in al-Sharīʿah (817).

13415
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whatever I inform you is from Allah is that concerning which there is no doubt.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (8900).

13416
Masrūq narrated:
I was reclining in the presence of ʿĀʾishah when she said:
“O Abū ʿĀʾishah, there are three matters: whoever claims any one of them has uttered a tremendous falsehood against Allah.”
I asked:
“What are they?”
She said:
“Whoever claims that Muḥammad ﷺ saw his Lord has uttered a tremendous falsehood against Allah.”
I had been reclining, but I sat upright and said:
“Mother of the Believers, give me time and do not be hasty with me. Did Allah, Mighty and Majestic, not say:
‘He certainly saw him on the clear horizon’ [Sūrat al-Takwīr 81:23]
and:
‘And he certainly saw him another time’ [Sūrat al-Najm 53:13]?”
She replied:
“I was the first person of this Ummah to ask the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about that. He said:
‘It was only Jibrīl. I did not see him in the form in which he was created except on those two occasions. I saw him descending from the heaven, the greatness of his form filling the space between heaven and earth.’”
She then said:
“Have you not heard that Allah says:
‘Vision does not encompass Him, but He encompasses all vision, and He is the Subtle, the All-Aware.’ [Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:103]
And have you not heard that Allah says:
‘It is not for any human being that Allah should speak to him except by revelation, or from behind a veil, or by sending a messenger who reveals by His permission whatever He wills. Indeed, He is Most High, All-Wise.’ [Sūrat al-Shūrā 42:51]?”
She continued:
“Whoever claims that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concealed anything from the Book of Allah has uttered a tremendous falsehood against Allah, for Allah says:
‘O Messenger, convey what has been revealed to you from your Lord; and if you do not, then you have not conveyed His message.’ [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:67]
And whoever claims that he could inform people of what would happen tomorrow has uttered a tremendous falsehood against Allah, for Allah says:
‘Say: None in the heavens and earth knows the unseen except Allah.’ [Sūrat al-Naml 27:65]”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (177).

13417
Masrūq narrated:
I asked ʿĀʾishah, may Allah be pleased with her:
“My mother, did Muḥammad ﷺ see his Lord?”
She replied:
“My hair has stood on end because of what you have said! There are three matters; whoever tells you any of them has lied.
Whoever tells you that Muḥammad ﷺ saw his Lord has lied.”
Then she recited:
“Vision does not encompass Him, but He encompasses all vision, and He is the Subtle, the All-Aware.” [Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:103]
and:
“It is not for any human being that Allah should speak to him except by revelation or from behind a veil.” [Sūrat al-Shūrā 42:51]
She continued:
“Whoever tells you that he knows what will happen tomorrow has lied.”
Then she recited:
“No soul knows what it will earn tomorrow.” [Sūrat Luqmān 31:34]
She continued:
“And whoever tells you that he concealed anything has lied.”
Then she recited:
“O Messenger, convey what has been revealed to you from your Lord…” [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:67]
Then she said:
“Rather, he saw Jibrīl in his true form twice.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4855) and Muslim (177:289).

13418
Abū Isḥāq al-Shaybānī narrated:
I asked Zirr ibn Ḥubaysh about the words of Allah:
“Then he was at a distance of two bow-lengths or nearer. Then He revealed to His servant what He revealed.”
He replied:
Ibn Masʿūd narrated to us:
“He saw Jibrīl, who had six hundred wings.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3232) and Muslim (174).

13419
Abū Hurayrah said regarding the verse:
“And he certainly saw him another time”:
“He saw Jibrīl.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (175).

13420
Ibn Masʿūd said regarding the verse:
“He certainly saw some of the greatest signs of his Lord”:
“He saw a vast green covering that filled the horizon.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4858).

13421
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was taken on the Night Journey, he was brought to the Lote Tree of the Utmost Boundary.
It is in the sixth heaven. Whatever ascends from the earth reaches it and is received there, and whatever descends from above reaches it and is received there.
Regarding the words:
“When there covered the lote tree that which covered it,”
Ibn Masʿūd said:
“Golden moths.”
He continued:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was given three things:
He was given the five prayers, he was given the concluding verses of Sūrat al-Baqarah, and the destructive major sins were forgiven for those of his Ummah who did not associate anything with Allah.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (173).

13422
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“Al-Lāt was a man who used to prepare sawīq for the pilgrims.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4859).
As for al-ʿUzzā, it was located at Nakhlah, a place between Makkah and al-Ṭāʾif. Quraysh used to venerate it, just as Abū Sufyān boasted of it and glorified it on the Day of Uḥud, as stated in the authentic hadith:

13423
Al-Barāʾ narrated:
On that day we met the polytheists, and the Prophet ﷺ stationed a force of archers and appointed ʿAbdullāh over them. He said:
“Do not leave your position. If you see us overcoming them, do not leave your position, and if you see them overcoming us, do not come to our aid.”
When we met the enemy, they fled, until I saw their women running up the mountain, lifting their garments from their shins so that their anklets were visible.
The Muslims began saying:
“The spoils! The spoils!”
ʿAbdullāh said:
“The Prophet ﷺ entrusted me that you must not leave your position.”
But they refused.
When they disobeyed, the Muslims were turned back, and seventy men were killed.
Abū Sufyān then appeared above them and called:
“Is Muḥammad among the people?”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not answer him.”
He asked:
“Is the son of Abū Quḥāfah among the people?”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not answer him.”
He asked:
“Is Ibn al-Khaṭṭāb among the people?”
Then he said:
“These men have been killed. Had they been alive, they would have answered.”
ʿUmar could no longer restrain himself and said:
“You have lied, enemy of Allah! Allah has preserved for you what will disgrace you.”
Abū Sufyān said:
“Exalted be Hubal!”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Answer him.”
They asked:
“What should we say?”
He replied:
“Say: Allah is Higher and More Majestic.”
Abū Sufyān then said:
“We have al-ʿUzzā, while you have no ʿUzzā.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Answer him.”
They asked:
“What should we say?”
He replied:
“Say: Allah is our Protector, and you have no protector.”
Abū Sufyān said:
“A day for a day of Badr. War alternates in fortune. You will find mutilation among your dead which I did not order, though it did not displease me.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4043).

13424
Abū al-Ṭufayl narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ conquered Makkah, he sent Khālid ibn al-Walīd to Nakhlah, where al-ʿUzzā was located.
Khālid went there. It stood on the mounds among the samurah trees. He cut down the trees and demolished the structure that had been built over it.
Then he returned to the Prophet ﷺ and informed him.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Go back, for you have done nothing.”
Khālid returned.
When the custodians of al-ʿUzzā saw him, they fled deep into the mountain, crying:
“O ʿUzzā, afflict him with madness! O ʿUzzā, blind him! Otherwise die in humiliation!”
Khālid approached and found a naked woman with disheveled hair throwing dust over her head.
He struck her with his sword until he killed her.
Then he returned to the Prophet ﷺ and informed him.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“That was al-ʿUzzā.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (902), whose wording is given here, and al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11483).

13425
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever swears and says in his oath, ‘By al-Lāt and al-ʿUzzā,’ should say: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah.’ And whoever says to his companion, ‘Come, let me gamble with you,’ should give charity.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4860) and Muslim (1647).

13426
Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
I swore by al-Lāt and al-ʿUzzā.
My companions said:
“You have uttered an improper statement.”
So I went to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“The time since Jāhiliyyah is still recent for me, and I swore by al-Lāt and al-ʿUzzā.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Say three times: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah alone.’ Then spit lightly to your left three times, seek refuge with Allah, and do not do it again.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (3776), Ibn Mājah (2097), Aḥmad (1589), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4364).
The meaning of the hadith is that whoever swears by al-Lāt and al-ʿUzzā has spoken as though he were setting up a rival alongside Allah. Therefore, he should correct himself by saying three times:
“There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah alone,”
and seek refuge with Allah from Satan, the accursed.
By doing so, he returns to the affirmation of pure monotheism and removes Satanic whispering from himself.
As for Manāt, it was located at al-Mushallal between Makkah and Madinah.
Khuzāʿah, al-Aws, and al-Khazraj used to venerate it during the pre-Islamic period, and they would enter the state of pilgrimage from there when intending the Sacred House, as stated in the authentic hadith:

13427
ʿUrwah narrated:
I asked ʿĀʾishah, may Allah be pleased with her:
“What do you think of the words of Allah:
‘Indeed, al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah are among the symbols of Allah. So whoever performs Ḥajj to the House or performs ʿUmrah, there is no blame upon him for going between them.’ [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:158]
By Allah, according to this, there would be no blame upon anyone who did not go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah.”
She replied:
“What you have said is very wrong, son of my sister. Had the verse meant what you have interpreted it to mean, it would have said: ‘There is no blame upon him if he does not go between them.’
Rather, it was revealed concerning the Anṣār. Before accepting Islam, they used to enter the state of pilgrimage for Manāt, the false deity which they worshipped at al-Mushallal. Those who had done so considered it sinful to go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah.
When they accepted Islam, they asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about this and said:
‘O Messenger of Allah, we used to consider it sinful to go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah.’
So Allah revealed:
‘Indeed, al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah are among the symbols of Allah…’ [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:158]”
ʿĀʾishah continued:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ established the practice of going between the two, so no one is permitted to abandon going between them.”
ʿUrwah said:
I then informed Abū Bakr ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān of this.
He said:
“This is knowledge I had not previously heard. Yet I have heard men of knowledge mention that all the people—apart from those whom ʿĀʾishah mentioned as having entered pilgrimage for Manāt—used to go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah.
When Allah mentioned circumambulation of the House but did not mention al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah in the Qur’an, they said:
‘O Messenger of Allah, we used to go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah, but Allah has revealed the command concerning ṭawāf around the House without mentioning al-Ṣafā. Is there any blame upon us for going between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah?’
Then Allah revealed:
‘Indeed, al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah are among the symbols of Allah…’”
Abū Bakr said:
“I therefore understand that this verse was revealed concerning both groups: those who had considered it sinful during Jāhiliyyah to go between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah, and those who used to do so but later hesitated to continue doing so in Islam because Allah had commanded circumambulation of the House without initially mentioning al-Ṣafā, until He mentioned it afterward.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1643) and Muslim (1277:261–263). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13428
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When one of you wishes for something, let him consider carefully what he wishes for, because he does not know what may be written for him as a result of his wish.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (8689), Abū Dāwūd al-Ṭayālisī (2341), al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (794), and Abū Yaʿlā (5907).

13429
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Beware of suspicion, for suspicion is the falsest of speech…”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Ḥusn al-Khuluq (15), al-Bukhārī (6066), and Muslim (2563).

13430
Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would rarely rise from a gathering without making these supplications for his Companions:
“O Allah, grant us such fear of You as will stand between us and disobedience to You; and such obedience to You as will bring us to Your Paradise; and such certainty as will make the calamities of this world easy for us.
Let us enjoy our hearing, our sight, and our strength for as long as You keep us alive, and make them remain with us until the end of our lives.
Let our retaliation be against those who wrong us, grant us victory over those who show us hostility, do not make our calamity be in our religion, do not make this world our greatest concern or the limit of our knowledge, and do not give authority over us to those who will show us no mercy.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3502) and al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa’l-Laylah (402).

13431
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
I have not seen anything more closely resembling al-lamam than what Abū Hurayrah narrated from the Prophet ﷺ:
“Allah has decreed for the son of Adam his share of fornication, which he will inevitably encounter. The fornication of the eye is looking, the fornication of the tongue is speaking, and the soul desires and longs, while the private parts either confirm that or deny it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6612) and Muslim (2657).

13432
Ibn ʿAbbās said regarding the words:
“Those who avoid the major sins and immoralities except for minor slips”:
“If You forgive, O Allah, You forgive sins in abundance; and which of Your servants has not committed some minor slip?”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3284), Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (27/66), and al-Ḥākim (2/469). Its chain of transmission is authentic.
The phrase:
“Except for minor slips”
is an interrupted exception, meaning: minor slips are pardoned. Al-lamam refers to lesser sins below the major immoralities.
Regarding the words:
“He knows you best, from when He produced you from the earth and when you were fetuses in the wombs of your mothers. So do not declare yourselves pure; He knows best who is truly God-conscious”:

13433
Thābit ibn al-Ḥārith al-Anṣārī narrated:
The Jews used to say whenever a young child of theirs died:
“He is a ṣiddīq.”
This reached the Prophet ﷺ, and he said:
“The Jews have lied. There is no soul whom Allah creates in its mother’s womb except that it is either wretched or blessed.”
Then Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“He knows you best, from when He produced you from the earth and when you were fetuses in the wombs of your mothers. So do not declare yourselves pure; He knows best who is truly God-conscious.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (2/75) and al-Wāḥidī in Asbāb al-Nuzūl (p. 422).

13434
Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAṭāʾ narrated:
I named my daughter Barrah.
Zaynab bint Abī Salamah said to me:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ prohibited this name. I myself had been named Barrah, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
‘Do not declare yourselves pure. Allah knows best which of you are people of righteousness.’”
They asked:
“Then what should we name her?”
He replied:
“Name her Zaynab.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2142:19).

13435
Abū Bakrah narrated:
A man praised another man in the presence of the Prophet ﷺ.
The Prophet ﷺ said repeatedly:
“Woe to you! You have cut your companion’s neck! You have cut your companion’s neck!”
Then he said:
“If one of you must praise his brother, let him say: ‘I consider so-and-so to be such-and-such, and Allah is his Reckoner. I do not declare anyone pure before Allah. I consider him to be such-and-such,’ if he truly knows that about him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2662) and Muslim (3000).

13436
Hammām ibn al-Ḥārith narrated:
A man began excessively praising ʿUthmān.
Al-Miqdād went toward him, knelt on his knees—he was a large man—and began throwing pebbly dust toward his face.
ʿUthmān asked him:
“What are you doing?”
He replied:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
‘When you see those who excessively praise others, throw dust in their faces.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3002:69).

13437
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The first sūrah in which a verse of prostration was revealed was Sūrat al-Najm.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ prostrated, and everyone behind him prostrated as well, except for one man whom I saw take a handful of dust and prostrate upon it.
I later saw that man killed as a disbeliever.
He was Umayyah ibn Khalaf.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4863) and Muslim (576:105).

13438
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
“The Prophet ﷺ prostrated while reciting Sūrat al-Najm, and the Muslims, the polytheists, the jinn, and mankind prostrated with him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4862).

13439
Zayd ibn Thābit narrated:
“I recited Sūrat al-Najm to the Prophet ﷺ, and he did not prostrate during it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1072) and Muslim (577).

13440
ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUtbah ibn Masʿūd narrated:
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb asked Abū Wāqid al-Laythī:
“What did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recite in the prayers of al-Aḍḥā and al-Fiṭr?”
He replied:
“He used to recite: ‘Qāf. By the Glorious Qur’an’ [Qāf 50:1] and ‘The Hour has drawn near, and the moon has split.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik in Kitāb al-ʿĪdayn (8) and Muslim (891).

13441
Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I and the Hour have been sent like this one in relation to this one—or like these two.”
He joined his index finger and middle finger together.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5301) and Muslim (2950). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13442
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I and the Hour have been sent like this.”
Shuʿbah joined his index finger and middle finger together while demonstrating how the Prophet ﷺ had indicated it.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6504) and Muslim (2951:134).

13443
Anas ibn Mālik al-Anṣārī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I and the Hour have been sent like these two.”
He joined together his index and middle fingers.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2951:135).

13444
Ismāʿīl ibn ʿUbaydullāh narrated:
Anas ibn Mālik came to al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, who asked him:
“What did you hear the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say concerning the Hour?”
He replied:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“You and the Hour are like these two.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (13336), whose wording is given here, and al-Ḥākim (4/494). Its chain of transmission is authentic.

13445
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I and the Hour have been sent like these two.”
Meaning two fingers.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6505).

13446
Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Your remaining lifespan in comparison with the lifespan of the nations that came before you is only like the time between the ʿAṣr prayer and sunset…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5021).

13447
Khālid ibn ʿUmayr al-ʿAdawī narrated:
ʿUtbah ibn Ghazwān delivered a sermon to us. He praised Allah and extolled Him, then said:
“To proceed: this world has announced that it is coming to an end and has quickly turned away. Nothing remains of it except a small remnant, like the last drops left in a vessel which its owner is trying to pour out.
You are moving from it to an abode that will never cease, so move to it with the best deeds that you presently possess.
We have been told that a stone will be thrown from the edge of Hell and will continue falling through it for seventy years without reaching its bottom. By Allah, it will certainly be filled. Are you amazed?
We have also been told that the distance between two panels of the gates of Paradise is a journey of forty years, yet a day will certainly come when they will be crowded with people.
I remember myself as the seventh of seven men with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. We had no food except leaves from trees, until the corners of our mouths became ulcerated.
I found a cloak and split it between myself and Saʿd ibn Mālik. I wrapped half of it around myself, and Saʿd wrapped the other half around himself.
Yet today, there is not one of us who has not become governor over one of the regions. I seek refuge with Allah from considering myself great while being insignificant before Allah.
There has never been a prophethood except that it eventually passed away, until its final outcome became kingship. You will come to know and experience the rulers who come after us.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2967).
The words of Allah:
“The Hour has drawn near,”
and the statement of the Prophet ﷺ:
“I and the Hour have been sent like this one in relation to this one,”
together with the other hadiths mentioned above, indicate that more of the lifespan of this world has already passed than remains.
According to the author, that past period has extended for millions of years, though none knows its precise duration except Allah, Glorified and Exalted.
As for what is mentioned concerning new discoveries regarding the age of the world and the existence of life upon the earth, the author states that these are estimates and conjectures that may be correct or may be otherwise.
Regarding the words:
“And the moon has split,”
this occurred during the lifetime of the Prophet ﷺ in Makkah, when the polytheists asked him to show them a sign proving the truthfulness of what he had brought and was calling them toward.
The Prophet ﷺ pointed toward the moon, and by Allah’s permission it became two separate parts.
A number of hadiths establish this, including:

13448
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
The people of Makkah asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to show them a sign.
So he showed them the moon split into two parts, until they could see Mount Ḥirāʾ between them.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3868) and Muslim (2802:46).

13449
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The moon split into two parts during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Bear witness.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3636) and Muslim (2800).
In another narration recorded by Muslim through a different route:
“While we were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at Minā, the moon split into two portions. One portion was behind the mountain and the other was on the nearer side of it.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to us:
‘Bear witness.’”
And in another wording:
“O Allah, bear witness.”

13450
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The moon split while we were in Makkah.
The disbelievers of Quraysh in Makkah said:
“This is magic with which Ibn Abī Kabshah has bewitched us. Wait for the travelers who will arrive. If they tell you that they saw the same thing that you saw, then it truly happened; otherwise, he has deceived you.”
He said:
No traveler came to them from any direction except that he informed them that they too had seen what the people of Makkah had seen.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd al-Ṭayālisī (293), al-Bazzār (1971), al-Shāshī in his Musnad (404)—whose wording is given here—and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (2/266).

13451
Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The moon split during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Bear witness.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in Ṣifat al-Qiyāmah (2801), though he did not quote its wording, and by al-Tirmidhī (2182), whose wording is given here.

13452
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
“The moon split during the time of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3638) and Muslim (2803).

13453
Abū Isḥāq narrated:
I saw a man ask al-Aswad ibn Yazīd while he was teaching the Qur’an in the mosque:
“How do you recite this verse, ‘فَهَلْ مِنْ مُدَّكِرٍ’? Is it pronounced with a dāl or a dhāl?”
He replied:
“With a dāl. I heard ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd say: I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ pronounce it:
‘Muddakir.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4871) and Muslim (823). The wording is that of Muslim.

13454
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
On the Day of Badr, while the Prophet ﷺ was inside a shelter, he supplicated:
“I appeal to You by Your covenant and Your promise. O Allah, if You will, You will never be worshipped after this day.”
Abū Bakr took him by the hand and said:
“That is enough, O Messenger of Allah. You have persistently pleaded with your Lord.”
The Prophet ﷺ was wearing his armor.
He then came out reciting:
“The gathering will be defeated, and they will turn their backs in flight. Rather, the Hour is their appointed time, and the Hour is far more disastrous and bitter.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4877).
Al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar said in Fatḥ al-Bārī (8/619):
“This is among the disconnected reports of Ibn ʿAbbās, because he was not present at the event. Perhaps he received it from ʿUmar. Muslim narrated through Simāk ibn al-Walīd, from Ibn ʿAbbās, that he said: ‘ʿUmar narrated part of it to me.’”

13455
Yūsuf ibn Māhak narrated:
I was with ʿĀʾishah, the Mother of the Believers, when she said:
“This verse was revealed to Muḥammad ﷺ in Makkah while I was still a young girl playing:
‘Rather, the Hour is their appointed time, and the Hour is far more disastrous and bitter.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4876).
Al-Bukhārī also narrated the same report through the same chain in greater detail in the Book of the Virtues of the Qur’an, as follows:

13456
Yūsuf ibn Māhak narrated:
I was with ʿĀʾishah, the Mother of the Believers, when a man from Iraq came to her and asked:
“Which type of shroud is best?”
She replied:
“May you be mindful! What harm does that question do you?”
He then said:
“Mother of the Believers, show me your copy of the Qur’an.”
She asked:
“Why?”
He replied:
“So that I may arrange the Qur’an according to it, because it is being recited in an order that is not arranged chronologically.”
She said:
“What harm is there in whichever portion you recite first?
The first revelation that came down consisted of sūrahs from the Mufaṣṣal mentioning Paradise and the Fire.
Then, when people became firmly established in Islam, the rulings concerning lawful and unlawful matters were revealed.
Had the first revelation been, ‘Do not drink wine,’ they would have said, ‘We will never abandon wine.’
And had the first revelation been, ‘Do not commit fornication,’ they would have said, ‘We will never abandon fornication.’
Indeed, while I was still a young girl playing, this verse was revealed to Muḥammad ﷺ in Makkah:
‘Rather, the Hour is their appointed time, and the Hour is far more disastrous and bitter.’
Sūrat al-Baqarah and Sūrat al-Nisāʾ were not revealed until I was already with him.”
Yūsuf said:
Then she brought out the muṣḥaf for him and dictated to him the verses and arrangement of the sūrahs.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4993).

13457
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The polytheists of Quraysh came disputing with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concerning divine decree.
Then the following verses were revealed:
“On the Day they are dragged into the Fire on their faces: ‘Taste the touch of Saqar.’ Indeed, We created all things according to a decree.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2656).

13458
ʿAṭāʾ ibn Abī Rabāḥ narrated:
I came to Ibn ʿAbbās while he was drawing water from Zamzam, and the lower part of his clothes had become wet.
I said to him:
“People have begun speaking about divine decree.”
He asked:
“Have they really done that?”
I replied:
“Yes.”
He said:
“By Allah, this verse was revealed concerning people like them:
‘Taste the touch of Saqar. Indeed, We created all things according to a decree.’
Those are the worst people of this Ummah. Do not visit their sick and do not pray over their dead. If you were to show me one of them, I would gouge out his eyes with these two fingers of mine.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim, as cited by Ibn Kathīr in his Tafsīr, al-Lālakāʾī in Sharḥ Uṣūl al-Iʿtiqād (948), and al-Bayhaqī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (10/205).

13459
Ṭāwūs said:
I met a number of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ who would say:
“Everything is by divine decree.”
He continued:
I heard ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar say that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Everything is according to divine decree, even incapacity and ability—or ability and incapacity.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik in Kitāb al-Qadar (4) and Muslim (2655).

13460
The Mother of the Believers ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me:
“O ʿĀʾishah, beware of deeds that are regarded as insignificant, for Allah will call them to account.”
In another narration:
“Beware of sins that are regarded as insignificant.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (4243), Aḥmad (24415), al-Dārimī (2768), and al-Quḍāʿī in Musnad al-Shihāb (955). Its isnād is authentic.

13461
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Indeed, those who act justly will be upon pulpits of light at the right hand of the Most Merciful, Mighty and Majestic—and both of His hands are right—those who act justly in their judgments, toward their families, and in whatever authority they are entrusted with.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1827).

13462
ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The angels were created from light, the jinn were created from a smokeless flame of fire, and Adam was created from what has been described to you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2996).

13463
Jābir narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came out to his Companions and recited Sūrat al-Raḥmān to them from beginning to end, but they remained silent.
He said:
“I recited it to the jinn on the Night of the Jinn, and they responded better than you did. Whenever I reached the words, ‘So which of your Lord’s favors will you both deny?’ they said: ‘We deny none of Your favors, our Lord, so all praise belongs to You.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3291) and al-Ḥākim (2/473).

13464
Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ recited Sūrat al-Raḥmān to his Companions, but they remained silent.
He said:
“The jinn responded better than you. Whenever I recited to them, ‘So which of your Lord’s favors will you both deny?’ they said: ‘We deny none of Your favors, our Lord, so all praise belongs to You.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (5853) and Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (22/190).

13465
Abū al-Dardāʾ narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said concerning the words:
“Every day He is bringing about a matter”:
“Among His affairs are that He forgives a sin, relieves a hardship, raises some people, and lowers others.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (202), Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Sunnah (301), and Ibn Abī Ḥātim, as cited by Ibn Kathīr in his Tafsīr.

13466
Abū al-Dardāʾ narrated that he heard the Prophet ﷺ while he was preaching from the pulpit recite:
“For whoever fears standing before his Lord are two gardens.”
I said:
“Even if he commits fornication and steals, O Messenger of Allah?”
The Prophet ﷺ repeated a second time:
“For whoever fears standing before his Lord are two gardens.”
I said again:
“Even if he commits fornication and steals, O Messenger of Allah?”
The Prophet ﷺ repeated for a third time:
“For whoever fears standing before his Lord are two gardens.”
I said for the third time:
“Even if he commits fornication and steals, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“Yes, even if Abū al-Dardāʾ’s nose is rubbed in the dust.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (8683), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11496), and al-Baghawī in his Tafsīr (4/292). Its isnād is authentic.
The words:
“Even if he commits fornication and steals”
mean: if afterward he fears Allah and repents.

13467
Asmāʾ bint Abī Bakr narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ mention the Lote Tree of the Utmost Boundary and say:
“A rider could travel beneath the shade of one of its branches for one hundred years, or one hundred riders could take shade beneath it”—Yaḥyā was uncertain—which contains golden moths, and its fruits are like great jars.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2541), Hannād ibn al-Sarī in al-Zuhd (115), and al-Ḥākim (2/469).

13468
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The first group to enter Paradise will have the appearance of the full moon on the night of its fullness. They will neither spit, blow their noses, nor defecate there. Their vessels will be of gold, their combs of gold and silver, their incense will be aloeswood, and their perspiration will be musk.
