Each one of them will have two wives, and the marrow of their shins will be visible through the flesh because of their beauty. There will be no disagreement or hatred among them. Their hearts will be as one heart, and they will glorify Allah morning and evening.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3245) and Muslim (2834:17).

13469
Muḥammad ibn Sīrīn narrated:
The people either boasted to one another or discussed whether there would be more men or women in Paradise.
Abū Hurayrah said:
“Did Abū al-Qāsim ﷺ not say:
‘The first group to enter Paradise will have the appearance of the full moon on the night of its fullness, and the group after them will be like the brightest shining star in the sky. Each man among them will have two wives, and the marrow of their shins will be visible through the flesh. There will be no unmarried person in Paradise.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2834).

13470
Anas narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A morning or evening journey in the cause of Allah is better than the world and everything in it. The space of one of your bows in Paradise—or the space occupied by a whip—is better than the world and everything in it.
If a woman from the people of Paradise were to look upon the people of the earth, she would illuminate everything between them and fill it with fragrance, and the veil upon her head is better than the world and everything in it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2796).

13471
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A man from the people of Paradise will have two wives from the ḥūr al-ʿīn. Each of them will wear seventy garments, yet the marrow of her shin will be visible through those garments.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (8542). Its isnād is authentic.

13472
ʿAbdullāh ibn Qays narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There are two gardens of silver, whose vessels and everything within them are of silver, and two gardens of gold, whose vessels and everything within them are of gold. Nothing will stand between the people and their looking upon their Lord except the Veil of Majesty over His Face in the Garden of Eden.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4878) and Muslim (180). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13473
ʿAbdullāh ibn Qays narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“In Paradise there is a pavilion made from a single hollow pearl, sixty miles wide. In each of its corners are inhabitants who cannot see the others, and the believers will visit them in turn.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4879) and Muslim (2838:24). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
In another narration recorded by Muslim through a different route, the wording is:
“Indeed, the believer will have in Paradise a pavilion made from a single hollow pearl, sixty miles in length…”
And in another narration:
“The pavilion is a pearl, rising sixty miles into the sky…”

13474
Rabīʿah ibn ʿĀmir narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Persist in saying: ‘O Possessor of Majesty and Honor.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (1796), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (7669), and al-Ḥākim (1/498–499).

13475
Thawbān narrated:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ finished his prayer, he would seek Allah’s forgiveness three times and say:
“O Allah, You are Peace, and from You comes peace. Blessed are You, O Possessor of Majesty and Honor.”
Al-Walīd said:
I asked al-Awzāʿī:
“How should the seeking of forgiveness be said?”
He replied:
“You say: ‘Astaghfirullāh, astaghfirullāh.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (591).

13476
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
When the Prophet ﷺ gave the salām at the end of the prayer, he would remain seated only long enough to say:
“O Allah, You are Peace, and from You comes peace. Blessed are You, O Possessor of Majesty and Honor.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (592).

13477
Anas narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to like good dreams, and he would sometimes ask:
“Has any of you seen a dream?”
If a man had seen a dream, he would ask about him, and if there was nothing objectionable about the man, the Prophet ﷺ would be even more pleased with his dream.
A woman came and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I saw in a dream as though I had entered Paradise. Then I heard a tremendous sound that caused Paradise to shake. I looked and saw that so-and-so son of so-and-so, and so-and-so son of so-and-so, were brought forward.”
She continued naming them until she had counted twelve men. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had previously sent out a military expedition.
She said:
“They were brought wearing dusty garments, with blood flowing from the veins of their necks.
Then it was said: ‘Take them to the river al-Baydhakh’—or he said, ‘al-Baydaḥ.’
They were immersed in it, and when they emerged, their faces were like the full moon.
Then golden chairs were brought for them and they sat upon them. A platter—or something similar—containing unripe dates was brought. Whichever side of it they turned toward, they ate whatever fruit they desired. And I ate with them.”
Then the bearer of good news arrived from that expedition and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, such-and-such happened to us, and so-and-so and so-and-so were killed…”
until he named the same twelve men whom the woman had counted.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Bring me the woman.”
She came, and he said:
“Tell this man your dream.”
She narrated it to him.
The man said:
“It happened exactly as she described, O Messenger of Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (12385), Abū Yaʿlā (3289), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6054).

13478
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was asked:
“What is al-Kawthar?”
He replied:
“It is a river that Allah has given me—meaning in Paradise—whiter than milk and sweeter than honey. In it are birds whose necks are like the necks of camels.”
ʿUmar said:
“Those birds must indeed be luxurious and delightful!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Those who eat them are even better than they are.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2542).

13479
Abū Umāmah narrated:
The Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to say:
“Allah benefits us through the Bedouins and the questions they ask.”
One day a Bedouin came and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Allah has mentioned in the Qur’an a tree that causes harm. I did not think there would be a tree in Paradise that could harm its owner.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Which tree is that?”
He replied:
“The lote tree, because it has thorns.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“It is a thornless lote tree. Allah will remove its thorns and place a fruit in place of every thorn. It will produce fruit, and each fruit will open into seventy-two varieties, no one variety resembling another.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/476), and from him by al-Bayhaqī in al-Baʿth wa’l-Nushūr (264).

13480
ʿUtbah ibn ʿAbd al-Sulamī narrated:
I was sitting with the Prophet ﷺ when a Bedouin came and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I hear you mentioning a tree in Paradise, and I do not know of any tree with more thorns than it”—meaning the ṭalḥ tree.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah will place in the position of every thorn a fruit like the testicle of a heavily-haired castrated goat. Within it will be seventy varieties of food, none resembling another.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (17/130).
Some scholars said that:
“Ṭalḥ”
means bananas, the singular being ṭalḥah, while:
“layered”
means piled one above another.
This interpretation has been narrated from Ibn ʿAbbās and ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib.
Regarding the words:
“And extended shade,”
this means a permanent shade that never comes to an end.

13481
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Indeed, in Paradise there is a tree beneath whose shade a rider could travel for one hundred years without crossing it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4881) and Muslim (2826:7).

13482
Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Indeed, in Paradise there is a tree beneath whose shade a rider could travel for one hundred years without crossing it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (2827) and al-Bukhārī (6552).

13483
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Indeed, in Paradise there is a tree beneath which a rider on a swift, well-trained racehorse could travel for one hundred years without crossing it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (2828) and al-Bukhārī (6553).

13484
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Indeed, in Paradise there is a tree beneath whose shade a rider could travel for one hundred years without crossing it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3251).
Regarding the words:
“And flowing water,”
maskūb comes from pouring water.
That is, the rivers of Paradise will flow continuously and powerfully, without interruption and without being confined to channels.
Regarding the words:
“And abundant fruit, never ending nor forbidden,”
Paradise contains many varied fruits that never cease at any time. They will always be available and within easy reach of those who desire them, so they may eat them with complete ease and delight in them.

13485
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The sun was eclipsed, so the Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed the eclipse prayer.
In the hadith, they said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we saw you reach out for something while standing here, and then we saw you draw back.”
He replied:
“I saw Paradise and reached for a cluster of its fruit. Had I taken it, you would have continued eating from it for as long as this world remained…”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in Ṣalāt al-Khusūf (445), al-Bukhārī (1052), and Muslim (907).

13486
ʿUtbah ibn ʿAbd al-Sulamī narrated:
A Bedouin came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked:
“What is this Ḥawḍ that you speak about?”
He replied:
“It is as wide as the distance from al-Bayḍāʾ to Buṣrā. Allah will supply it for me through a channel whose two ends no created person knows.”
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said Allāhu akbar and said:
“The poor among the Muhājirūn who fight in the cause of Allah and die in the cause of Allah will come to the Ḥawḍ. I hope that Allah will grant me that channel so that I may drink from it.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ also said:
“My Lord has promised me that seventy thousand from my Ummah will enter Paradise without reckoning. Then each thousand will intercede for seventy thousand, and then my Lord, Blessed and Exalted, will take three handfuls with His two Hands.”
ʿUmar said Allāhu akbar and said:
“The first seventy thousand will surely be permitted by Allah to intercede for their fathers, sons, and clans, and I hope Allah will place me among one of those final handfuls.”
The Bedouin asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, is there fruit in it?”
He replied:
“Yes. In it is a tree called Ṭūbā, which spreads throughout al-Firdaws.”
He asked:
“Which tree in our land does it resemble?”
He replied:
“It does not resemble any tree in your land. Have you ever been to Syria?”
He said:
“No, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“It resembles a tree in Syria called the walnut tree. It grows upon a single trunk and then spreads outward at the top.”
He asked:
“How large is its trunk?”
He replied:
“If a young she-camel from your family were to set out around it, she would not complete the circuit before her collarbone broke from old age.”
He asked:
“Does it contain grapes?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
He asked:
“How large is one of its clusters?”
He replied:
“A piebald crow could fly for a month without turning aside or tiring before reaching the end of it.”
He asked:
“How large is one grape?”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“Has your father ever slaughtered a large male goat from his flock?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
He said:
“Did he skin it and give the hide to your mother, saying: ‘Tan this, then cut from it a bucket for us with which we can water our livestock’?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The Bedouin said:
“Then one such grape would satisfy me and my household.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“And most of your entire clan.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (404), whose wording is given here, in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (17/126–127), and by Aḥmad (17642).
Regarding the words:
“And raised furnishings,”
furush is the plural of firāsh, just as kutub is the plural of kitāb.
Originally, a firāsh is something spread upon the ground.
The word:
“raised”
means that these furnishings are spread upon elevated couches rather than directly upon the ground.

13487
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
It was asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, will we have intimate relations with our wives in Paradise?”
He replied:
“Yes, by the One in Whose hand is my soul. A man will have intimate relations in a single day with one hundred virgins.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bazzār (10072), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Ṣaghīr (795), and in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (722). Its isnād is authentic.

13488
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was asked:
“Will there be sexual intercourse in Paradise?”
He replied:
“Yes, by the One in Whose hand is my soul, with vigorous intimacy. When he rises from her, she returns pure and a virgin.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7402) and Abū Nuʿaym in Ṣifat al-Jannah (393).

13489
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
One night we remained talking in the presence of the Prophet of Allah ﷺ until our conversation became prolonged. Then we returned home.
The next morning we went to the Prophet of Allah ﷺ, and he said:
“Last night the prophets and their followers from their respective nations were shown to me. A prophet would come with three people from his nation; another prophet would come with a small group; another with only a few people; and another prophet would come with no one from his nation at all.
Then Mūsā ibn ʿImrān ﷺ came before me with a large crowd from the Children of Israel. When I saw them, they pleased me, and I said: ‘My Lord, who are these?’ He said: ‘This is your brother Mūsā ibn ʿImrān ﷺ.’”
He continued:
“Then I saw what appeared like the hills of Makkah, with people filling the entire horizon before me. I said: ‘My Lord, who are these?’ He said: ‘Your Ummah.’”
He said:
“Then it was said to me: ‘Are you pleased?’ I said: ‘My Lord, I am pleased. My Lord, I am pleased.’”
Then he said:
“It was then said to me: ‘Among these are seventy thousand who will enter Paradise without reckoning.’”
ʿUkāshah ibn Miḥṣan, from Banū Asad ibn Khuzaymah, stood and said:
“Prophet of Allah, ask your Lord to make me one of them.”
He said:
“O Allah, make him one of them.”
Then another man stood and said:
“Prophet of Allah, ask your Lord to make me one of them.”
He replied:
“ʿUkāshah has preceded you to it.”
Then the Prophet of Allah ﷺ said:
“May my father and mother be sacrificed for you. If you can be among the seventy thousand, then be among them. If you fall short of that, then be among the people filling the hills. If you fall short of that, then be among the people filling the horizon, for I saw many people gathered there.”
Then the Prophet of Allah ﷺ said:
“I certainly hope that those who follow me from my Ummah will constitute one quarter of the people of Paradise.”
We said Allāhu akbar.
Then he said:
“I certainly hope that they will constitute one third.”
We said Allāhu akbar.
Then he said:
“I certainly hope that they will constitute one half.”
We said Allāhu akbar.
Then the Prophet of Allah ﷺ recited:
“A multitude from the earlier peoples and a multitude from the later peoples.”
The Muslims then discussed who these seventy thousand might be. They said:
“We think they are people who were born into Islam and continued practicing it until they died upon it.”
Their discussion reached the Prophet of Allah ﷺ, and he said:
“It is not as you have said. Rather, they are those who do not ask others to perform ruqyah for them, do not cauterize themselves, do not believe in evil omens, and place their trust in their Lord.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (3989), al-Bazzār (1440, 1441), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6431), and al-Ḥākim (4/577–578).

13490
Anas narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is no Muslim who plants a tree or sows a crop from which a bird, a human being, or an animal eats except that it is counted as charity for him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2320) and Muslim (1553).
Al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar said:
“This contains evidence that it is permissible to attribute cultivation to a human being. A hadith prohibiting such attribution has been reported, but it is not strong.”
That report has been referenced in the original work.

13491
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The fire that the children of Adam kindle is one part out of seventy parts of the Fire of Hell.”
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, this fire alone would have been sufficient!”
He replied:
“The Fire of Hell has been made sixty-nine parts hotter than it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in the Book of Hell (1), al-Bukhārī (3265), and Muslim (2843).
Muslim added at the end:
“Every one of those parts is equal to its heat.”
Regarding the words:
“And a benefit for al-muqwīn,”
this means a source of benefit.
Here, al-muqwīn refers to travelers. The meaning is that people of the desert and those on journeys benefit from fire more than those who remain settled, because they kindle it at night to drive away wild animals, to guide those who have lost their way, and for other benefits.
It has also been said that the word al-muqwī is one of those Arabic words that can carry opposite meanings.
A poor person may be called muqwī because he is devoid of wealth, while a wealthy person may also be called muqwī because of his ability and strength to obtain what he desires.
According to this interpretation, fire is a benefit for both rich and poor alike, and no one can dispense with it.
Al-Baghawī mentioned this in his Tafsīr.

13492
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Rain fell upon the people during the lifetime of the Prophet ﷺ.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Among the people this morning are those who are grateful and those who are ungrateful. Some said, ‘This is the mercy of Allah,’ while others said, ‘The rising of such-and-such a star has proven true.’”
Then these verses were revealed:
“So I swear by the positions of the stars…”
until the words:
“And you make your provision that you deny it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (73).

13493
Zayd ibn Khālid al-Juhanī narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ led us in the Fajr prayer at al-Ḥudaybiyah after rain had fallen during the night.
When he finished the prayer, he turned toward the people and said:
“Do you know what your Lord has said?”
They replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“He said: ‘This morning some of My servants have become believers in Me and others have become disbelievers in Me. As for the one who said, “We were given rain by the favor and mercy of Allah,” he is a believer in Me and a disbeliever in the stars. As for the one who said, “We were given rain because of the rising of such-and-such a star,” he is a disbeliever in Me and a believer in the star.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in al-Istisqāʾ (4), al-Bukhārī (846), and Muslim (71).

13494
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Have you not considered what your Lord has said? He said: ‘I do not bestow any blessing upon My servants except that a group among them begins the morning disbelieving in it, saying: “The stars,” and, “By the stars.”’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (72).

13495
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
“I heard the Prophet ﷺ recite it as: ‘Farūḥun wa rayḥānun,’ with the letter rāʾ pronounced with a ḍammah.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3991), al-Tirmidhī (2938), Aḥmad (24352), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11502), and al-Ḥākim (2/236).

13496
ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever loves to meet Allah, Allah loves to meet him; and whoever dislikes meeting Allah, Allah dislikes meeting him.”
I said:
“O Prophet of Allah, does this refer to disliking death? All of us dislike death.”
He replied:
“It is not like that. Rather, when the believer is given the glad tidings of Allah’s mercy, His pleasure, and His Paradise, he loves to meet Allah, so Allah loves to meet him. But when the disbeliever is given the news of Allah’s punishment and wrath, he dislikes meeting Allah, so Allah dislikes meeting him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2684).

13497
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Two phrases are light upon the tongue, heavy upon the Scale, and beloved to the Most Merciful: ‘Glory and praise be to Allah’ and ‘Glory be to Allah, the Most Great.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6406) and Muslim (2694).

13498
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever says, ‘Glory and praise be to Allah, the Most Great,’ a palm tree will be planted for him in Paradise.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3464, 3465), al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa’l-Laylah (827), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (826, 827).

13499
Suhayl narrated:
Abū Ṣāliḥ used to instruct us that when one of us intended to sleep, he should lie upon his right side and say:
“O Allah, Lord of the heavens, Lord of the earth, and Lord of the Mighty Throne; our Lord and the Lord of all things; Splitter of the grain and the date-stone; Revealer of the Torah, the Gospel, and the Criterion; I seek refuge in You from the evil of everything whose forelock You hold.
O Allah, You are the First, and there is nothing before You. You are the Last, and there is nothing after You. You are the Evident, and there is nothing above You. You are the Innermost, and there is nothing beyond You. Settle our debts for us and enrich us from poverty.”
He narrated this from Abū Hurayrah, from the Prophet ﷺ.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2713:61).
The words:
“You are the Evident, and there is nothing above You”
affirm Allah’s exaltedness above His creation. According to the author, this includes exaltedness in His Essence, His rank, and His overwhelming power.

13500
Ibn Masʿūd said:
“Only four years passed between our acceptance of Islam and Allah’s admonishing us with this verse:
‘Has the time not come for those who believe that their hearts should humble themselves at the remembrance of Allah?’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3027).
The statement of Ibn Masʿūd indicates that this particular verse is Makkan, because he accepted Islam at an early stage and participated in both migrations, the first being the migration to Abyssinia in the fifth year.
The remaining verses of the sūrah are Madinan without disagreement among the scholars, due to the words in verse 10:
“Those among you who spent and fought before the conquest are not equal to those who did so afterward. They are greater in rank than those who spent and fought afterward.”
The “conquest” here refers either to the conquest of Makkah or to the victory of al-Ḥudaybiyah. In either case, the verse is Madinan.

13501
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The people of Paradise will look up at the inhabitants of the lofty chambers above them just as you look at a brilliant star disappearing on the horizon in the east or the west, because of the difference in rank between them.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, are those the ranks of the prophets which no one else can attain?”
He replied:
“Yes. By the One in Whose hand is my soul, they are men who believed in Allah and affirmed the truthfulness of the messengers.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3256) and Muslim (2831). I did not find it in Mālik's al-Muwaṭṭaʾ in the recension of Yaḥyā al-Laythī.

13502
ʿĀʾishah said:
“All praise belongs to Allah whose hearing encompasses all voices. Khawlah bint Thaʿlabah came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ complaining about her husband. Some of what they were saying was so quiet that I could not hear it, yet Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
‘Allah has certainly heard the words of the woman who was pleading with you concerning her husband and complaining to Allah. And Allah hears the conversation between you both. Indeed, Allah is All-Hearing, All-Seeing.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (3460), Ibn Mājah (2063), Aḥmad (24195), and al-Ḥākim (2/481). Its isnād is authentic, and al-Ḥākim authenticated it.
Al-Ḥākim added at the beginning some of the words of the woman who was pleading, which ʿĀʾishah heard. She said:
“O Messenger of Allah! He consumed my youth, and I bore children for him, until when I became old and could no longer bear children for him, he pronounced ẓihār against me. O Allah, I complain to You.”

13503
Khawlah bint Thaʿlabah said:
“By Allah, it was concerning me and Aws ibn al-Ṣāmit that Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed the opening verses of Sūrat al-Mujādilah.”
She said:
I was married to him, and he was an elderly man whose temperament had become harsh and irritable.
One day he came to me, and I responded to him concerning something. He became angry and said:
“You are to me like the back of my mother.”
Then he went out and sat for a while in the gathering place of his people.
Afterward he returned to me and wanted to have marital relations with me.
I said:
“No! By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Khuwaylah, you will not reach me after saying what you said until Allah and His Messenger give their judgment concerning us.”
He then sprang upon me, but I resisted him. I overcame him as a woman can overcome a weak elderly man and pushed him away from me.
Then I went to one of my female neighbors and borrowed some clothes from her. I went out until I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and sat before him.
I told him what had happened to me and began complaining to him about the harsh treatment I suffered because of my husband’s bad temper.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ kept saying:
“O Khuwaylah! Your cousin is an elderly man, so fear Allah regarding him.”
She continued:
By Allah, I had not yet left when Qur’an was revealed concerning me.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was overtaken by the state that would come upon him when revelation descended. Then, when it passed from him, he said to me:
“O Khuwaylah, Allah has revealed concerning you and your husband.”
He then recited to me:
“Allah has certainly heard the words of the woman who was pleading with you concerning her husband and complaining to Allah. And Allah hears the conversation between you both. Indeed, Allah is All-Hearing, All-Seeing…”
until the words:
“…and for the disbelievers is a painful punishment.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said to me:
“Tell him to free a slave.”
I said:
“By Allah, O Messenger of Allah, he does not have the means to free one.”
He said:
“Then let him fast for two consecutive months.”
I said:
“By Allah, O Messenger of Allah, he is an elderly man and is unable to fast.”
He said:
“Then let him feed sixty poor people with a wasq of dates.”
I said:
“By Allah, O Messenger of Allah, he does not possess that either.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Then we will assist him with a basket of dates.”
I said:
“And I, O Messenger of Allah, will assist him with another basket.”
He said:
“You have done what is right and good. Go and give it in charity on his behalf, and then treat your cousin well.”
She said:
So I did.
ʿAbdullāh said: My father said that Saʿd explained:
“Al-ʿaraq means a large basket.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (27319), whose wording is given here, Abū Dāwūd (2214, 2215), Ibn al-Jārūd (746), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4279), and al-Bayhaqī (7/389).

13504
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
A group of Jews sought permission to enter upon the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“Death be upon you.”
I said:
“Rather, death and the curse be upon you!”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“O ʿĀʾishah, Allah is gentle and loves gentleness in every matter.”
I said:
“Did you not hear what they said?”
He replied:
“I said: ‘And upon you.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6927) and Muslim (2165).

13505
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
Some Jews came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“Death be upon you, O Abū al-Qāsim.”
He replied:
“And upon you.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
I replied:
“And upon you be death and disgrace!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“O ʿĀʾishah, do not be obscene in speech.”
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, did you not hear what they said? They said, ‘Death be upon you.’”
He replied:
“Did I not return upon them what they said? I said: ‘And upon you.’”
Ibn Numayr explained in his narration from ʿĀʾishah:
“Indeed, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, does not love obscenity or deliberately obscene speech.”
Ibn Numayr also said in his narration:
Then this verse was revealed:
“And when they come to you, they greet you with that by which Allah has not greeted you…”
until the end of the verse.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (25924).

13506
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
Some Jews greeted the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“Death be upon you, O Abū al-Qāsim.”
He replied:
“And upon you.”
ʿĀʾishah became angry and said:
“Did you not hear what they said?”
He replied:
“Yes, I heard them and responded to them. Our supplication against them is answered, while their supplication against us is not answered.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2166).

13507
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated:
The Jews used to say to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“Death be upon you.”
Then they would say to themselves:
“Why does Allah not punish us for what we say?”
So this verse was revealed:
“And when they come to you, they greet you with that by which Allah has not greeted you…”
to the end of the verse.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (6589), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (3/75), and al-Bayhaqī in Shuʿab al-Īmān (8679).

13508
ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated:
When the verse was revealed:
“O you who believe, when you privately consult the Messenger, then give charity before your private consultation,”
the Prophet ﷺ said to me:
“What do you think? A dīnār?”
I said:
“They would not be able to afford that.”
He asked:
“Then half a dīnār?”
I said:
“They would not be able to afford that.”
He asked:
“Then how much?”
I said:
“A grain of barley.”
He replied:
“You are indeed one who proposes little.”
Then the verse was revealed:
“Are you afraid of giving charities before your private consultations?”
ʿAlī said:
“Through me, Allah lightened this matter for this Ummah.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3300), al-Nasāʾī in Khaṣāʾiṣ ʿAlī (152), and Ibn Ḥibbān (6941).

13509
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was sitting in the shade of his room when the shade had nearly withdrawn from him.
He said to his Companions:
“A man will come to you who looks at you with the eyes of a devil. When you see him, do not speak to him.”
Then a blue-eyed man came.
When the Prophet ﷺ saw him, he summoned him and asked:
“Why do you and your companions insult me?”
The man replied:
“Remain where you are until I bring them to you.”
He went and returned with them.
They began swearing by Allah that they had neither said nor done such things.
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“On the Day Allah resurrects them all, they will swear to Him as they swear to you, thinking that they are upon something. Indeed, they are the liars.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (3277), whose wording is given here, al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2270), and al-Ḥākim (2/482).

13510
Abū al-Dardāʾ narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“There are no three people in a village among whom the adhān is not called and the prayer is not established except that Satan gains mastery over them. So adhere to the congregation, for the wolf devours the sheep that strays away from the flock.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (547), al-Nasāʾī (847), Aḥmad (21710), whose wording is given here, Ibn Khuzaymah (1486), and al-Ḥākim (1/211).

13511
Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated:
I asked Ibn ʿAbbās:
“What about Sūrat al-Tawbah?”
He replied:
“Al-Tawbah is the exposer. It continued revealing, ‘And among them… and among them…,’ until they thought that it would leave none of them without mentioning him.”
I asked:
“What about Sūrat al-Anfāl?”
He replied:
“It was revealed concerning Badr.”
I asked:
“What about Sūrat al-Ḥashr?”
He replied:
“It was revealed concerning Banū al-Naḍīr.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4882) and Muslim (3031:31).

13512
Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated:
I said to Ibn ʿAbbās:
“Sūrat al-Ḥashr?”
He replied:
“Say: Sūrat al-Naḍīr.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4883).

13513
A man from among the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ narrated:
The disbelievers of Quraysh wrote to ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl and to those among al-Aws and al-Khazraj who still worshipped idols. At that time, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was in Madinah, before the Battle of Badr.
They wrote:
“You have given refuge to our man, and you are the largest group among the people of Madinah. By Allah, either you kill him or expel him, or we will call upon the Arabs for assistance against you. Then all of us will march against you until we kill your fighting men and take your women captive.”
When this message reached ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy and those idolaters who were with him, they communicated with one another, gathered together, and prepared to confront the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions.
When news of this reached the Prophet ﷺ, he met them while they were gathered and said:
“The threat of Quraysh has affected you greatly. Yet Quraysh could not harm you more than the harm you are now intending to inflict upon yourselves. Here you are intending to kill your own sons and brothers.”
When they heard this from the Prophet ﷺ, they dispersed.
The disbelievers of Quraysh learned what had happened. Then the Battle of Badr took place.
After Badr, Quraysh wrote to the Jews:
“You are people of armor and fortresses. Either you fight our man or we will do such-and-such to you, and nothing will prevent us from reaching the anklets of your women.”
When the Jews received this letter, Banū al-Naḍīr unanimously resolved upon treachery.
They sent word to the Prophet ﷺ:
“Come out to us with thirty of your Companions, and we will bring thirty of our rabbis. Let us meet at a certain place midway between us and you. They will listen to you. If they believe you and affirm you, then all of us will believe.”
The Prophet ﷺ went out with thirty of his Companions, and thirty Jewish rabbis came out to meet him.
When they reached an open area, some of the Jews said to one another:
“How will you manage to get to him when he has thirty of his Companions with him, every one of whom would love to die before him?”
So they sent to him:
“How can we properly understand one another when there are sixty of us? Come with three of your Companions, and three of our scholars will come to you. Let them listen to you; if they believe in you, then all of us will believe and affirm you.”
The Prophet ﷺ went out with three of his Companions.
The three Jewish men concealed daggers beneath their garments and intended to assassinate the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
A sincere woman from Banū al-Naḍīr sent word to her brother’s son, a Muslim man from the Anṣār, informing him of Banū al-Naḍīr’s plan to betray the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He hurried until he caught up with the Prophet ﷺ and secretly informed him of their plot before the Prophet ﷺ reached them.
So the Prophet ﷺ returned.
The next morning, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ marched against Banū al-Naḍīr with military units and besieged them.
He said to them:
“You will not be secure with me unless you enter into a covenant with me.”
They refused to give him such a covenant, so he and the Muslims fought them that day.
The following morning, he marched with cavalry and military units against Banū Qurayẓah, leaving Banū al-Naḍīr temporarily. He called upon Banū Qurayẓah to enter into a covenant with him, and they did so.
He then departed from them and marched again against Banū al-Naḍīr with his military units.
He fought them until they agreed to evacuation, on the condition that they could take whatever their camels could carry except weapons—al-ḥalqah meaning weaponry.
Banū al-Naḍīr then loaded their camels with whatever possessions they could carry, including the doors and timber of their houses.
They themselves began destroying their houses, demolishing them in order to carry away whatever timber suited them.
Their expulsion was the first gathering and movement of people toward al-Shām.
Banū al-Naḍīr were one of the tribes of the Children of Israel. They had not suffered expulsion since Allah decreed exile upon the Children of Israel.
For that reason, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ expelled them.
Had Allah not decreed expulsion for them, He would have punished them in this world as Banū Qurayẓah were later punished.
Then Allah revealed:
“Whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth glorifies Allah, and He is the Almighty, the All-Wise…”
until the words:
“…and Allah has power over all things.”
The palm groves of Banū al-Naḍīr became exclusively the property of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Allah granted them to him and singled him out with them, saying:
“Whatever Allah restored to His Messenger from them, you did not spur toward it any horses or riding camels…”
meaning that it was acquired without actual battle.
The Prophet ﷺ gave most of it to the Muhājirūn and distributed it among them.
He also gave shares to two men from the Anṣār who were in need, while no other man from the Anṣār received a share.
What remained became part of the charitable property of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and remained in the hands of the descendants of Fāṭimah.
Authentic: Narrated by ʿAbd al-Razzāq (9733).

13514
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ burned and cut down some of the palm trees of Banū al-Naḍīr at al-Buwayrah.
Then Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“Whatever palm trees you cut down or left standing upon their roots, it was by Allah’s permission, and so that He might disgrace the defiantly disobedient.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4884) and Muslim (1746:29).

13515
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“Whatever you cut down of līnah or left standing upon its roots…”
He said:
“Al-līnah means the palm tree.”
Regarding:
“And so that He might disgrace the defiantly disobedient,”
he said:
“He caused them to come down from their fortresses.”
He continued:
The Muslims were commanded to cut down some of the palm trees, but this troubled their hearts.
They said:
“We have cut down some and left some standing. Let us ask the Messenger of Allah ﷺ whether we will receive a reward for what we cut down and whether we bear any sin for what we left standing.”
Then Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“Whatever palm trees you cut down or left standing upon their roots…”
to the end of the verse.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3303) and al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11510).

13516
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said:
The property of Banū al-Naḍīr was among that which Allah granted as fayʾ to His Messenger ﷺ, without the Muslims having spurred horses or riding camels to acquire it.
Therefore, it belonged specifically to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
From it, he would provide his family with their expenses for one year.
Then he would use whatever remained for weapons and riding animals, preparing them for the cause of Allah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4885) and Muslim (1757).

13517
Ibn Masʿūd said:
“May Allah curse the women who tattoo others and those who have themselves tattooed, those who pluck facial hair, and those who create spaces between their teeth for beauty, thereby altering the creation of Allah.”
News of this reached a woman from Banū Asad called Umm Yaʿqūb.
She came and said:
“I have heard that you cursed such-and-such women.”
He replied:
“Why should I not curse those whom the Messenger of Allah ﷺ cursed and whom the Book of Allah condemns?”
She said:
“I have read everything between the two covers of the Qur’an, yet I did not find in it what you are saying.”
He replied:
“If you truly read it, then you found it. Have you not read:
‘Whatever the Messenger gives you, take it; and whatever he forbids you from, refrain from it’?”
She said:
“Yes.”
He said:
“Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ prohibited these things.”
She said:
“But I think your own wife does these things.”
He said:
“Go and look.”
She went and looked but found none of what she had expected.
He then said:
“Had she been like that, I would not have remained with her.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4886) and Muslim (2125). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim narrated similar wording.
Muslim added:
“And the women who pluck facial hair.”
Al-Bukhārī (4887) also added:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ cursed the woman who attaches hair extensions.”

13518
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Leave me as long as I leave you. Those who came before you were destroyed because of their excessive questioning and their disagreement with their prophets. So when I forbid you from something, avoid it; and when I command you to do something, do as much of it as you are able.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7288) and Muslim (1337:131).

13519
ʿAmr ibn Maymūn narrated that ʿUmar said:
“I advise the next caliph concerning the earliest Muhājirūn: he should recognize their rights.
And I advise him concerning the Anṣār, those who had settled in the home and in faith before the Prophet ﷺ migrated to them: he should accept the good deeds of those among them who do good and pardon those among them who fall short.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Tafsīr (4888), through Aḥmad ibn Yūnus, from Abū Bakr—meaning Ibn ʿAyyāsh—from Ḥuṣayn, from ʿAmr ibn Maymūn.

13520
Anas ibn Mālik narrated, when he went with him to al-Walīd:
The Prophet ﷺ proposed granting the Anṣār lands in Bahrain.
They said:
“No, unless you grant our brothers among the Muhājirūn the same.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“If not, then remain patient until you meet me, for after me you will encounter others being favored over you.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3794).

13521
Anas narrated:
When the Prophet ﷺ came to Madinah, the Muhājirūn came to him and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we have never seen a people more generous with abundance, nor more excellent in sharing even when they possess little, than the people among whom we have settled.
They have relieved us of our burdens and shared their comforts and prosperity with us, to the point that we fear they may take all the reward.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“No, not as long as you supplicate to Allah for them and praise them for what they have done.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2487), Aḥmad (13075, 13122), and al-Bayhaqī (6/183).

13522
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Anṣār said to the Prophet ﷺ:
“Divide the palm trees between us and our brothers among the Muhājirūn.”
He replied:
“No.”
So they said to the Muhājirūn:
“You take care of the work for us, and we will share the fruit with you.”
They replied:
“We hear and obey.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2325).
Regarding the words:
“They give preference to others over themselves even though they themselves are in need”:

13523
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
A man came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I am suffering from severe hunger and hardship.”
The Prophet ﷺ sent to his wives, but nothing was found with any of them.
So the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Is there any man who will host him tonight? May Allah have mercy on him.”
A man from the Anṣār stood and said:
“I will, O Messenger of Allah.”
He went home and said to his wife:
“This is the guest of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Do not hold anything back from him.”
She said:
“By Allah, I have nothing except the food of the children.”
He said:
“When the children ask for dinner, put them to sleep. Then come and extinguish the lamp, and tonight we will go hungry ourselves.”
She did so.
The next morning, the man went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, was amazed—or laughed—at what so-and-so and his wife did.”
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“They give preference to others over themselves even though they themselves are in need.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4889) and Muslim (2054).
Regarding the words:
“Whoever is protected from the stinginess of his own soul,”
shuḥḥ, with a ḍammah on the letter shīn, is an ingrained disposition within the soul that is difficult to separate from it.
Allah says:
“Souls are prone to stinginess.” [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:128]
That is, no soul is completely free from it.
Whoever wishes to protect his soul from its stinginess must train and refine it upon beautiful qualities and virtues.
Miserliness is an effect of shuḥḥ. When the soul is trained upon generosity and nobility, its stinginess is removed and miserliness disappears.
But whoever fails to discipline his soul may fall into shuḥḥ, and it may drive him to commit forbidden acts, including consuming people’s wealth unjustly.
It is narrated that a man said to ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd:
“I fear that I may have been ruined.”
He asked:
“Why?”
The man said:
“I hear Allah say:
‘Whoever is protected from the stinginess of his own soul, those are the successful,’
yet I am a miserly man and hardly anything ever leaves my hand.”
ʿAbdullāh replied:
“That is not the shuḥḥ which Allah mentioned in the Qur’an. Rather, shuḥḥ is that you consume your brother’s wealth unjustly. What you are describing is miserliness, and miserliness is indeed an evil quality.”
Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (22/529). Its chain contains al-Masʿūdī, whose narrations became confused later in his life.

13524
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Beware of oppression, for oppression will be darknesses on the Day of Resurrection. And beware of greed and stinginess, for greed and stinginess destroyed those who came before you. It drove them to shed one another’s blood and to make lawful what had been forbidden to them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2578).

13525
ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr narrated:
ʿĀʾishah said to me:
“O son of my sister, they were commanded to seek forgiveness for the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ, yet instead they reviled them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Tafsīr (3022), through several routes from Hishām ibn ʿUrwah, from his father.
Al-Qāḍī ʿIyāḍ said:
“What appears most likely is that she said this when she heard what the people of Egypt were saying about ʿUthmān, what the people of al-Shām were saying about ʿAlī, and what the Ḥarūriyyah were saying about all of them.”
As for the command to seek forgiveness to which she referred, it is the words of Allah:
“And those who came after them say, ‘Our Lord, forgive us and our brothers who preceded us in faith.’”

13526
Jarīr ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ early in the day when a group of people came to him barefoot, scantily clothed, wearing striped woolen garments—or cloaks—with swords hanging from their necks.
Most of them were from Muḍar; rather, all of them were from Muḍar.
The face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ changed when he saw the severity of their poverty.
He entered his home and then came out.
He instructed Bilāl to call the adhān and iqāmah. The Prophet ﷺ prayed and then delivered a sermon.
He recited:
“O mankind, fear your Lord, who created you from a single soul…”
until the end of the verse:
“Indeed, Allah is ever Watchful over you.” [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:1]
He then recited the verse from Sūrat al-Ḥashr:
“Fear Allah, and let every soul consider what it has sent ahead for tomorrow. And fear Allah.”
Then he said:
“Let a man give charity from his dīnār, from his dirham, from his clothing, from a ṣāʿ of his wheat, or from a ṣāʿ of his dates.”
He continued until he said:
“Even if it is only half a date.”
Then a man from the Anṣār came carrying a pouch so heavy that his hand could scarcely hold it—indeed, it could not.
Then the people followed one after another in giving charity until I saw two piles, one of food and one of clothing.
I then saw the face of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ shining with happiness, as though it were gilded.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever establishes a good practice in Islam will have its reward and the reward of everyone who acts upon it after him, without their rewards being diminished in the slightest. And whoever establishes an evil practice in Islam will bear its sin and the sin of everyone who acts upon it after him, without their sins being diminished in the slightest.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1017).

13527
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah has ninety-nine Names, one hundred minus one. Whoever preserves them will enter Paradise. He is One and loves what is odd-numbered.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6410) and Muslim (2677).
There are other hadiths with the same meaning mentioned in the Book of Faith.

13528
ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent me, al-Zubayr, and al-Miqdād, saying:
“Set out until you reach Rawḍat Khākh. There you will find a woman traveling with a letter. Take the letter from her.”
So we set out, our horses racing with us, until we reached the garden. There we found the woman.
We said to her:
“Bring out the letter.”
She said:
“I have no letter.”
We said:
“Either you bring out the letter or we will remove the clothing.”
So she took it out from the braids of her hair.
We brought it to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. It contained a message from Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah to some of the polytheists in Makkah, informing them of certain plans of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“O Ḥāṭib, what is this?”
Ḥāṭib replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, do not be hasty in judging me. I was a man attached to Quraysh”—meaning that he was their ally but not originally one of them—“while the Muhājirūn who are with you have relatives in Makkah who protect their families and their wealth.
Since I lacked such blood ties among them, I wished to do them a favor so that they would protect my relatives.
I did not do this because I had apostatized from my religion, nor because I was pleased with disbelief after accepting Islam.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“He has indeed spoken the truth to you.”
ʿUmar said:
“O Messenger of Allah, allow me to strike the neck of this hypocrite.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“He participated in Badr. And what do you know? Perhaps Allah looked upon the people of Badr and said: ‘Do whatever you wish, for I have forgiven you.’”
Then Allah revealed Sūrat al-Mumtaḥanah:
“O you who believe, do not take My enemies and your enemies as allies, offering them affection even though they have disbelieved in the truth that has come to you, driving out the Messenger and yourselves merely because you believe in Allah, your Lord. If you have truly gone forth striving in My cause and seeking My pleasure, how can you secretly show them affection? I know best what you conceal and what you reveal. Whoever among you does this has certainly strayed from the straight path.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4274) and Muslim (2494:161).
The word “al-ẓaʿīnah” means a woman.
Regarding the wording:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent me, al-Zubayr, and al-Miqdād,”
another narration in al-Bukhārī and Muslim mentions Marthad al-Ghanawī instead of al-Miqdād.
Al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar said:
“It is possible that all three were with him, and one of the narrators from ʿAlī mentioned what the other did not mention.”
Fatḥ al-Bārī (7/520).

13529
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever is held back by his deeds will not be brought forward by his lineage.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2699).

13530
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the verse was revealed:
“And warn your closest relatives.” [Sūrat al-Shuʿarāʾ 26:214]
the Prophet ﷺ climbed al-Ṣafā and began calling:
“O Banū Fihr! O Banū ʿAdī!”
calling the various clans of Quraysh until they gathered.
If a man was unable to come personally, he would send someone to find out what was happening.
Abū Lahab and the people of Quraysh came.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Tell me: if I informed you that cavalry in the valley were preparing to attack you, would you believe me?”
They replied:
“Yes. We have never known you to say anything but the truth.”
He said:
“Then I am a warner to you before the coming of a severe punishment.”
Abū Lahab said:
“May you perish for the rest of this day! Is this why you gathered us?”
Then Allah revealed:
“May the hands of Abū Lahab perish, and may he perish. His wealth and whatever he earned will not benefit him.” [Sūrat al-Masad 111:1–2]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4770) and Muslim (208).

13531
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Ibrāhīm will meet his father Āzar on the Day of Resurrection, and Āzar’s face will be covered with darkness and dust.
Ibrāhīm will say to him: ‘Did I not tell you not to disobey me?’
His father will say: ‘Today I will not disobey you.’
Ibrāhīm will then say: ‘My Lord, You promised me that You would not disgrace me on the Day they are resurrected. What disgrace could be greater than my father being far removed from mercy?’
Allah, the Exalted, will say: ‘I have forbidden Paradise to the disbelievers.’
Then it will be said: ‘O Ibrāhīm, what is beneath your feet?’
He will look and see a bloodstained male hyena. It will be seized by its legs and cast into the Fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3350).

13532
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
A man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, where is my father?”
He replied:
“In the Fire.”
When the man turned away, the Prophet ﷺ called him back and said:
“Indeed, my father and your father are in the Fire.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (203).

13533
Asmāʾ bint Abī Bakr narrated:
My mother came to me during the time of the Prophet ﷺ while she was still inclined toward her former religion.
I asked the Prophet ﷺ:
“Should I maintain ties with her?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
Ibn ʿUyaynah said:
Then Allah, the Exalted, revealed concerning her:
“Allah does not forbid you from being kind and just toward those who have not fought you because of religion…”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5978) and Muslim (1003).

13534
Al-Miswar ibn Makhramah and Marwān—each confirming the narration of the other—reported:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out during the time of al-Ḥudaybiyah.
They then narrated the hadith, in which it states:
Suhayl ibn ʿAmr came and said:
“Come, let us write an agreement between us and you.”
The Prophet ﷺ summoned the scribe and said:
“In the Name of Allah, the Most Merciful, the Especially Merciful.”
Suhayl said:
“As for ‘the Most Merciful,’ by Allah, I do not know what that means. Rather write: ‘In Your Name, O Allah,’ as you used to write.”
The Muslims said:
“By Allah, we will write nothing except: ‘In the Name of Allah, the Most Merciful, the Especially Merciful.’”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Write: ‘In Your Name, O Allah.’”
Then he said:
“This is what Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah, has agreed upon.”
Suhayl said:
“By Allah, if we knew that you were the Messenger of Allah, we would not have prevented you from reaching the House, nor would we have fought you. Rather write: ‘Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh.’”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“By Allah, I am indeed the Messenger of Allah even though you deny me. Write: ‘Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh.’”
Al-Zuhrī said that this was because of the Prophet’s statement:
“They will not ask me for any condition through which they seek to honor the sacred limits of Allah except that I will grant it to them.”
The Prophet ﷺ then said:
“On the condition that you allow us access to the House so that we may perform ṭawāf around it.”
Suhayl said:
“By Allah, the Arabs must not say that we were forced into this. Rather, you may do so next year.”
So it was written.
Suhayl then said:
“And on the condition that no man from among us comes to you—even if he follows your religion—except that you return him to us.”
The Muslims said:
“Glory be to Allah! How can he be returned to the polytheists after coming as a Muslim?”
While they were in this situation, Abū Jandal ibn Suhayl ibn ʿAmr came dragging his chains. He had escaped from the lower part of Makkah and threw himself among the Muslims.
Suhayl said:
“Muḥammad, this is the first person concerning whom I demand that you fulfill our agreement by returning him to me.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“We have not yet completed the written agreement.”
Suhayl said:
“Then, by Allah, I will never make peace with you over anything.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Then grant him to me as an exception.”
He said:
“I will not grant him to you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Yes, do so.”
He said:
“I will not.”
Mikraz said:
“We have granted him to you.”
Abū Jandal cried:
“O Muslims! Am I to be returned to the polytheists after I have come as a Muslim? Do you not see what I have suffered?”
He had been severely tortured for the sake of Allah.
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said:
I went to the Prophet of Allah ﷺ and asked:
“Are you not truly the Prophet of Allah?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
I said:
“Are we not upon the truth and our enemy upon falsehood?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
I said:
“Then why should we accept humiliation in our religion?”
He replied:
“I am the Messenger of Allah. I do not disobey Him, and He will support me.”
I asked:
“Did you not tell us that we would come to the House and perform ṭawāf around it?”
He said:
“Yes. But did I tell you that we would come to it this year?”
I said:
“No.”
He replied:
“Then you will certainly come to it and perform ṭawāf around it.”
ʿUmar continued:
I then went to Abū Bakr and said:
“Abū Bakr, is he not truly the Prophet of Allah?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
I said:
“Are we not upon the truth and our enemy upon falsehood?”
He said:
“Yes.”
I asked:
“Then why should we accept humiliation in our religion?”
He replied:
“Man, he is indeed the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He does not disobey his Lord, and Allah will support him. So hold firmly to his path, for by Allah, he is upon the truth.”
I said:
“Did he not tell us that we would come to the House and perform ṭawāf?”
He said:
“Yes. But did he tell you that you would come to it this year?”
I said:
“No.”
He replied:
“Then you will indeed come to it and perform ṭawāf around it.”
Al-Zuhrī said that ʿUmar later said:
“Because of that, I performed many deeds.”
When the written agreement had been completed, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to his Companions:
“Get up, slaughter your sacrificial animals, and then shave your heads.”
By Allah, not one of them stood up, even after he repeated it three times.
When none of them rose, he entered upon Umm Salamah and told her how the people had responded.
Umm Salamah said:
“O Prophet of Allah, do you want them to do that? Go out and do not speak a single word to any of them until you slaughter your sacrificial camel and call your barber to shave your head.”
So he went out and spoke to no one until he had done exactly that. He slaughtered his sacrificial animal and called his barber, who shaved his head.
When the Companions saw this, they got up and slaughtered their animals, and they began shaving one another’s heads, to the point that they nearly harmed one another out of grief.
Then believing women came to him, so Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“O you who believe, when believing women come to you as emigrants, test them…”
until the words:
“…and do not hold on to marriage bonds with disbelieving women.”
On that day, ʿUmar divorced two wives of his who remained upon polytheism. Muʿāwiyah ibn Abī Sufyān later married one of them, while Ṣafwān ibn Umayyah married the other.
The Prophet ﷺ then returned to Madinah.
Abū Baṣīr, a man from Quraysh who had embraced Islam, came to him.
Quraysh sent two men after him and said:
“Honor the covenant that you made with us.”
So the Prophet ﷺ handed Abū Baṣīr over to the two men.
They took him away until they reached Dhū al-Ḥulayfah, where they stopped to eat some dates.
Abū Baṣīr said to one of the men:
“By Allah, I think that sword of yours is a fine one.”
The man drew it and said:
“Yes, by Allah, it is indeed fine. I have tested it many times.”
Abū Baṣīr said:
“Let me see it.”
The man handed it to him, and Abū Baṣīr struck him with it until he died.
The other man fled until he reached Madinah and entered the mosque running.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw him, he said:
“This man has certainly seen something terrifying.”
When the man reached the Prophet ﷺ, he said:
“By Allah, my companion has been killed, and I am going to be killed as well!”
Then Abū Baṣīr arrived and said:
“O Prophet of Allah, by Allah, Allah has fulfilled your covenant. You returned me to them, then Allah saved me from them.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Woe to his mother! He is a kindler of war, if only he had others with him.”
When Abū Baṣīr heard this, he understood that the Prophet ﷺ would return him to Quraysh.
So he left and went to the coast.
Abū Jandal ibn Suhayl later escaped and joined Abū Baṣīr.
After that, whenever a man from Quraysh became Muslim, he would join Abū Baṣīr, until a group of them had gathered.
By Allah, whenever they heard of a Quraysh caravan heading toward al-Shām, they would intercept it, kill its men, and seize its wealth.
Quraysh eventually sent word to the Prophet ﷺ, appealing to him by Allah and by the ties of kinship to summon these men, promising that whoever came to him would be safe.
So the Prophet ﷺ sent for them.
Then Allah revealed:
“He is the One who restrained their hands from you and your hands from them in the valley of Makkah after He had given you victory over them…”
until:
“…the zeal of ignorance.” [Sūrat al-Fatḥ 48:24–26]
Their zeal of ignorance consisted of refusing to acknowledge that he was the Prophet of Allah, refusing the words “In the Name of Allah, the Most Merciful, the Especially Merciful,” and preventing the Muslims from reaching the Sacred House.
ʿUqayl narrated from al-Zuhrī that ʿUrwah said:
ʿĀʾishah informed me that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to test the believing women.
We were also informed that when Allah revealed that the Muslims should return to the polytheists whatever dowries they had spent upon wives who migrated away from them, and ruled that the Muslims should not retain marriage bonds with disbelieving women, ʿUmar divorced two wives: Qarībah bint Abī Umayyah and the daughter of Jarwal al-Khuzāʿī.
Muʿāwiyah married Qarībah, while Abū Jahm married the other woman.
When the disbelievers refused to repay what the Muslims had spent upon their wives, Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“And if any of your wives have gone over to the disbelievers and you later obtain compensation…” [Sūrat al-Mumtaḥanah 60:11]
The compensation referred to what the Muslims were to pay to a man whose wife from among the disbelievers had migrated away.
Allah commanded that any Muslim whose wife had gone over to the disbelievers should be reimbursed with an amount equal to the dowry he had spent, taken from what was due concerning the disbelieving women who had migrated to the Muslims.
And we do not know of any of the believing emigrant women who apostatized after having believed.
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2731, 2732, 2733).

13535
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
When believing women migrated to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, they would be tested according to the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“O Prophet, when the believing women come to you pledging that they will not associate anything with Allah, steal, commit fornication…”
to the end of the verse.
ʿĀʾishah said:
Whoever among the believing women affirmed these conditions had passed the test.
When they verbally affirmed them, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would say to them:
“Go, for I have accepted your pledge.”
By Allah, the hand of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ never touched the hand of a woman. He accepted their pledge only verbally.
ʿĀʾishah said:
By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ never imposed upon the women anything other than what Allah had commanded him, and the palm of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ never touched the palm of a woman.
When he accepted their pledge, he would simply say:
“I have accepted your pledge.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4891) and Muslim (1866). The wording is that of Muslim; al-Bukhārī’s wording is abbreviated.

13536
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
I attended the ʿĪd al-Fiṭr prayer with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, Abū Bakr, ʿUmar, and ʿUthmān. All of them would perform the prayer before the sermon and then deliver the sermon afterward.
The Prophet of Allah ﷺ came down, and I can still picture him motioning with his hand for the men to remain seated.
Then he made his way through the people until he reached the women, accompanied by Bilāl.
He recited:
“O Prophet, when the believing women come to you pledging that they will not associate anything with Allah, steal, commit fornication, kill their children, or fabricate a falsehood between their hands and feet…”
until he completed the entire verse.
Then he asked:
“Are you committed to this?”
One woman replied, while none of the others answered:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
Al-Ḥasan did not know who that woman was.
The Prophet ﷺ then said:
“Then give charity.”
Bilāl spread out his garment, and the women began throwing their large rings and other jewelry into Bilāl’s garment.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4895) and Muslim (884). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim narrated similar wording.

13537
Umm ʿAṭiyyah narrated:
We pledged allegiance to the Prophet ﷺ, and he recited to us:
“That they will not associate anything with Allah.”
He also forbade us from wailing over the dead.
One woman among us withdrew her hand and said:
“So-and-so’s family supported me in wailing, and I wish to repay them.”
The Prophet ﷺ did not say anything to her.
She went away and later returned.
No woman fully fulfilled the pledge except Umm Sulaym, Umm al-ʿAlāʾ, the daughter of Abū Sabrah—the wife of Muʿādh—or the daughter of Abū Sabrah, and the wife of Muʿādh.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7215). Muslim (937) also narrated it through another route from Ḥafṣah.
Muslim’s wording states that Umm ʿAṭiyyah said:
When this verse was revealed, one of the things it included was the prohibition of wailing.
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, except the family of so-and-so. They supported me in wailing during the Jāhiliyyah, so I must repay them in the same way.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Except the family of so-and-so.”

13538
Umm ʿAṭiyyah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ took from us, as part of our pledge, that we would not wail over the dead.
Only five women among us fully upheld this: Umm Sulaym, Umm al-ʿAlāʾ, the daughter of Abū Sabrah—the wife of Muʿādh—and two other women.
Or: the daughter of Abū Sabrah, the wife of Muʿādh, and another woman.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1306) and Muslim (936).

13539
ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit narrated:
We were sitting with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when he said:
“Pledge allegiance to me that you will not associate anything with Allah, commit fornication, steal, or kill a soul whom Allah has forbidden to be killed except with right.
Whoever among you fulfills this, his reward is with Allah. Whoever commits any of these acts and is punished for it, that punishment will be an expiation for him. And whoever commits any of these acts and Allah conceals it for him, his affair rests with Allah: if He wills, He will pardon him, and if He wills, He will punish him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4894) and Muslim (1709).

13540
ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit said:
I was among the chiefs who pledged allegiance to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He said:
“We pledged allegiance to him that we would not associate anything with Allah, steal, commit fornication, kill a soul whom Allah has forbidden to be killed except with right, plunder, or disobey. If we fulfilled this, Paradise would be ours. If we committed any of these things, its judgment would be left to Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3893) and Muslim (1709:44).

13541
ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit al-Anṣārī narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ took from us the same pledge that he took from the women:
that we would not associate anything with Allah, steal, commit fornication, kill our children, or falsely accuse one another.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever among you fulfills this, his reward is with Allah. Whoever among you commits an offense carrying a prescribed punishment and that punishment is carried out upon him, it is an expiation for him. And whoever Allah conceals, his affair is with Allah: if He wills, He will punish him, and if He wills, He will forgive him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1709:43).

13542
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words of Allah:
“And they will not disobey you in what is right”:
“This was a condition that Allah specifically stipulated for the women.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4893).
Important benefit: The pledge of allegiance was first specifically given to the Prophet ﷺ. After him, it was given to the caliphs, and thereafter this pledge continued to be given to the rulers and leaders of the Muslims.
As for what the Sufis and those who followed similar practices later introduced, the author states that it has no basis in the Qur’an or the Sunnah.

13543
ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām narrated:
A group of us from among the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sat together and discussed matters among ourselves.
We said:
“If only we knew which deeds are most beloved to Allah, we would perform them.”
So Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“Whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth glorifies Allah, and He is the Almighty, the All-Wise. O you who believe, why do you say what you do not do?”
ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām said:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then recited it to us.”
Abū Salamah said:
“Ibn Salām recited it to us.”
Yaḥyā said:
“Abū Salamah recited it to us.”
Ibn Kathīr said:
“Al-Awzāʿī recited it to us.”
ʿAbdullāh said:
“Ibn Kathīr recited it to us.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3309), al-Dārimī (2435), al-Ḥākim (2/69), and Ibn Ḥibbān (4594).
Some commentators said that these verses were revealed concerning the hypocrites, who would promise support to the believers while lying.
Thus, failing to fulfill one’s promise became one of the characteristics of hypocrisy.

13544
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The signs of a hypocrite are three: when he speaks, he lies; when he is entrusted, he betrays the trust; and when he promises, he breaks his promise.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2682) and Muslim (59). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13545
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“There are four qualities which, if all are found in a person, make him a complete hypocrite; and whoever possesses one of them possesses a characteristic of hypocrisy until he abandons it: when entrusted, he betrays; when he speaks, he lies; when he makes a covenant, he acts treacherously; and when he disputes, he behaves wickedly.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (34) and Muslim (58). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī; Muslim’s wording is similar.

13546
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There are three at whom Allah laughs: a man who rises during the night, people when they form rows for prayer, and people when they form rows for battle.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (200) and Aḥmad (11761).

13547
Abū Dharr narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“There are three whom Allah, Mighty and Majestic, loves:
A man who comes to a people and asks them by Allah, not because of any kinship between him and them, but they refuse him. Then one man remains behind after them and secretly gives to him, so that no one knows of his gift except Allah, Mighty and Majestic, and the one who received it.
And a group of people who travel through the night until sleep becomes dearer to them than anything comparable to it, so they stop and lay down their heads, but one man rises and humbles himself before Me and recites My verses.
And a man who is in a military detachment that meets the enemy, and his companions flee, but he advances facing the enemy until he is killed or victory is granted to him.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (1615) and al-Tirmidhī (2568).

13548
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
On the Day of Ḥunayn, the Prophet ﷺ favored certain people in the distribution of wealth.
He gave al-Aqraʿ ibn Ḥābis one hundred camels, gave ʿUyaynah the same, and gave gifts to a number of the Arab chiefs, favoring them in the distribution that day.
A man said:
“By Allah, there is no justice in this distribution, and it was not intended for the sake of Allah!”
I said:
“By Allah, I will certainly inform the Prophet ﷺ.”
So I went to him and told him.
He said:
“Who, then, will act justly if Allah and His Messenger do not act justly? May Allah have mercy on Mūsā. He was harmed with more than this, yet he remained patient.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3150) and Muslim (1062).

13549
Khālid ibn Maʿdān narrated from some of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that they said:
“O Messenger of Allah, tell us about yourself.”
He replied:
“I am the supplication of my father Ibrāhīm and the glad tidings of ʿĪsā. And when my mother was pregnant with me, she saw a light emerge from her by which Buṣrā in the land of al-Shām was illuminated.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Isḥāq in al-Sīrah (paragraph 33), al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (2/573), al-Ḥākim (2/600), and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (1/83). Al-Ḥākim authenticated it.

13550
Ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent us to al-Najāshī, and we were approximately eighty men.
He then mentioned the hadith concerning the migration of the Muslims to the land of Abyssinia. It includes that al-Najāshī picked up a small stick from the ground and said:
“O people of Abyssinia, priests, and monks! By Allah, what these people say about him does not exceed what we say by even the amount of this stick. Welcome to you and to the one from whom you have come. I bear witness that he is the Messenger of Allah. He is the one whom we find mentioned in the Gospel, and he is the Messenger whose coming ʿĪsā son of Maryam foretold. Settle wherever you wish. By Allah, were it not for the kingship that I hold, I would go to him so that I might carry his sandals and pour water for his ablution.”
He ordered that the gifts of the other two men be returned to them.
The hadith continues.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (4400), al-Ḥākim (2/623), and al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (2/298).

13551
Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“I have several names: I am Muḥammad, I am Aḥmad, I am al-Māḥī—the one through whom Allah erases disbelief; I am al-Ḥāshir—the one at whose feet the people will be gathered; and I am al-ʿĀqib.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4896) and Muslim (2354:125).

13552
Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to mention to us several of his names, saying:
“I am Muḥammad, Aḥmad, al-Muqaffī, al-Ḥāshir, the Prophet of Repentance, and the Prophet of Mercy.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2355).

13553
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that in the Fajr prayer on Friday, the Prophet ﷺ used to recite:
“Alif-Lām-Mīm. The revelation…” [Sūrat al-Sajdah]
and:
“Has there not come upon man a period of time…” [Sūrat al-Insān 76:1]
The Prophet ﷺ would also recite Sūrat al-Jumuʿah and Sūrat al-Munāfiqūn in the Friday prayer.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Friday Prayer (789).

13554
Ibn Abī Rāfiʿ narrated:
Marwān appointed Abū Hurayrah as his deputy over Madinah and departed for Makkah.
Abū Hurayrah led us in the Friday prayer. In the second rakʿah, after reciting Sūrat al-Jumuʿah in the first, he recited:
“When the hypocrites come to you…”
I caught up with Abū Hurayrah after he finished and said to him:
“You recited two sūrahs that ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib used to recite in Kūfah.”
Abū Hurayrah replied:
“I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recite these two sūrahs on Friday.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (877).

13555
ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
Al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays wrote to al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr asking him:
“What did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recite in the Friday prayer besides Sūrat al-Jumuʿah?”
He replied:
“He used to recite: ‘Has there reached you…’” [Sūrat al-Ghāshiyah 88:1]
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (878:63).

13556
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
We were sitting with the Prophet ﷺ when Sūrat al-Jumuʿah was revealed to him.
When he recited:
“And others from among them who have not yet joined them,”
a man asked:
“Who are these people, O Messenger of Allah?”
The Prophet ﷺ did not answer him until the man asked once, twice, or three times.
Salmān al-Fārisī was among us.
The Prophet ﷺ placed his hand upon Salmān and said:
“If faith were at the Pleiades, men from among these people would certainly reach it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4898) and Muslim (2546:231). The wording is that of Muslim; al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.

13557
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“If the religion were at the Pleiades, a man from Persia—or from the descendants of Persia—would go until he attained it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2546).
The author states that the miraculous nature of the statement of the Prophet ﷺ concerning the people of Persia became evident.
By this he means the non-Arab peoples who lived in the regions beyond the river, from whom emerged great hadith scholars, Qur’anic commentators, jurists, scholars of legal theory, linguists, and many other scholars of Islam.

13558
Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Indeed, in the loins of the loins of the loins of men are men and women from my Ummah who will enter Paradise without reckoning.”
Then he recited:
“And others from among them who have not yet joined them. And He is the Almighty, the All-Wise.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Sunnah (309), Ibn Abī Ḥātim in his Tafsīr (10/3355), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (6/248).

13559
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Abū Jahl said:
“If I see Muḥammad praying near the Kaʿbah, I will go to him and place my foot upon his neck.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Had he done so, the angels would have seized him before everyone’s eyes. Had the Jews wished for death, they would have died and seen their places in the Fire. And had those who challenged the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to mutual invocation of Allah’s curse gone forth for it, they would have returned to find neither family nor wealth.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (2226), Abū Yaʿlā (2604)—whose wording is given here—and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2189). Its isnād is authentic.
These events occurred at different times during the life of the Prophet ﷺ.
Some occurred in Makkah, others took place in Madinah after his migration there, and others happened near the end of his life.
Ibn ʿAbbās gathered all of these events together in a single narration even though he had not personally witnessed some of them.

13560
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“We are the last, yet we will be the first on the Day of Resurrection. They were given the Scripture before us, while we were given it after them. This is the day that was prescribed for them, but they differed concerning it, so Allah guided us to it. Thus they come after us regarding it: the Jews have tomorrow, and the Christians the day after tomorrow.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6624) and Muslim (855:21).

13561
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The best day upon which the sun has risen is Friday. On it Ādam was created, on it he was admitted into Paradise, and on it he was expelled from it. And the Hour will not be established except on a Friday.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (854:18).

13562
Ibn ʿUmar narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“When one of you comes for the Friday prayer, let him perform ghusl.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in the Book of Friday Prayer (5), al-Bukhārī (877), and Muslim (844).

13563
Salmān al-Fārisī narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked me:
“Do you know what Friday is?”
I replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
Then he asked again:
“Do you know what Friday is?”
I replied:
“Yes.”
The narrator said that he did not know whether the Prophet ﷺ asked him a fourth time or not.
Salmān said:
“It is the day on which your father—or our father—was brought together.”
