The Prophet ﷺ then said:
“Shall I tell you about Friday? No Muslim man purifies himself, then walks to the mosque, and then remains silent and listens until the imam completes his prayer, except that it is an expiation for what occurs between that Friday and the following Friday, so long as the grave sins are avoided.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (23729) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (6089).
Regarding the words:
“Hasten to the remembrance of Allah,”
this is a command from Allah, the Exalted, to the believers to give due importance to the Friday prayer and to proceed to it promptly, especially after hearing the adhān.
It does not mean physically running to the prayer, because that has been prohibited, as stated in authentic hadiths:

13564
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“When the prayer has been established, do not come to it running. Come walking and maintain calmness. Whatever you catch, pray; and whatever you miss, complete.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (636) and Muslim (602). The wording is that of Muslim.

13565
Abū Qatādah narrated:
While we were praying with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he heard the noise of some men.
After completing the prayer, he asked:
“What happened to you?”
They replied:
“We hurried to catch the prayer.”
He said:
“Do not do that. When you come to the prayer, maintain calmness. Whatever you catch, pray; and whatever you miss, complete.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (635) and Muslim (603).

13566
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
We were with the Prophet ﷺ on a Friday when a small trading caravan arrived.
The people went out toward it until only twelve men remained, and I was among them.
Then Allah revealed:
“When they see trade or amusement, they rush toward it and leave you standing…”
to the end of the verse.
In another narration:
The Prophet ﷺ was standing and delivering the Friday sermon when a caravan arrived from al-Shām, so the people turned away toward it.
In another narration:
The Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ rushed toward it until only twelve men remained with him, among them Abū Bakr and ʿUmar.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4899) and Muslim (863). The wording is that of Muslim. The second and third versions are also narrated by Muslim through another route.
The word “suwayqah” is the diminutive form of sūq, meaning a small market.
What is intended here is the caravan, as stated in the second narration. It refers to camels carrying food or merchandise.
The author explains that it was called a market because merchandise was driven and brought to it.
The phrase:
“The people turned away toward it”
means that they left for it and hurried toward it.

13567
Abū ʿUbaydah narrated:
Kaʿb ibn ʿUjrah entered the mosque while ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Umm al-Ḥakam was delivering the sermon while seated.
Kaʿb said:
“Look at this wicked man delivering the sermon while seated, when Allah, the Exalted, says:
‘When they see trade or amusement, they rush toward it and leave you standing.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (864).
Sūrat al-Jumuʿah contains a refutation of three claims of the Jews:
The first was their boast that they were a people of Scripture while the Arabs had no Scripture.
Allah compared them to a donkey in His words:
“The example of those who were entrusted with the Torah but then failed to uphold it is like that of a donkey carrying books.”
The second was their claim that they were Allah’s allies and beloved ones to the exclusion of other people.
Allah invalidated their claim by saying:
“Then wish for death, if you are truthful.”
The third was their boast that they had the Sabbath while the Muslims had no equivalent day.
So Allah prescribed Friday for the Muslims and said:
“O you who believe, when the call is made for prayer on Friday, hasten to the remembrance of Allah.”
As established in the authentic hadith mentioned above, the Jews and Christians went astray concerning the appointed day, while Allah guided the Muslims to Friday.

13568
Zayd ibn Arqam narrated:
We went out with the Prophet ﷺ on a journey during which the people suffered hardship.
ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy said to his companions:
“Do not spend on those who are with the Messenger of Allah until they disperse from around him.”
He also said:
“If we return to Madinah, the more honorable will surely expel from it the more humiliated.”
I went to the Prophet ﷺ and informed him.
He sent for ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy and questioned him, but ʿAbdullāh swore emphatically that he had not said it.
The people said:
“Zayd has lied to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
What they said caused me great distress, until Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed confirmation of my truthfulness in:
“When the hypocrites come to you…”
The Prophet ﷺ then called them so that he might seek forgiveness for them, but they turned their heads away.
Regarding the words:
“They are like propped-up pieces of timber,”
Zayd said:
“They were men of extremely handsome appearance.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4903) and Muslim (2772).
Al-Bukhārī (4901) added through another route from Zayd ibn Arqam:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent for me, recited the sūrah to me, and then said:
“Indeed, Allah has confirmed that you spoke the truth.”
This sūrah was revealed concerning the expedition of Banū al-Muṣṭaliq in the fifth year after the Hijrah. The discussion of this incident is given in detail in the Book of Sīrah.

13569
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
We were on a military expedition when a man from the Muhājirūn struck a man from the Anṣār on his backside.
The Anṣārī called out:
“O Anṣār!”
And the Muhājir called out:
“O Muhājirūn!”
Allah caused His Messenger ﷺ to hear this, and he asked:
“What is this?”
They told him:
“A man from the Muhājirūn struck a man from the Anṣār. The Anṣārī called out, ‘O Anṣār!’ and the Muhājir called out, ‘O Muhājirūn!’”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Leave it, for it is foul.”
Jābir said:
When the Prophet ﷺ first came to Madinah, the Anṣār were more numerous, but afterward the Muhājirūn increased in number.
ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy said:
“Have they really done this? By Allah, if we return to Madinah, the more honorable will surely expel from it the more humiliated.”
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said:
“O Messenger of Allah, allow me to strike the neck of this hypocrite.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Leave him, lest the people say that Muḥammad kills his own Companions.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4907) and Muslim (2584:63). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
The word “kasaʿa” means that he struck him on his backside.

13570
Busr ibn Jaḥḥāsh al-Qurashī narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ spat into his palm, then placed his index finger upon it and said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says: ‘How can you escape Me, O son of Ādam, when I created you from something like this? Then when your soul reaches here’”—and he pointed to his throat—“‘you say: I will give charity! But how can it be the time for charity then?’”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (2707), Aḥmad (17842), and al-Ḥākim (2/502).
Al-Ḥākim added that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recited:
“What is the matter with those who disbelieve, rushing toward you, from the right and from the left in separate groups? Does every man among them hope to be admitted into a Garden of Bliss? No indeed! We created them from what they know.” [Sūrat al-Maʿārij 70:36–39]
Al-Ḥākim said:
“Its chain is authentic.”

13571
Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was delivering a sermon to us when al-Ḥasan and al-Ḥusayn came wearing two red shirts, walking and stumbling.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ descended from the pulpit, picked them both up, and placed them in front of him.
Then he said:
“Allah has spoken the truth: ‘Your wealth and your children are only a trial.’ I looked at these two boys walking and stumbling, and I could not bear it until I interrupted my speech and picked them up.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1109), al-Tirmidhī (3774), al-Nasāʾī (1413, 1585), and Ibn Mājah (3600). It was authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (1456), Ibn Ḥibbān (6039), and al-Ḥākim (1/287).

13572
Al-Ashʿath ibn Qays narrated:
I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ as part of the delegation of Kindah.
He asked me:
“Do you have any children?”
I replied:
“A boy was born to me from the daughter of Jamd while I was setting out to come to you, and I would have preferred that in his place the people had been given a satisfying meal.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not say that. Indeed, children can be a source of joy to the eyes, and there is reward when they are taken in death. Yet if you say such a thing, then indeed they can be a cause of cowardice and sorrow; indeed, they can be a cause of cowardice and sorrow.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (21840) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (1/207). The wording is that of Aḥmad; al-Ṭabarānī’s wording is similar and adds: “a cause of miserliness.”

13573
Al-Ashʿath ibn Qays narrated:
A boy was born to me, and I received the glad tidings while I was with the Prophet ﷺ.
I said:
“I would have preferred that, in his place, you had a bowl of bread and meat.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“If you say that! Indeed, children can be a cause of miserliness, cowardice, and sorrow; yet they are also the fruit of the hearts and the delight of the eyes.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (4/239). Its isnād is authentic. Al-Ḥākim said: “This hadith is authentic according to the conditions of the two Shaykhs.”
Regarding the words:
“So fear Allah as much as you are able,”
this means by obeying His commands and avoiding His prohibitions.
Allah restricted this by a person’s ability and capacity. Thus, according to the author, this verse indicates that any obligation which a servant is genuinely unable to perform is lifted from him.
If he is able to perform part of what he has been commanded but unable to perform another part, then he must do what he is capable of, while what he is incapable of is waived from him.
There is no contradiction between this verse and the verse in Sūrat Āl ʿImrān:
“O you who believe, fear Allah as He truly deserves to be feared, and do not die except as Muslims.” [Sūrat Āl ʿImrān 3:102]
For truly fearing Allah means that the servant does everything within his ability and does not act negligently regarding it.

13574
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah descends to the lowest heaven at the middle of the night—or during the last third of the night—and says: ‘Who will call upon Me so that I may answer him? Who will ask Me so that I may give him?’ Then He says: ‘Who will lend to One who is neither poor nor unjust?’”
In another narration:
“Then He, Blessed and Exalted, extends His hands and says: ‘Who will lend to One who is neither deprived nor unjust?’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (758:171). Muslim also narrated the second wording.

13575
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar divorced his wife while she was menstruating.
ʿUmar mentioned this to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ became angry about it.
He then said:
“Let him take her back, then keep her until she becomes pure, then menstruates again and becomes pure. If he then wishes to divorce her, let him divorce her while she is pure, before having intercourse with her. That is the waiting period according to which Allah has commanded women to be divorced.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4908) and Muslim (1471:4). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī; Muslim’s wording is similar.

13576
Ibn Jurayj said:
Abū al-Zubayr informed me that he heard ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ayman, the freedman of ʿAzzah, ask Ibn ʿUmar, while Abū al-Zubayr was listening:
“What is your view concerning a man who divorces his wife while she is menstruating?”
Ibn ʿUmar replied:
Ibn ʿUmar divorced his wife while she was menstruating during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
ʿUmar asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about it, saying:
“ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar divorced his wife while she was menstruating.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Let him take her back.”
So he took her back.
The Prophet ﷺ then said:
“When she becomes pure, let him either divorce her or keep her.”
Ibn ʿUmar said that the Prophet ﷺ recited:
“O Prophet, when you divorce women, divorce them at the beginning of their waiting period.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1471:14).
The wording:
“Divorce them at the beginning of their waiting period”
is an irregular recitational wording. It appears to function as an explanatory interpretation from some of the Companions; otherwise, the mass-transmitted Qur’anic recitation is the wording found in the muṣḥaf.

13577
ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn was asked about a man who divorced his wife and then resumed marital relations with her without having witnesses either to the divorce or to taking her back.
He replied:
“You divorced contrary to the Sunnah, and you took her back contrary to the Sunnah. Have witnesses to her divorce and to taking her back, and do not do this again.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2186) and Ibn Mājah (2025).
The discussion of this matter is given in detail in the Book of Divorce.
The command to have witnesses here is, according to the author, a recommendation rather than an obligation.
It is similar to the words of Allah:
“And take witnesses when you conduct a transaction.” [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:282]
Having witnesses to a sale is not obligatory; likewise, according to the author, having witnesses when taking a wife back or resolving upon separation is not obligatory.
Regarding the words:
“Whoever relies upon Allah, He is sufficient for him,”
Allah will protect and suffice such a person even if people oppose him and become hostile toward him.
This is stated in the hadith:

13578
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
One day I was riding behind the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when he said:
“Young man, I will teach you some words: preserve the limits of Allah and He will preserve you. Preserve the limits of Allah and you will find Him before you. When you ask, ask Allah; and when you seek help, seek help from Allah.
Know that if the entire nation were to gather together to benefit you with something, they could benefit you only with something Allah had already written for you. And if they all gathered together to harm you with something, they could harm you only with something Allah had already written against you. The pens have been lifted and the pages have dried.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2516) and Ibn Wahb in al-Qadar (28).
The discussion of this hadith is given in detail in the Book of Faith.

13579
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever is afflicted by poverty and turns to people to relieve it, his poverty will not be satisfied. But whoever turns to Allah with it, Allah will soon bring him sufficiency, either through an imminent death or an imminent enrichment.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1645) and al-Tirmidhī (2327).
The discussion of this hadith is given in detail in the Book of Zakāh.

13580
Abū Salamah narrated:
A man came to Ibn ʿAbbās while Abū Hurayrah was sitting with him and asked:
“Give me a ruling concerning a woman who gave birth forty nights after the death of her husband.”
Ibn ʿAbbās replied:
“She must wait until the later of the two prescribed periods.”
I said:
“But Allah says: ‘The term of pregnant women is until they deliver their burdens.’”
Abū Hurayrah said:
“I am with my nephew,”
meaning Abū Salamah.
Ibn ʿAbbās then sent his servant Kurayb to Umm Salamah to ask her.
She replied:
“The husband of Subayʿah al-Aslamiyyah was killed while she was pregnant. She gave birth forty nights after his death. Men then proposed marriage to her, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ married her to one of them. Abū al-Sanābil was among those who had proposed to her.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4909) and Muslim (1485).

13581
Muḥammad ibn Sīrīn narrated:
I sat in a gathering containing many prominent men from the Anṣār, among them ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Laylā.
I mentioned the hadith of ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUtbah concerning Subayʿah bint al-Ḥārith.
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said:
“But his uncle did not hold that view.”
I replied, raising my voice:
“I would certainly be bold indeed if I were to lie about a man who is there in Kūfah.”
Then I left and met Mālik ibn ʿĀmir—or Mālik ibn ʿAwf.
I asked:
“What was Ibn Masʿūd’s view regarding a pregnant woman whose husband dies?”
He replied that Ibn Masʿūd said:
“Would you impose the stricter ruling upon her and not grant her the concession? The shorter Sūrat al-Nisāʾ was certainly revealed after the longer one.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4532).
The expression:
“The shorter Sūrat al-Nisāʾ after the longer one”
means that Sūrat al-Ṭalāq was revealed after Sūrat al-Baqarah.

13582
Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm narrated that Abū Salamah told him that there was a dispute between him and some of his people concerning a piece of land.
He entered upon ʿĀʾishah and mentioned the matter to her.
She said:
“O Abū Salamah, keep away from taking land unjustly, for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
‘Whoever wrongfully takes even a span of land will have it encircled around him from seven earths.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2453) and Muslim (1612). The wording is that of Muslim; al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.
Regarding the words:
“His command descends among them,”
this means revelation and divine administration.
Qatādah said:
“In every earth of His earths and every heaven of His heavens are creatures from among His creation, commands from among His commands, and decrees from among His decrees.”
Regarding the words:
“So that you may know that Allah has power over all things and that Allah has encompassed all things in knowledge,”
nothing whatsoever is hidden from Him.

13583
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ used to stay for a while with Zaynab bint Jaḥsh and drink honey at her home.
Ḥafṣah and I agreed that whichever one of us the Prophet ﷺ entered upon should say to him:
“I smell the odor of maghāfīr on you. Have you eaten maghāfīr?”
When he entered upon one of them, she said this to him.
He replied:
“Rather, I drank honey at the home of Zaynab bint Jaḥsh, and I will not drink it again.”
Then the verses were revealed:
“O Prophet, why do you prohibit what Allah has made lawful for you…”
until:
“If you both repent to Allah…”
concerning ʿĀʾishah and Ḥafṣah.
In another narration, the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I have sworn an oath, so do not tell anyone about this.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5267) and Muslim (1474). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī; Muslim’s wording is similar.
This account is more authentic than the story that follows.
ʿĀʾishah also narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ loved honey and sweet foods. When he finished the ʿAṣr prayer, he would visit his wives and draw close to one of them.
He entered upon Ḥafṣah bint ʿUmar and stayed with her longer than usual, so I became jealous and asked about it.
I was told:
“A woman from her people gave her a container of honey, and she gave the Prophet ﷺ some of it to drink.”
I said:
“By Allah, we will devise a plan concerning this.”
I said to Sawdah bint Zamʿah:
“He will come close to you. When he does, say: ‘Have you eaten maghāfīr?’ He will say: ‘No.’ Then say: ‘What is this odor I smell on you?’ He will say: ‘Ḥafṣah gave me some honey to drink.’ Then say: ‘Its bees must have fed on ʿurfuṭ.’ I will say the same thing, and you, Ṣafiyyah, should say the same.”
Sawdah said:
“By Allah, as soon as he stood at the door, I almost blurted out what you had told me to say because I was afraid of you.”
When he came near her, Sawdah asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, have you eaten maghāfīr?”
He replied:
“No.”
She asked:
“Then what is this odor I smell on you?”
He said:
“Ḥafṣah gave me a drink of honey.”
She said:
“Its bees must have fed on the ʿurfuṭ plant.”
When he later came to me, I said something similar.
When he came to Ṣafiyyah, she said the same.
Then when he returned to Ḥafṣah, she asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, shall I give you some more of it?”
He replied:
“I have no need for it.”
Sawdah said:
“By Allah, we deprived him of it!”
I said to her:
“Be quiet.”
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5268) and Muslim (1474:21).
Regarding the words:
“And when the Prophet confided a matter to one of his wives,”
the author explains that this refers to the incident of drinking honey and his statement:
“I will not return to it.”
It has also been said that the reason for the revelation was that the Prophet ﷺ prohibited his bondwoman Māriyah al-Qibṭiyyah to himself.

13584
Anas narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had a bondwoman with whom he had intimate relations.
ʿĀʾishah and Ḥafṣah continued pressing him concerning her until he prohibited her to himself.
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“O Prophet, why do you prohibit what Allah has made lawful for you?”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (3959).
The bondwoman was Māriyah al-Qibṭiyyah, the mother of Ibrāhīm.

13585
ʿUmar narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ said to Ḥafṣah:
“Do not tell anyone, and indeed the mother of Ibrāhīm is forbidden to me.”
She said:
“Do you forbid what Allah has made lawful for you?”
He replied:
“By Allah, I will not approach her.”
He did not approach her until Ḥafṣah informed ʿĀʾishah.
Then Allah revealed:
“Allah has already prescribed for you a way to release yourselves from your oaths.”
Good: Narrated by al-Haytham ibn Kulayb in his Musnad, as cited by Ibn Kathīr in his Tafsīr.

13586
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the verse:
“O Prophet, why do you prohibit what Allah has made lawful for you?”
“This was revealed concerning his bondwoman.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (4946), whose wording is given here, and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (11/86).
In summary, the author states that there is nothing preventing all of these incidents from having been among the causes connected to the revelation of the verse of prohibition. Some commentators held this view.
Nevertheless, the most authentic report is the incident of drinking honey at the home of Zaynab, through the narration of Ḥajjāj ibn Muḥammad from Ibn Jurayj, as previously mentioned.
Al-Qāḍī ʿIyāḍ said that this account is more consistent with the apparent meaning of the Qur’anic text and carries the fullest benefit.

13587
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“If a man declares his wife forbidden to himself, it is an oath for which he must offer expiation.”
Then he recited:
“Indeed, in the Messenger of Allah you have an excellent example.” [Sūrat al-Aḥzāb 33:21]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5266) and Muslim (1473:19). The wording is that of Muslim.

13588
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
For an entire year I wanted to ask ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb about a verse, but I could not bring myself to ask him because of the awe I felt toward him.
Then he went out for Ḥajj, and I went with him.
On the return journey, while we were somewhere along the road, he turned aside toward some arāk trees to relieve himself. I waited for him until he had finished, then walked alongside him and asked:
“O Commander of the Believers, which two of the wives of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ were the ones who supported one another against him?”
He replied:
“They were Ḥafṣah and ʿĀʾishah.”
I said:
“By Allah, I have wanted to ask you about this for a year, but I could not because of my awe of you.”
He said:
“Do not do that. Whatever knowledge you think I may possess, ask me about it. If I know it, I will tell you.”
ʿUmar continued:
“By Allah, during the Jāhiliyyah we did not regard women as having much say in matters until Allah revealed concerning them what He revealed and allotted to them what He allotted.”
He said:
While I was considering some matter of mine, my wife said to me:
“Why do you not do such-and-such?”
I said to her:
“What concern is it of yours what I am doing here? Why do you involve yourself in a matter that I intend?”
She replied:
“How astonishing you are, O son of al-Khaṭṭāb! You do not want anyone to answer you back, yet your own daughter answers the Messenger of Allah ﷺ back until he remains angry for the entire day!”
ʿUmar said:
I immediately took my cloak and went out until I entered upon Ḥafṣah.
I said to her:
“My daughter! Do you really answer the Messenger of Allah ﷺ back until he remains angry throughout the day?”
Ḥafṣah replied:
“By Allah, we do indeed answer him back.”
I said:
“You know that I warn you of the punishment of Allah and the anger of His Messenger. My daughter, do not let this woman deceive you—the one who has been pleased by her own beauty and by the love of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for her.”
He meant ʿĀʾishah.
ʿUmar continued:
Then I went out and entered upon Umm Salamah because of the family relationship between us, and I spoke to her.
Umm Salamah said:
“How astonishing you are, O son of al-Khaṭṭāb! You have involved yourself in everything, until now you even wish to come between the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his wives!”
ʿUmar said:
She spoke to me in a way that broke some of the intensity I had been feeling.
I left her.
I had a companion from the Anṣār. Whenever I was absent, he would bring me the news, and whenever he was absent, I would bring him the news.
At that time, we were fearful of one of the kings of Ghassān. We had been told that he intended to march against us, and our hearts were filled with fear because of him.
Then my Anṣārī companion came knocking at the door and called:
“Open! Open!”
I asked:
“Has the Ghassānid come?”
He replied:
“Something even more serious than that. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has separated himself from his wives.”
I said:
“May the noses of Ḥafṣah and ʿĀʾishah be rubbed in the dust!”
Then I took my clothes and went out until I reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
He was in an upper room that could be reached by a ladder. A black servant of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was sitting at the top of the stairs.
I said:
“It is ʿUmar.”
So permission was given for me to enter.
ʿUmar continued:
I related this whole matter to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
When I reached the part concerning what Umm Salamah had said to me, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ smiled.
He was lying upon a woven mat with nothing between him and it. Beneath his head was a leather cushion stuffed with palm fiber. Near his feet was a pile of tanning leaves, and near his head were some hides hanging.
I saw the marks of the mat upon the side of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and I began to weep.
He asked:
“What makes you weep?”
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Kisrā and Caesar enjoy what they enjoy, while you are the Messenger of Allah?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Are you not pleased that they should have this world while you have the Hereafter?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4913) and Muslim (1479:31). This wording is that of Muslim.

13589
Anas narrated that ʿUmar said:
“My view coincided with my Lord in three matters.”
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, if only we were to take the Station of Ibrāhīm as a place of prayer.”
Then Allah revealed:
“Take the Station of Ibrāhīm as a place of prayer.” [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:125]
And concerning the verse of ḥijāb, I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, if only you instructed your wives to observe seclusion, for both the righteous and the wicked speak to them.”
Then the verse of ḥijāb was revealed.
And when the wives of the Prophet ﷺ united in jealousy concerning him, I said to them:
“Perhaps, if he divorces you, his Lord will replace you with wives better than you.”
Then this verse was revealed.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (402).

13590
Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Many men attained perfection, but among women none attained perfection except Maryam bint ʿImrān and Āsiyah, the wife of Pharaoh. And the superiority of ʿĀʾishah over other women is like the superiority of tharīd over all other foods.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3769) and Muslim (2431:37).

13591
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ drew four lines on the ground and asked:
“Do you know what this is?”
They replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The best women of Paradise are Khadījah bint Khuwaylid, Fāṭimah bint Muḥammad, Āsiyah bint Muzāḥim—the wife of Pharaoh—and Maryam bint ʿImrān.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (2668), ʿAbd ibn Ḥumayd (597), al-Ṭaḥāwī in Sharḥ al-Mushkil (148), and Ibn Ḥibbān (7010).
Regarding the words:
“And Maryam, daughter of ʿImrān,”
she is the mother of ʿĪsā ﷺ.
She is referred to as the sister of Hārūn, but this Hārūn was not the Prophet Hārūn who lived in the time of Mūsā ﷺ, as stated in the authentic hadith:

13592
Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah narrated:
When I came to Najrān, they asked me:
“You recite: ‘O sister of Hārūn,’ yet Mūsā lived such-and-such a long time before ʿĪsā.”
When I returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, I asked him about this.
He replied:
“They used to name people after the prophets and righteous people who came before them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2135).
This means that Maryam, the mother of ʿĪsā, was not the sister of Hārūn, the brother of Mūsā.
Rather, she was associated with another Hārūn who had been named after Hārūn, the brother of Mūsā, because he belonged to his people.
Other hadiths concerning the virtues of Āsiyah and Maryam have also been narrated, and they are mentioned in the Book of Accounts of the People of the Past.

13593
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is a sūrah of the Qur’an consisting of only thirty verses which pleaded on behalf of its companion until it caused him to enter Paradise. It is Sūrat Tabārak.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (3667) and al-Muʿjam al-Ṣaghīr (490).

13594
Abū Hurayrah narrated from the Prophet ﷺ:
“There is a sūrah of the Qur’an consisting of thirty verses which interceded for a man until he was forgiven. It is: ‘Blessed is He in whose Hand is the dominion.’”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1400), al-Tirmidhī (2891), and Ibn Mājah (3786). Ibn Ḥibbān authenticated it (787), as did al-Ḥākim (1/565).

13595
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would not go to sleep until he had recited Sūrat al-Sajdah:
“Alif-Lām-Mīm…”
and Sūrat al-Mulk:
“Blessed is He in whose Hand is the dominion, and He has power over all things.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2892), Aḥmad (14695), al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (1209), and al-Nasāʾī in ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (707–708).

13596
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Sūrat Tabārak is the protector from the punishment of the grave.”
Good: Narrated by Abū al-Shaykh in Ṭabaqāt al-Muḥaddithīn bi-Aṣbahān (526).

13597
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“There are seven whom Allah will shade on the Day of Resurrection in His shade, on the Day when there will be no shade except His shade: a just ruler; a young man who grew up in the worship of Allah; a man who remembered Allah while alone and his eyes overflowed with tears; a man whose heart is attached to the mosque; two men who love one another for the sake of Allah; a man whom a woman of status and beauty invited to herself and he said, ‘Indeed, I fear Allah’; and a man who gave charity so secretly that his left hand did not know what his right hand had given.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6806) and Muslim (1031:91).

13598
Anas narrated:
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, when we are with you we are in one state, but when we leave you we are in another state. We fear that this may be hypocrisy.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked them:
“How are you regarding your Lord?”
They replied:
“Allah is our Lord in private and in public.”
He asked:
“How are you regarding your Prophet?”
They replied:
“You are our Prophet in private and in public.”
He said:
“That is not hypocrisy.”
Good: Narrated by ʿAbd ibn Ḥumayd (1377), whose wording is given here, al-Bazzār (6904), and Abū Yaʿlā (3396).

13599
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“If you were to rely upon Allah with true reliance, He would provide for you just as He provides for the birds: they leave in the morning with empty stomachs and return in the evening with full stomachs.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2344), Ibn Mājah (4164), and Aḥmad (205). Ibn Ḥibbān authenticated it (730), as did al-Ḥākim (4/318). Its isnād is authentic.

13600
Saʿīd ibn Hishām narrated:
I said:
“O Mother of the Believers, tell me about the character of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
She replied:
“Do you not read the Qur’an?”
I said:
“Yes.”
She said:
“The character of the Prophet of Allah ﷺ was the Qur’an.”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (746:139).

13601
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was the finest of all people in character.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6203) and Muslim (2310:55).
There are many other hadiths with this meaning, and they are mentioned in the Book of the Biography of the Prophet ﷺ.

13602
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed by two graves and said:
“Indeed, these two are being punished, and they are not being punished for something they regarded as difficult to avoid. As for one of them, he used to go about spreading malicious gossip, while the other did not protect himself from his urine.”
Then he called for a fresh palm stalk, split it into two pieces, placed one on each grave, and said:
“Perhaps their punishment will be lightened so long as these do not dry out.”
In another narration:
“The other would not keep himself clean from urine,”
or:
“from his urine.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (218, 1361) and Muslim (292). The wording is that of Muslim; al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.

13603
Abū Wāʾil narrated that Ḥudhayfah was informed that a certain man used to carry tales between people.
Ḥudhayfah said:
“I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say: ‘A tale-bearer will not enter Paradise.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (105).

13604
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Harsh, and after all that, zanīm”:
“It refers to a man from Quraysh who had a fleshy appendage like the appendage of a sheep.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4917).

13605
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“After all that, zanīm”:
The following verses were revealed to the Prophet ﷺ:
“Do not obey every habitual swearer, contemptible, a persistent slanderer, going about spreading malicious gossip…”
He said:
“We did not know who was meant until the words were revealed to the Prophet ﷺ:
‘After all that, zanīm.’
Then we recognized him, for he had an appendage like the appendage of a sheep.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (23/166).
The statement:
“Then we recognized him”
refers to a man from Quraysh, as mentioned in the preceding report.
The commentators said that he was al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīrah, because he possessed abundant wealth and many sons, and his father claimed him as his son eighteen years after his birth.
The description mentioned in this sūrah in the words of Allah:
“Because he possesses wealth and sons. When Our verses are recited to him, he says, ‘Legends of the former peoples.’”
is the same person mentioned in Sūrat al-Muddaththir [74:11–15]:
“Leave Me with the one whom I created alone, and to whom I granted abundant wealth, and sons constantly present, and for whom I made life smooth, yet after all that he desires that I should give him more.”
The author states that the person intended in that passage is al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīrah al-Makhzūmī, one of the leaders of Quraysh, and that there is no disagreement concerning this identification.
It is also stated in an authentic hadith that among the people of the Fire are those who are harsh, coarse, and arrogant:

13606
Ḥārithah ibn Wahb al-Khuzāʿī narrated:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“Shall I tell you who the people of Paradise are? Every weak person who is regarded as insignificant; if he were to swear an oath by Allah, Allah would fulfill it for him.
And shall I tell you who the people of the Fire are? Every harsh, coarse, arrogant person.”
In Muslim’s narration:
“Every coarse, zanīm, arrogant person.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4918) and Muslim (2853:47).

13607
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“Our Lord will uncover His Shin, and every believing man and woman will prostrate to Him. Those who used to prostrate in this world merely for show and reputation will remain. Each of them will attempt to prostrate, but his back will become rigid like a single solid plate.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4919) and Muslim (183).

13608
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“If you see Allah giving a servant the worldly things he loves despite his persistent sins, then that is only a gradual drawing toward punishment.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17311), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (9268), and al-Bayhaqī in al-Qaḍāʾ wa-l-Qadar (2/566).

13609
Abū Mūsā narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah certainly grants respite to the wrongdoer, but when He finally seizes him, He does not let him escape.”
He then recited:
“Such is the seizure of your Lord when He seizes the towns while they are wrongdoing. Indeed, His seizure is painful and severe.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4686) and Muslim (2583). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī; Muslim’s wording is similar.

13610
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I was granted victory by the east wind, and ʿĀd were destroyed by the west wind.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (900).
A group of scholars also stated that this wind was intensely cold and that it destroyed everyone whom it struck.

13611
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“No one’s deeds will cause him to enter Paradise.”
They asked:
“Not even you, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“Not even me, unless Allah encompasses me with His bounty and mercy. So remain upright and strive to come close to perfection. None of you should wish for death: if he is a doer of good, perhaps he will increase in goodness; and if he is a wrongdoer, perhaps he will seek Allah’s pleasure through repentance.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5673) and Muslim (2816:75). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī; Muslim’s wording is shorter.

13612
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever believes in Allah and His Messenger, establishes the prayer, and fasts Ramaḍān, it is a right upon Allah to admit him into Paradise, whether he migrated in the cause of Allah or remained in the land in which he was born.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, should we not give the people this good news?”
He replied:
“Indeed, in Paradise there are one hundred levels which Allah has prepared for those who strive in His cause. The distance between every two levels is like the distance between the heaven and the earth. So when you ask Allah, ask Him for al-Firdaws, for it is the middle of Paradise and the highest part of Paradise. Above it is the Throne of the Most Merciful, and from it the rivers of Paradise spring forth.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7423).

13613
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“If a piece of lead this size”—and he gestured to something like a skull—“were sent down from the heaven to the earth, a distance of five hundred years’ travel, it would reach the earth before nightfall. But if it were sent down from the top of that chain, it would continue traveling for forty autumns, night and day, before reaching its bottom or its depth.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2588), Aḥmad (6856), and al-Ḥākim (2/438–439).

13614
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is no owner of gold or silver who fails to pay what is due from it except that, on the Day of Resurrection, plates of fire will be prepared for him and heated in the Fire of Hell. His side, forehead, and back will be branded with them. Whenever they cool, they will be reheated for him, on a Day whose measure is fifty thousand years, until judgment is passed among the servants and he sees his path, either to Paradise or to the Fire.”
It was asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what about camels?”
He said:
“There is no owner of camels who fails to fulfill what is due from them—and among their rights is that they be milked on the day they are brought to water—except that, on the Day of Resurrection, he will be thrown down for them upon a wide, level plain. They will be in their fullest number, with not even a single young camel missing. They will trample him with their hooves and bite him with their mouths. Whenever the first of them passes over him, the last will be returned over him, on a Day whose measure is fifty thousand years, until judgment is passed among the servants and he sees his path, either to Paradise or to the Fire.”
It was asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what about cattle and sheep?”
He replied:
“There is no owner of cattle or sheep who fails to fulfill what is due from them except that, on the Day of Resurrection, he will be thrown down for them upon a wide, level plain. None of them will be missing. None will have twisted horns, none will be hornless, and none will have broken horns. They will gore him with their horns and trample him with their hooves. Whenever the first of them passes over him, the last will be returned over him, on a Day whose measure is fifty thousand years, until judgment is passed among the servants and he sees his path, either to Paradise or to the Fire.”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (987).

13615
Asmāʾ bint Abī Bakr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Spend, and do not count, lest Allah count against you; and do not hoard, lest Allah withhold from you.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2591) and Muslim (1029). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13616
Abū Saʿīd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Nūḥ and his people will be brought, and Allah, the Exalted, will ask: ‘Did you convey the message?’
He will say: ‘Yes, my Lord.’
Then Allah will ask his people: ‘Did he convey the message to you?’
They will say: ‘No, no prophet ever came to us.’
Allah will say to Nūḥ: ‘Who will testify for you?’
He will say: ‘Muḥammad ﷺ and his Ummah.’
So we will testify that he conveyed the message. This is the meaning of the words of Allah, Glorious is His mention: ‘Thus We have made you a justly balanced nation so that you may be witnesses over mankind.’” [Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:143]
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Al-wasaṭ means just.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3339).

13617
Ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who is the truthful and the one whose truthfulness is confirmed, told us:
“The creation of each one of you is gathered in the womb of his mother for forty days. Then he becomes a clinging clot for a similar period, then a lump of flesh for a similar period. Then Allah sends an angel who is commanded concerning four matters, and it is said to him: ‘Write down his deeds, his provision, his lifespan, and whether he will be wretched or blessed.’ Then the soul is breathed into him…”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6594) and Muslim (2643). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13618
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The idols that had existed among the people of Nūḥ later came to be among the Arabs.
As for Wadd, it belonged to Kalb at Dūmat al-Jandal.
As for Suwāʿ, it belonged to Hudhayl.
As for Yaghūth, it belonged to Murād, and afterward to Banū Ghuṭayf in al-Jawf near Sabaʾ.
As for Yaʿūq, it belonged to Hamdān.
And as for Nasr, it belonged to Ḥimyar, to the family of Dhū al-Kalāʿ.
These were the names of righteous men from the people of Nūḥ. When they died, Satan inspired their people:
“Set up monuments in the places where they used to sit, and name them after them.”
So they did so.
They were not worshipped at first. But when that generation died and knowledge was forgotten, those monuments were worshipped.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4920).
Regarding the statement:
“The idols that had existed among the people of Nūḥ later came to be among the Arabs,”
the author explains that those idols were buried by the Flood and remained buried until Satan brought them out for the Arab polytheists, as Ibn ʿAbbās stated.
As for the Arabs, among their idols were:
al-Lāt, which belonged to Thaqīf;
al-ʿUzzā, which belonged to Sulaym, Ghaṭafān, and Jusham;
Manāt, which was associated with Qudayd;
and Isāf, Nāʾilah, and Hubal, which belonged to the people of Makkah.

13619
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out with a group of his Companions heading toward the market of ʿUkāẓ.
At that time, the devils had been prevented from obtaining news from heaven, and shooting stars were sent against them.
The devils returned and were asked:
“What has happened to you?”
They replied:
“We have been prevented from obtaining news from heaven, and shooting stars have been sent against us.”
They said:
“Nothing could have prevented you from obtaining news from heaven except some new event. Travel throughout the east and west of the earth and find out what has happened.”
So they spread throughout the eastern and western regions of the earth, seeking to discover what event had prevented them from obtaining news from heaven.
Those who headed toward Tihāmah came upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at Nakhlah while he was on his way to the market of ʿUkāẓ.
He was leading his Companions in the Fajr prayer.
When the jinn heard the Qur’an, they listened attentively to it and said:
“This is what has come between you and the news of heaven.”
Then they returned to their people and said:
“O our people, indeed we have heard a wondrous Qur’an. It guides to right conduct, so we have believed in it, and we will never associate anyone with our Lord.”
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, then revealed to His Prophet ﷺ:
“Say: It has been revealed to me that a group of the jinn listened…”
What was revealed to him was the statement of the jinn.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4921) and Muslim (449). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
It appears that this sūrah was revealed in the tenth year of the Prophethood.
The apparent meaning of the hadith is that they believed in the Prophet ﷺ after hearing the Qur’an and then returned to their people calling them to Islam.
At the beginning of Muslim’s narration, ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās says:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not recite directly to the jinn, nor did he see them.”
He was referring to this particular incident in which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was performing the Fajr prayer.
As for the hadith of Ibn Masʿūd in which the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A caller from the jinn came to me, so I went with him and recited the Qur’an to them,”
that was a separate incident, as stated in the authentic hadith:

13620
ʿĀmir narrated:
I asked ʿAlqamah:
“Was Ibn Masʿūd present with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Night of the Jinn?”
ʿAlqamah replied:
I myself asked Ibn Masʿūd:
“Was any one of you present with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Night of the Jinn?”
He replied:
“No. But one night we were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and suddenly could not find him. We searched for him in the valleys and mountain passes and said, ‘Perhaps he has been carried away or assassinated.’ We spent the worst night that any people could spend.”
When morning came, he appeared to us from the direction of Ḥirāʾ.
We said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we lost you and searched for you but could not find you, and we spent the worst night any people could spend.”
He said:
“A caller from the jinn came to me, so I went with him and recited the Qur’an to them.”
Then he took us and showed us their traces and the traces of their fires.
They asked him for provision, and he said:
“For you is every bone over which the name of Allah has been mentioned that comes into your hands; it will be covered with as much meat as possible. And every piece of dung is fodder for your animals.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“Do not clean yourselves after relieving yourselves with either of these, for they are the food of your brothers.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (450).
Ibn Masʿūd also said:
“I was not with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on the Night of the Jinn, though I wished that I had been with him.”
Muslim also narrated this through another route from Ibrāhīm, from ʿAlqamah, from Ibn Masʿūd.
Abū Hurayrah also refers to this incident, as stated in the authentic hadith:

13621
Abū Hurayrah narrated that he used to carry a small water vessel for the Prophet ﷺ for his ablution and personal needs.
While he was following him carrying it, the Prophet ﷺ asked:
“Who is this?”
He replied:
“It is Abū Hurayrah.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Bring me some stones with which I can clean myself, but do not bring me a bone or dung.”
So I brought him some stones, carrying them in the edge of my garment, until I placed them beside him. Then I withdrew.
When he had finished, I walked with him and asked:
“What is the matter with bones and dung?”
He replied:
“They are among the food of the jinn. A delegation of the jinn of Naṣībīn came to me—and excellent jinn they were—and asked me for provision. So I supplicated to Allah for them that whenever they pass by a bone or a piece of dung, they will find food upon it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3860).
This incident occurred in Makkah, whereas Abū Hurayrah accepted Islam after the Hijrah, in the seventh year.
Therefore, the statement of the Prophet ﷺ:
“A delegation of the jinn of Naṣībīn came to me”
is a narration of an earlier event.
The author considers it unlikely that the incident was repeated, although some scholars held that view.
The word Naṣībīn refers to a well-known town in al-Jazīrah.
It has also been said to be in al-Shām, near the borders of Turkey. The author explains that this usage is broad, because al-Jazīrah can refer to the extensive lands lying between al-Shām and Iraq.

13622
Maʿn ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān narrated:
I heard my father say:
I asked Masrūq:
“Who informed the Prophet ﷺ of the presence of the jinn on the night when they listened to the Qur’an?”
He replied:
“Your father—meaning ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd—told me that a tree informed him of their presence.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3859) and Muslim (450:153).
The phrase:
“Who informed the Prophet ﷺ?”
means: who made him aware that the jinn were present?
The statement:
“A tree informed him of their presence”
means that the tree beside him informed him.
The author counts this among the miracles of the Prophet ﷺ, just as it is authentically reported that he said:
“Indeed, I know a stone in Makkah that used to greet me with salām.”
Allah gave speech to inanimate things.
Allah says:
“There is not a single thing except that it glorifies Him with praise, but you do not understand their glorification.” [Sūrat al-Isrāʾ 17:44]
Miracles of this kind are numerous, as discussed in the Book of the Biography of the Prophet ﷺ.
The discussion concerning the jinn and their listening to the Qur’an is given in detail in the Book of the Beginning of Creation.

13623
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah said: ‘The son of Ādam has denied Me, though he had no right to do so, and he has insulted Me, though he had no right to do so. As for his denial of Me, it is his claim that I am unable to restore him as he was. As for his insult of Me, it is his statement that I have a child. Glory be to Me above taking a wife or a child.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4482).

13624
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
One day I was riding behind the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when he said:
“Young man, I will teach you some words: preserve the limits of Allah and He will preserve you. Preserve the limits of Allah and you will find Him before you. When you ask, ask Allah; and when you seek help, seek help from Allah.
Know that if the entire nation were to gather together to benefit you with something, they could benefit you only with something Allah had already written for you. And if they gathered together to harm you with something, they could harm you only with something Allah had already written against you. The pens have been lifted and the pages have dried.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2516).

13625
Abū Hurayrah narrated that when the verse:
“And warn your closest relatives.” [Sūrat al-Shuʿarāʾ 26:214]
was revealed to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he said:
“O people of Quraysh! Save yourselves from Allah, for I cannot avail you anything against Allah.
O Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib! I cannot avail you anything against Allah.
O ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib! I cannot avail you anything against Allah.
O Ṣafiyyah, aunt of the Messenger of Allah! I cannot avail you anything against Allah.
O Fāṭimah, daughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ! Ask me for whatever you wish of my wealth, but I cannot avail you anything against Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2753) and Muslim (206).

13626
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
One day the Prophet ﷺ was sitting openly among the people when a man came to him and asked:
“What is faith?”
He replied:
“Faith is that you believe in Allah, His angels, His Book, meeting Him, His messengers, and that you believe in the resurrection.”
The man asked:
“What is Islam?”
He replied:
“Islam is that you worship Allah and associate nothing with Him, establish the prayer, pay the obligatory zakāh, and fast Ramaḍān.”
The man asked:
“What is iḥsān?”
He replied:
“It is that you worship Allah as though you see Him; and if you do not see Him, then indeed He sees you.”
The man asked:
“When is the Hour?”
He replied:
“The one being asked knows no more about it than the one asking. But I will tell you some of its signs: when the slave woman gives birth to her master, and when the shepherds of black camels compete with one another in constructing tall buildings. These are among its signs. It is one of five matters that no one knows except Allah.”
Then the Prophet ﷺ recited:
“Indeed, Allah alone has knowledge of the Hour…” [Sūrat Luqmān 31:34]
Then the man turned and left.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Bring him back.”
But they could no longer see him.
The Prophet ﷺ then said:
“That was Jibrīl. He came to teach the people their religion.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (50) and Muslim (9).

13627
Saʿd ibn Hishām ibn ʿĀmir intended to go out in jihād in the cause of Allah. He came to Madinah intending to sell some property he owned there, spend the proceeds on weapons and mounts, and then fight the Romans until he died.
When he arrived in Madinah, he met some of its people, who forbade him from doing that. They informed him that six men had intended to do something similar during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ forbade them and said:
“Do you not have an example in me?”
When they told him this, he took back his wife, whom he had previously divorced, and called witnesses to the resumption of the marriage.
He then went to Ibn ʿAbbās and asked him about the witr prayer of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“Shall I direct you to the person on earth who knows most about the witr prayer of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
He asked:
“Who?”
Ibn ʿAbbās replied:
“ʿĀʾishah. Go to her and ask her, then return to me and tell me what she says.”
Saʿd said:
I set out toward her and came across Ḥakīm ibn Aflaḥ, so I asked him to accompany me.
He said:
“I am not going near her, because I had forbidden her from saying anything concerning these two factions, yet she insisted on continuing to speak concerning them.”
I swore that he should accompany me, so he came.
We went together to ʿĀʾishah and asked permission to enter. She gave us permission, and we entered.
She asked:
“Ḥakīm?”
She recognized him.
He replied:
“Yes.”
She asked:
“Who is with you?”
He replied:
“Saʿd ibn Hishām.”
She asked:
“Who is Hishām?”
He replied:
“Ibn ʿĀmir.”
She invoked Allah’s mercy upon him and spoke well of him. Qatādah said that Hishām had been killed on the Day of Uḥud.
I said:
“O Mother of the Believers, tell me about the character of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
She replied:
“Do you not read the Qur’an?”
I said:
“Yes.”
She said:
“The character of the Prophet of Allah ﷺ was the Qur’an.”
I almost stood up and decided that I would never ask anyone about anything again until I died. But then another thought occurred to me, so I asked:
“Tell me about the night prayer of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
She said:
“Do you not recite: ‘O you wrapped in garments’?”
I replied:
“Yes.”
She said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, made night prayer obligatory at the beginning of this sūrah. The Prophet of Allah ﷺ and his Companions therefore stood in prayer at night for an entire year. Allah withheld the ending of the sūrah in heaven for twelve months, until Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed the concession at the end of this sūrah. Thereafter, night prayer became voluntary after having been obligatory.”
I asked:
“O Mother of the Believers, tell me about the witr prayer of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
She replied:
“We used to prepare his siwāk and water for purification. Allah would awaken him whenever He willed during the night. He would use the siwāk, perform ablution, and pray nine rakʿahs. He would not sit during them except after the eighth rakʿah. There he would remember Allah, praise Him, and supplicate to Him. Then he would rise without giving salām and pray the ninth rakʿah. Then he would sit, remember Allah, praise Him, and supplicate to Him, after which he would give salām loudly enough for us to hear.
Then he would pray two rakʿahs while seated after giving salām. That made eleven rakʿahs, my son.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ grew old and put on weight, he performed witr with seven rakʿahs and in the following two rakʿahs did as he had done previously. That made nine, my son.
Whenever the Prophet of Allah ﷺ performed a prayer, he loved to remain constant in it. If sleep or illness prevented him from standing for night prayer, he would pray twelve rakʿahs during the daytime.
I do not know that the Prophet of Allah ﷺ ever recited the entire Qur’an in a single night, nor that he ever prayed throughout an entire night until morning, nor that he ever fasted an entire month other than the month of Ramaḍān.”
Saʿd said:
I then went to Ibn ʿAbbās and told him what she had narrated.
He said:
“She has spoken the truth. Had I been closer to her, or able to enter upon her, I would have gone to her so that I could hear it directly from her mouth.”
I replied:
“Had I known that you did not enter upon her, I would not have told you her narration.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (746).

13628
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“When the beginning of Sūrat al-Muzzammil was revealed, they used to stand in night prayer for approximately as long as they would stand during Ramaḍān, until the ending of the sūrah was revealed. There was one year between the revelation of its beginning and its ending.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1305), al-Ḥākim (2/505), and al-Bayhaqī (2/500).
Regarding the words:
“And recite the Qur’an with measured recitation,”
this means a clear, deliberate recitation without rushing.
This is stated in the authentic hadith:

13629
Qatādah narrated:
Anas was asked:
“How was the recitation of the Prophet ﷺ?”
He replied:
“It was prolonged and measured.”
Then he recited:
“In the name of Allah, the Most Merciful, the Especially Merciful.”
He prolonged “In the name of Allah,” prolonged “the Most Merciful,” and prolonged “the Especially Merciful.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5046).

13630
Abū Wāʾil narrated:
A man came to Ibn Masʿūd and said:
“Last night I recited the entire Mufaṣṣal section in one rakʿah.”
Ibn Masʿūd replied:
“Rushing through it like the rapid recitation of poetry? I know the paired sūrahs that the Prophet ﷺ used to recite together.”
He then mentioned twenty sūrahs from the Mufaṣṣal, two sūrahs in each rakʿah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (775) and Muslim (822). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13631
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“It will be said to the companion of the Qur’an: ‘Recite and ascend, and recite with measured recitation just as you used to recite in the world, for your rank will be at the last verse you recite.’”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1464), al-Tirmidhī (3141), and Aḥmad (6799). Ibn Ḥibbān authenticated it (766), as did al-Ḥākim (1/552).

13632
ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, narrated that al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“O Messenger of Allah, how does revelation come to you?”
He replied:
“Sometimes it comes to me like the ringing of a bell, and this is the most difficult form for me. Then it leaves me after I have fully grasped what was said. At other times, the angel appears to me in the form of a man and speaks to me, and I comprehend what he says.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
“I indeed saw revelation descend upon him on an extremely cold day, and when it ceased from him, sweat would be pouring from his forehead.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in the Book of the Qur’an (7), al-Bukhārī (2), and Muslim (2333).
In Muslim’s wording, ʿĀʾishah said:
“Revelation would descend upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on a cold morning, and afterward sweat would pour from his forehead.”

13633
ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, said in the long hadith concerning the Incident of the Slander:
“Allah knew that I was innocent and that He would declare my innocence. But by Allah, I never imagined that Allah would reveal concerning me revelation that would be recited. In my own estimation, my matter was too insignificant for Allah to speak concerning me through revelation. Rather, I hoped that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ might see a dream in which Allah would declare my innocence.
By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had not yet left his place, nor had anyone from the household gone out, when revelation descended upon him. He was seized by the intense strain that used to seize him during revelation, to the extent that drops of sweat like pearls poured from him, even though it was a cold winter day, because of the heaviness of the word being revealed to him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4141) and Muslim (2770).
There are many hadiths with this meaning, and they are mentioned in the Book of Revelation.
The soundest view among the scholars is that these verses of Sūrat al-Muzzammil were revealed after the period during which revelation had temporarily ceased.

13634
Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ narrated:
When the matter of ʿUthmān ibn Maẓʿūn arose because he had abandoned women, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent for him and said:
“O ʿUthmān, I have not been commanded to practice monasticism. Have you turned away from my Sunnah?”
He replied:
“No, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Part of my Sunnah is that I pray and I sleep, I fast and I eat, I marry and I divorce. Whoever turns away from my Sunnah is not of me. O ʿUthmān, your family has a right over you, and your own self has a right over you.”
Saʿd said:
“By Allah, a number of Muslim men had already resolved that if the Messenger of Allah ﷺ approved ʿUthmān in what he was doing, we would castrate ourselves and devote ourselves entirely to celibacy.”
Good: Narrated by al-Dārimī (2215).

13635
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
Three men came to the houses of the wives of the Prophet ﷺ asking about the worship of the Prophet ﷺ.
When they were informed, they seemed to regard it as little and said:
“Where are we compared with the Prophet ﷺ? Allah has forgiven him his past and future sins.”
One of them said:
“As for me, I will pray throughout every night forever.”
Another said:
“I will fast every day and never break my fast.”
The third said:
“I will keep away from women and never marry.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came to them and said:
“Are you the ones who said such-and-such? By Allah, I am the one among you who fears Allah most and is most mindful of Him, yet I fast and I break my fast, I pray and I sleep, and I marry women. Whoever turns away from my Sunnah is not of me.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5063), whose wording is given here, and Muslim (1401).

13636
Samurah narrated:
“The Prophet ﷺ forbade celibate renunciation of marriage.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1082), Ibn Mājah (1849), al-Nasāʾī (3214), and Aḥmad (20192). Its isnād is authentic.

13637
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Which of you loves the wealth of his heir more than his own wealth?”
They replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, there is none among us except that his own wealth is more beloved to him.”
He said:
“His own wealth is what he sends ahead, while the wealth of his heir is what he leaves behind.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6442).

13638
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated that he heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ speaking about the period when revelation had temporarily ceased:
“While I was walking, I heard a voice from the sky. I raised my gaze toward the sky and saw the angel who had come to me at Ḥirāʾ sitting upon a chair between heaven and earth. I was so terrified of him that I fell down to the ground.
I went to my family and said, ‘Wrap me up! Wrap me up!’ So they wrapped me up. Then Allah, the Exalted, revealed: ‘O you who are covered up, arise and warn…’ until the words, ‘and shun all idolatry.’”
Abū Salamah said:
“Al-rujz means idols.”
Thereafter revelation became intense and continued in succession.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4926) and Muslim (161:256).

13639
Yaḥyā ibn Abī Kathīr narrated:
I asked Abū Salamah ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān:
“What was the first part of the Qur’an to be revealed?”
He replied:
“‘O you who are covered up.’”
I said:
“But people say it was: ‘Read in the Name of your Lord who created.’” [Sūrat al-ʿAlaq 96:1]
Abū Salamah said:
I asked Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh the same question and said to him what you have just said to me.
Jābir replied:
“I will tell you only what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ told us.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I stayed in seclusion at Ḥirāʾ. When I completed my period of seclusion, I descended. Then I was called. I looked to my right but saw nothing. I looked to my left but saw nothing. I looked in front of me but saw nothing. I looked behind me but saw nothing. Then I raised my head and saw something.
I went to Khadījah and said, ‘Cover me! Pour cold water over me!’ So they covered me and poured cold water over me. Then the verses were revealed: ‘O you who are covered up, arise and warn, and magnify your Lord.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4922) and Muslim (161:257).
The expression:
“The first part of the Qur’an to be revealed”
means the first revelation after the period in which revelation had ceased.
This is explicitly stated in the two Ṣaḥīḥ collections.
Another narration in both collections says:
“There was the angel who had come to me at Ḥirāʾ, sitting upon a chair between heaven and earth. I was overcome with fear of him until I fell to the ground. Then I came to my family and said, ‘Wrap me up! Wrap me up!’”
This establishes that Jibrīl had already previously come down with revelation, namely:
“Read in the Name of your Lord who created.”

13640
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“How can I enjoy ease when the bearer of the Trumpet has already placed it to his mouth, lowered his forehead, and is waiting for the moment when he will be commanded to blow?”
We said:
“O Messenger of Allah, what should we say then?”
He replied:
“Say: ‘Allah is sufficient for us and He is the best Disposer of affairs. Upon Allah we rely.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (1084), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (823).

13641
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Since the bearer of the Trumpet was entrusted with it, his gaze has remained ready, fixed toward the Throne, fearing that he may be commanded before his glance can return to him. His eyes are like two brilliant stars.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (4/558–559).

13642
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīrah came to the Prophet ﷺ, and the Prophet ﷺ recited the Qur’an to him.
It was as though al-Walīd softened toward it.
News of this reached Abū Jahl, so he came to him and said:
“Uncle, your people are thinking of collecting wealth for you.”
Al-Walīd asked:
“Why?”
He replied:
“So they may give it to you, because you went to Muḥammad seeking what he has.”
Al-Walīd said:
“Quraysh already knows that I am among the wealthiest of them.”
Abū Jahl said:
“Then say something about him that will reach your people and make clear that you reject him or dislike him.”
Al-Walīd said:
“What can I say? By Allah, there is no man among you who knows poetry better than I do, nor rajaz, nor qaṣīdah, nor the poetry of the jinn. By Allah, what he says resembles none of these things.
By Allah, there is sweetness in what he says, and there is beauty upon it. Its upper part is fruitful and its lower part abundant. It rises above everything and nothing rises above it, and it crushes whatever lies beneath it.”
Abū Jahl said:
“Your people will not be satisfied with you until you say something against it.”
Al-Walīd replied:
“Leave me until I think.”
After thinking, he said:
“This is nothing but transmitted magic which he relates from someone else.”
Then Allah revealed:
“Leave Me with the one whom I created alone.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (2/506–507), and from him by al-Bayhaqī in Shuʿab al-Īmān (133).

13643
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Hell will be brought forth on that Day with seventy thousand reins, and with every rein there will be seventy thousand angels dragging it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2842).

13644
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“Do not move your tongue with it in haste”:
When Jibrīl would descend upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ with revelation, the Prophet ﷺ would move his tongue and lips along with it, and this would become difficult for him, something that could be visibly recognized.
So Allah revealed the verses in the sūrah beginning, “I swear by the Day of Resurrection”:
“Do not move your tongue with it in haste. Indeed, upon Us is its collection and its recitation.”
Ibn ʿAbbās explained:
“It is upon Us to gather it in your chest.”
Regarding:
“and its recitation. So when We recite it, follow its recitation,”
he said:
“When We reveal it, listen to it.”
And regarding:
“Then indeed, upon Us is its explanation,”
he said:
“It is upon Us to make you explain it with your tongue.”
Thereafter, when Jibrīl came to him, the Prophet ﷺ would remain silent and attentive; once Jibrīl departed, he would recite it exactly as Allah had promised him.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4929) and Muslim (448). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13645
Saʿīd ibn Jubayr narrated from Ibn ʿAbbās concerning the words of Allah:
“Do not move your tongue with it”:
The Prophet ﷺ used to experience great difficulty when revelation descended, and he would move his lips.
Ibn ʿAbbās said to me:
“I will move them for you just as the Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to move them.”
Saʿīd then said:
“And I will move them just as Ibn ʿAbbās used to move them.”
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“Do not move your tongue with it in haste. Indeed, upon Us is its collection and its recitation.”
Ibn ʿAbbās explained:
“Its collection means gathering it in your chest so that afterward you may recite it.”
Regarding:
“So when We recite it, follow its recitation,”
he said:
“Listen to it and remain silent.”
Then Allah would ensure that he could recite it.
Thereafter, whenever Jibrīl, peace be upon him came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he would listen attentively. When Jibrīl departed, the Prophet ﷺ would recite it exactly as Jibrīl had recited it to him.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7524) and Muslim (448:148).

13646
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
When the Qur’an was revealed to the Prophet ﷺ, he would hasten to repeat it in order to memorize it.
So Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“Do not move your tongue with it…”
through the words:
“and its recitation.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11572) and Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (23/496–497). Ibn Jarīr additionally narrates: “Ibn ʿAbbās said: ‘Like this,’ and moved his lips.”
This verse indicates that the Prophet ﷺ did not need to repeatedly rehearse the Qur’anic verses in order to memorize them in the ordinary way.
Rather, Allah, the Exalted, Himself undertook to preserve the Qur’an in his memory.
The author states that this was one of the great miracles granted to the Prophet ﷺ.
Regarding the words:
“Indeed, upon Us is its collection and its recitation,”
the author explains that Allah, the Exalted, first gathered the Qur’an in the chest of the Prophet ﷺ.
Then the Companions collected it upon written sheets and pieces of leather under the direction of the Prophet ﷺ.
Thus, according to the author, the words of Allah were fulfilled:
“Indeed, upon Us is its collection and its recitation.”
See the detailed discussion of this at the beginning of the Tafsīr.
Regarding the words:
“Then indeed, upon Us is its explanation,”
Allah Himself undertook to explain the meanings of the Qur’an and inspired that explanation to the Prophet ﷺ.
Thus the Sunnah is one of the two forms of revelation, although it is revelation that is not recited as Qur’an.

13647
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the people asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, will we see our Lord on the Day of Resurrection?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Do you have any difficulty seeing the full moon on a night when it is full?”
They replied:
“No, O Messenger of Allah.”
He asked:
“Do you have any difficulty seeing the sun when there are no clouds beneath it?”
They replied:
“No, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“You will see Him in the same way.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (806, 6573) and Muslim (182).

13648
Busr ibn Jaḥḥāsh al-Qurashī narrated that one day the Prophet ﷺ spat into his palm, placed his finger upon it, and said:
“Allah says: ‘Son of Ādam! How can you think you will escape Me when I created you from something like this? Then when I formed you and proportioned you, you walked about between two garments, making the earth resound beneath you. You gathered wealth and withheld it, until when the soul reached the collarbones you said, “Now I will give charity.” But how can this be the time for charity?’”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (2707) and Aḥmad (17842), whose wording is given here. Al-Ḥākim authenticated it (2/502).

13649
Saʿīd ibn Jubayr said:
I asked Ibn ʿAbbās concerning the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“Woe to you, and woe!”
I asked:
“Was this something the Messenger of Allah ﷺ himself said, or was it something Allah revealed?”
He replied:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said it, and then Allah revealed it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11574), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (11/458), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/510). Its isnād is authentic.

13650
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to recite in the Fajr prayer on Friday:
Sūrat al-Sajdah, beginning: “Alif-Lām-Mīm. The revelation…”
and:
Sūrat al-Insān, beginning: “Has there come upon man…”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (891) and Muslim (880). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13651
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ used to recite in the Fajr prayer on Friday:
Sūrat al-Sajdah, beginning: “Alif-Lām-Mīm. The revelation…”
and:
“Has there come upon man a period of time…”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Friday Prayer (879).

13652
Umm Sulaym asked the Prophet of Allah ﷺ about a woman who sees in her sleep what a man sees.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“If a woman sees that, she must perform ghusl.”
Umm Sulaym said—and she felt shy about asking:
“Does this really happen?”
The Prophet of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Yes. Otherwise, from where would resemblance come? The fluid of the man is thick and white, while the fluid of the woman is thin and yellow. Whichever of the two predominates or precedes, the resemblance comes from it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (311).

13653
ʿĀʾishah narrated that a woman asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“Must a woman perform ghusl if she has a sexual dream and sees the fluid?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
ʿĀʾishah said to her:
“May your hands be covered in dust!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“Leave her. From where else would resemblance come? If her fluid predominates over the man’s fluid, the child resembles his maternal relatives; and if the man’s fluid predominates over hers, the child resembles his paternal relatives.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (314:33).
The statement:
“The child resembles his maternal relatives”
does not mean resemblance of the face alone.
Rather, resemblance is general: it may appear in the face or in other parts of the body such as the feet, hands, nose, or eyes, and it may also appear in complexion, height, shortness, and other characteristics.

13654
Thawbān, the freedman of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, narrated:
I was standing beside the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when a Jewish rabbi came and said:
“Peace be upon you, O Muḥammad.”
I pushed him with such force that he nearly fell.
He asked:
“Why did you push me?”
I said:
“Why did you not say, ‘O Messenger of Allah’?”
The Jewish man replied:
“We simply call him by the name his family gave him.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“My name is Muḥammad, the name my family gave me.”
The Jewish man said:
“I have come to ask you questions.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Will it benefit you if I answer you?”
He replied:
“I will listen with my own ears.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ began drawing upon the ground with a stick he had and said:
“Ask.”
The Jewish man asked:
“Where will the people be on the Day when the earth is changed into another earth and the heavens as well?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“They will be in darkness before the Bridge.”
He asked:
“Who will be the first people to cross?”
He replied:
“The poor among the Muhājirūn.”
The Jewish man asked:
“What will be their welcoming delicacy when they enter Paradise?”
He replied:
“The extra lobe of the liver of the fish.”
He asked:
“What food will they have after that?”
He replied:
“The bull of Paradise, which used to graze along its edges, will be slaughtered for them.”
He asked:
“And what will they drink with it?”
He replied:
“From a spring therein called Salsabīl.”
The Jewish man said:
“You have spoken the truth.”
He continued:
“I have also come to ask you about something that no one on earth knows except a prophet, or perhaps one or two other men.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“Will it benefit you if I tell you?”
He replied:
“I will listen with my own ears.”
He said:
“I have come to ask you about the child.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“The fluid of the man is white and the fluid of the woman is yellow. When they come together, if the man’s fluid prevails over the woman’s fluid, the child is male by Allah’s permission; and if the woman’s fluid prevails over the man’s fluid, the child is female by Allah’s permission.”
The Jewish man said:
“You have spoken the truth, and you are indeed a prophet.”
Then he turned and departed.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“This man asked me about things of which I had no knowledge whatsoever until Allah brought me the knowledge of them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (315).

13655
Abū Mālik al-Ashʿarī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Purification is half of faith. ‘Al-ḥamdu lillāh’ fills the Scale. ‘Subḥān Allāh’ and ‘al-ḥamdu lillāh’ fill what is between the heavens and the earth. Prayer is light, charity is proof, patience is illumination, and the Qur’an is evidence either for you or against you. Every person goes out in the morning and sells his own soul, either freeing it or destroying it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (223).

13656
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said to Kaʿb ibn ʿUjrah:
“O Kaʿb ibn ʿUjrah, fasting is a shield, charity extinguishes sin, and prayer is an offering of nearness—or he said: a proof. Flesh that grows from unlawful earnings will not enter Paradise; the Fire has more right to it. O Kaʿb ibn ʿUjrah, people set out in two ways: one purchases his soul and frees it, while another sells his soul and destroys it.”
