Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (14441), ʿAbd al-Razzāq (20719), and al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1609). Ibn Ḥibbān authenticated it (4514).

13657
ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever vows to obey Allah should obey Him, and whoever vows to disobey Allah must not disobey Him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik in the Book of Vows and Oaths (8) and al-Bukhārī (6700).

13658
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
A man came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I have been overcome by severe hunger and hardship.”
The Prophet ﷺ sent word to his wives, but found that they had nothing.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“Is there a man who will host him tonight? May Allah have mercy upon him.”
A man from the Anṣār stood and said:
“I will, O Messenger of Allah.”
He went home and said to his wife:
“This is the guest of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Do not hold anything back from him.”
She replied:
“By Allah, I have nothing except the food of the children.”
He said:
“When the children ask for dinner, put them to sleep. Then come and extinguish the lamp, and we will go hungry tonight.”
So she did that.
The next morning, the man went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, who said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, was amazed—or laughed—at what so-and-so and his wife did.”
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“They give others preference over themselves even though they themselves are in need.” [Sūrat al-Ḥashr 59:9]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4889) and Muslim (2054). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī; Muslim’s wording is similar.
In one narration of Muslim:
“A man from the Anṣār stood up whose name was Abū Ṭalḥah.”

13659
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, which charity brings the greatest reward?”
He replied:
“That you give charity while you are healthy and reluctant to part with your wealth, fearing poverty and hoping for wealth. Do not delay until the soul reaches the throat and then say, ‘So-and-so is to have this much, and so-and-so this much,’ when it has already become the property of so-and-so.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1419) and Muslim (1032:93). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13660
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, whenever I see you, my soul becomes joyful and my eyes are comforted. Tell me about everything.”
He replied:
“Everything was created from water.”
I said:
“Tell me about an action which, if I hold fast to it, will cause me to enter Paradise.”
He replied:
“Spread salām, feed people, maintain family ties, and stand in prayer at night while the people are asleep; then enter Paradise in peace.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (7932), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (508) and al-Ḥākim (4/160).

13661
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
While we were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in a cave, Sūrat al-Mursalāt was revealed to him.
We received it directly from his mouth while his mouth was still fresh with its recitation.
Then a snake came out.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Kill it.”
We rushed toward it, but it escaped us.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“It has been protected from your harm just as you have been protected from its harm.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4931) and Muslim (2234). Al-Bukhārī added in another narration (4934):
“In a cave at Minā.”

13662
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Umm al-Faḍl bint al-Ḥārith heard him reciting:
“By those sent forth in succession…”
She said:
“My son, by reciting this sūrah you have reminded me that it was the last sūrah I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recite in the Maghrib prayer.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in the Book of Prayer (24), al-Bukhārī (763), and Muslim (462).
Al-Bukhārī (4429) and Muslim also narrated:
“He did not lead us in prayer after that until Allah took him.”

13663
Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words:
“It throws sparks”:
“We used to take a piece of wood three cubits long or more and raise it up for use during winter, and we called that al-qaṣr.”
And concerning:
“As though they were yellow camels,”
he said:
“They are like the ropes of ships gathered together until they become as thick as the waists of men.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4933).

13664
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Between the two blows of the Trumpet there will be forty.”
They asked:
“O Abū Hurayrah, forty days?”
He replied:
“I refuse to say.”
They asked:
“Forty months?”
He replied:
“I refuse to say.”
They asked:
“Forty years?”
He replied:
“I refuse to say.”
The Prophet ﷺ continued:
“Then Allah will send down water from the sky, and they will grow just as vegetation grows.”
The Prophet ﷺ also said:
“Every part of the human being will decay except one bone, the tailbone, and from it the creation will be reconstructed on the Day of Resurrection.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4935) and Muslim (2955).

13665
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah will judge between His creatures—the jinn, mankind, and the animals. On that Day, justice will even be exacted for the hornless animal from the horned one. Then, when no creature has any remaining claim against another, Allah will say: ‘Become dust.’ At that moment the disbeliever will say: ‘If only I were dust.’”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī in his Tafsīr (24/55).

13666
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The first convulsion has come, followed by the second. Death has come with all that it contains.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (21241).
Ibn Jarīr also narrated it in his Tafsīr (24/67) from Kurayb, who said that Wakīʿ narrated it with his chain. In that version, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recited:
“On the Day when the first blast will convulse, followed by the second,”
then said:
“The first convulsion has come, followed by the second. Death has come with all that it contains.”
Regarding the words:
“They say, ‘Will we really be restored to our former state?’”
the meaning is:
“Will we really live again after our death and be resurrected from where we are?”
This was the objection of all the polytheists and stubborn deniers. They regarded resurrection as impossible, but they forgot the power of Allah, the Exalted, who created them from nothing, while the second creation is easier than the first.
Al-ḥāfirah means the original state or beginning of a matter.
The Arabs say:
“So-and-so returned upon his ḥāfirah,”
meaning that he returned to the place or state from which he came.
Regarding the words:
“Even after we have become decayed bones?”
nakhirah means decayed and worn away.
Regarding:
“They say, ‘Then that would indeed be a losing return,’”
meaning the deniers say that it would be a disastrous return.
In other words:
“If we really are returned after death, then we will certainly lose because of the punishment that will strike us afterward.”
Regarding the words:
“But it will only be a single blast, and suddenly they will be upon the surface of the earth,”
they will suddenly be upon the face of the earth after having been inside it.
This will occur after the final blowing of the Trumpet.
The expression:
“a single blast”
means a single cry, namely the blowing of the Trumpet.

13667
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ continued to be asked about the Hour until Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“What concern do you have with mentioning its time? Its ultimate knowledge belongs to your Lord.”
Meaning:
Its knowledge is with Allah.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2279), Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (24/99), and al-Ḥākim (2/513).

13668
Ṭāriq ibn Shihāb narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ continued to speak about the matter of the Hour until the verse was revealed:
“They ask you about the Hour: when will it arrive?”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Tafsīr (11581), Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (24/100), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (8/387).
The Prophet ﷺ nevertheless informed us in many hadiths that the Hour is near. Among them are:

13669
Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated:
I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gesture with his fingers like this—with the middle finger and the one next to the thumb—and say:
“I have been sent, and the Hour is like these two.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4936) and Muslim (2950).

13670
Anas narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I have been sent, and the Hour is like these two.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6504) and Muslim (2951:134).

13671
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I have been sent, and the Hour is like these two.”
He then joined together his index finger and middle finger.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2951:135).

13672
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I have been sent, and the Hour is like these two.”
Meaning two fingers.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6505).
Regarding the words:
“On the Day they see it, it will seem to them as though they had remained only an evening or its morning,”
this refers to the disbelievers who denied resurrection and the raising of the dead.
When the Resurrection occurs and they emerge from their graves, the overwhelming terror of that Day will make the period they spent in their graves seem extremely short—as though it had been only an evening, which is the latter part of the day, or the morning of that same day.

13673
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The verses:
“He frowned and turned away”
were revealed concerning the blind man Ibn Umm Maktūm.
He came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and kept saying:
“O Messenger of Allah, guide me.”
At that time, a prominent man from among the polytheists was with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ kept turning away from Ibn Umm Maktūm and directing his attention toward the other man, saying:
“Do you see anything wrong with what I am saying?”
The man would reply:
“No.”
It was concerning this that the verses were revealed.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3331), whose wording is given here, Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (24/102), and al-Ḥākim (2/514).

13674
Anas ibn Mālik said concerning the words:
“He frowned and turned away”:
Ibn Umm Maktūm came to the Prophet ﷺ while he was speaking to Ubayy ibn Khalaf, so he turned away from him.
Then Allah revealed:
“He frowned and turned away.”
He said:
“After that, the Prophet ﷺ used to honor him.”
Qatādah said:
Anas ibn Mālik also informed me:
“I saw him on the Day of al-Qādisiyyah wearing armor and carrying a black banner,”
meaning Ibn Umm Maktūm.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (3123).

13675
ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The one who recites the Qur’an while having mastered it will be with the noble, righteous scribes. And the one who recites it while continually struggling with it and finding it difficult will have two rewards.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4937) and Muslim (798). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13676
ʿĀʾishah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“You will be gathered barefoot, naked, and uncircumcised.”
ʿĀʾishah said:
“O Messenger of Allah, will the men and women be looking at one another?”
He replied:
“The matter will be far too severe for them to be concerned with that.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6527) and Muslim (2859). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13677
Sawdah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The people will be resurrected barefoot, naked, and uncircumcised. Sweat will overwhelm them and rise as high as the lobes of their ears.”
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, how shameful! Will we be looking at one another?”
He replied:
“The people will be too preoccupied for that: ‘For every person among them on that Day will have enough concern of his own.’”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Āḥād wa al-Mathānī (3066), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (24/34), al-Ḥākim (2/514–515), and al-Baghawī in his Tafsīr (4/557).

13678
Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever would like to look upon the Day of Resurrection as though seeing it with his own eyes, let him recite: ‘When the sun is folded up,’ ‘When the sky is split open,’ and ‘When the sky is torn apart.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3333), Aḥmad (4806), and al-Ḥākim (4/576).

13679
ʿAmr ibn Ḥurayth narrated that he heard the Prophet ﷺ reciting in the Fajr prayer:
“And by the night as it departs.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (456).

13680
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The sun and the moon will be folded up on the Day of Resurrection.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of the Beginning of Creation (3200), from Musaddad, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Mukhtār, from ʿAbdullāh al-Dānāj, from Abū Salamah ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from Abū Hurayrah.

13681
Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr said:
I heard ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, when asked about the words:
“And when the girl buried alive is asked,”
say:
Qays ibn ʿĀṣim came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“I buried eight of my daughters alive during the Jāhiliyyah.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Free a slave on behalf of each one of them.”
He said:
“I own camels.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“If you wish, sacrifice a camel on behalf of each one of them.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in his Musnad (238) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (18/337), whose wording is given here.
Regarding the words of the Prophet ﷺ:
“Free a slave on behalf of each one of them,”
the author explains that the Prophet ﷺ instructed him to do this to bring comfort to his heart. It was not an expiation for what he had done during the Jāhiliyyah, because Islam wipes away what came before it.

13682
Masrūq narrated:
I was reclining in the presence of ʿĀʾishah when she said:
“O Abū ʿĀʾishah, whoever says any one of three things has fabricated a tremendous lie against Allah.”
I asked:
“What are they?”
She said:
“Whoever claims that Muḥammad ﷺ saw his Lord has fabricated a tremendous lie against Allah.”
I had been reclining, but I sat upright and said:
“O Mother of the Believers, give me a moment and do not rush me. Did Allah, Mighty and Majestic, not say: ‘And he certainly saw him on the clear horizon’ [81:23], and: ‘And he certainly saw him another time’ [53:13]?”
She replied:
“I was the first person of this Ummah to ask the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about that. He said: ‘It was only Jibrīl. I did not see him in the form in which he was created except on those two occasions. I saw him descending from the sky, the enormity of his creation filling what was between the sky and the earth.’”
The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (177).

13683
Jābir narrated:
Muʿādh stood to lead the ʿIshāʾ prayer and prolonged it.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Are you putting people to trial, O Muʿādh? Why did you not recite ‘Glorify the Name of your Lord, the Most High,’ ‘By the morning brightness,’ and ‘When the sky is split open’?”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11588).
Al-Bukhārī (6106) and Muslim (465) also narrated the report, both through ʿAmr ibn Dīnār from Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh.
In Muslim’s wording, the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Recite ‘By the sun and its brightness,’ ‘Glorify the Name of your Lord, the Most High,’ ‘Read in the Name of your Lord,’ and ‘By the night when it covers.’”
In al-Bukhārī’s wording:
“Recite ‘By the sun and its brightness,’ ‘Glorify the Name of your Lord, the Most High,’ and similar sūrahs.”
The mention of:
“When the sky is split open”
does not occur in the narrations of al-Bukhārī or Muslim. It was uniquely narrated by Muḥārib ibn Dithār from Jābir in al-Nasāʾī. Muḥārib ibn Dithār is trustworthy, so his additional wording is accepted.

13684
Busr ibn Jaḥḥāsh al-Qurashī narrated that one day the Prophet ﷺ spat into his palm, placed his finger upon it, and said:
“Allah says: ‘Son of Ādam! How can you think you will escape Me when I created you from something like this? Then when I formed you and proportioned you, you walked about between two garments, making the earth resound beneath you. You gathered wealth and withheld it, until when the soul reached the collarbones you said, “Now I will give charity.” But how can this be the time for charity?’”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (2707) and Aḥmad (17842), whose wording is given here. Al-Ḥākim authenticated it (2/502).

13685
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when he laughed.
He asked:
“Do you know what makes me laugh?”
We replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“It is the servant’s conversation with his Lord. He says: ‘My Lord, did You not protect me from injustice?’ Allah says: ‘Yes.’ The servant then says: ‘Then I will accept no witness against myself except one from myself.’
Allah says: ‘Today, you yourself are sufficient as a witness against you, along with the noble recording angels as witnesses.’
Then his mouth will be sealed, and it will be said to his limbs: ‘Speak.’ So they will speak about his deeds. Then he will be allowed to speak again, and he will say to his limbs: ‘Away with you and curse you! It was for your sake that I was arguing!’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2969).

13686
Abū Hurayrah narrated that when the verse:
“And warn your closest relatives.” [Sūrat al-Shuʿarāʾ 26:214]
was revealed to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he said:
“O people of Quraysh! Save yourselves from Allah, for I cannot avail you anything against Allah.
O Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib! I cannot avail you anything against Allah.
O ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib! I cannot avail you anything against Allah.
O Ṣafiyyah, aunt of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ! I cannot avail you anything against Allah.
O Fāṭimah, daughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ! Ask me for whatever you wish, but I cannot avail you anything against Allah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2753) and Muslim (206).
There are other hadiths with this same meaning, and they are mentioned in the commentary on Sūrat al-Shuʿarāʾ.

13687
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the Prophet ﷺ arrived in Madinah, its people were among the worst of people in giving measure.
So Allah, Glorified and Exalted, revealed:
“Woe to those who give less than due.”
After that, they became fair and accurate in their measuring.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (2223), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4919) and al-Ḥākim (2/33).
Among those in Madinah who became well known for giving deficient measure was a man whose kunyah was Abū Juhaynah and whose name was ʿAmr. He had two measuring vessels: with one he would take from others, and with the other he would give to them.

13688
Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“One of them will stand in his sweat until it reaches halfway up his ears.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6531) and Muslim (2862).

13689
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The people will sweat on the Day of Resurrection until their sweat penetrates seventy cubits into the earth, and it will overwhelm them until it reaches their ears.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6532) and Muslim (2863). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13690
Al-Miqdād ibn al-Aswad narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“On the Day of Resurrection the sun will be brought close to creation until it is only the distance of a mīl from them.”
Sulaym ibn ʿĀmir said:
“By Allah, I do not know what he meant by mīl: whether a mile of distance or the small stick used for applying kohl to the eye.”
The Prophet ﷺ continued:
“People will be immersed in sweat according to their deeds. Some will have it reach their ankles, some their knees, some their waists, and some will be completely bridled by their sweat.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ pointed with his hand toward his mouth.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2864).

13691
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir said:
I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ saying:
“The sun will draw near to the earth and the people will sweat. Among them will be those whose sweat reaches their ankles; among them those whose sweat reaches halfway up their shins; among them those whose sweat reaches their knees; among them those whose sweat reaches their buttocks; among them those whose sweat reaches their waist; among them those whose sweat reaches their neck; and among them those whose sweat reaches the middle of their mouth.”
He gestured with his hand toward his mouth, showing that the sweat would bridle him.
He then said:
“And among them will be those whom their sweat completely covers.”
And he gestured with his hand.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (17/302), Ibn Ḥibbān (7329), whose wording is given here, and al-Ḥākim (4/571).

13692
Abū Umāmah al-Bāhilī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“On the Day of Resurrection the sun will draw near to within a mile, and its heat will be increased by such-and-such. Heads will boil from it just as cooking pots boil. People will sweat according to the measure of their sins. Among them will be those whose sweat reaches their ankles, among them those whose sweat reaches their shins, among them those whose sweat reaches their waist, and among them those whom the sweat will bridle.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22186) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (8/222).

13693
ʿĀṣim ibn Ḥumayd said:
I asked ʿĀʾishah:
“With what did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ begin his night prayer?”
She replied:
“You have asked me about something no one before you has ever asked me about. When he stood for prayer, he would say Allāhu akbar ten times, praise Allah ten times, glorify Allah ten times, say lā ilāha illa Allāh ten times, and seek Allah’s forgiveness ten times. Then he would say:
‘O Allah, forgive me, guide me, and provide for me.’
And he would seek refuge from the distress of the standing on the Day of Resurrection.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (766), al-Nasāʾī (1618), Ibn Mājah (1356), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (2602).

13694
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“When a believer commits a sin, a black spot appears upon his heart. If he repents, abandons the sin, and seeks forgiveness, his heart is polished clean. But if he increases in sin, the blackness increases. That is the stain which Allah mentioned in His Book: ‘Rather, a stain has covered their hearts because of what they used to earn.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3334), Ibn Mājah (4244), and Aḥmad (7952). Ibn Ḥibbān authenticated it (930).

13695
Abū Salamah said:
I saw Abū Hurayrah recite:
“When the sky is split open.”
Then he prostrated during its recitation.
I said:
“O Abū Hurayrah, did I not just see you prostrate?”
He replied:
“Had I not seen the Prophet ﷺ prostrate upon reciting it, I would not have prostrated.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1074) and Muslim (578).

13696
ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“No one will be subjected to a detailed reckoning except that he will perish.”
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, may Allah make me your ransom, does Allah, Mighty and Majestic, not say: ‘As for the one who is given his record in his right hand, he will be given an easy reckoning’?”
He replied:
“That is merely the presentation of the deeds. Whoever is interrogated in detail concerning his reckoning will perish.”
In another narration:
“He will be punished.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4939) and Muslim (2876:80). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13697
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say during one of his prayers:
“O Allah, call me to account with an easy reckoning.”
When he finished, I asked:
“O Prophet of Allah, what is the easy reckoning?”
He replied:
“It is that a person’s record is looked at and then he is pardoned. Indeed, whoever is subjected to a detailed reckoning on that Day, O ʿĀʾishah, will perish. And everything that afflicts a believer is made by Allah, Mighty and Majestic, an expiation for him, even a thorn that pricks him.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (24215), and authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (849) and al-Ḥākim (1/57, 255).

13698
ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever is subjected to a detailed reckoning will perish.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Sunnah (886) and al-Bazzār in his Musnad (2198).

13699
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The time for the Maghrib prayer lasts as long as the twilight glow has not disappeared.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (612:173).

13700
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said regarding the words:
“You will surely pass from one state to another”:
“From one condition to another.”
He said:
“This refers to your Prophet ﷺ.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4940).

13701
Ṣuhayb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Among the people before you there was a king who had a magician. When the magician grew old, he said to the king, ‘I have grown old, so send me a boy whom I can teach magic.’ So the king sent him a boy to teach.
On the boy’s route there was a monk. The boy sat with him and listened to his words, and he was impressed by them. Thereafter, whenever he went to the magician, he would pass by the monk and sit with him. When he arrived late to the magician, the magician would beat him.
The boy complained about this to the monk, who told him: ‘When you fear the magician, say, “My family detained me,” and when you fear your family, say, “The magician detained me.”’
While this continued, he came upon a huge beast that had blocked the people’s way. He said, ‘Today I will know whether the magician or the monk is better.’ He picked up a stone and said, ‘O Allah, if the affair of the monk is more beloved to You than that of the magician, kill this beast so that the people may pass.’ He threw the stone at it, killed it, and the people passed.
He went to the monk and informed him. The monk said, ‘My son, today you have become better than I. Your affair has reached the level I now see, and you will certainly be tested. If you are tested, do not reveal my identity.’
The boy used to cure those born blind and those suffering from leprosy, and he treated people suffering from all kinds of illnesses. A close companion of the king who had become blind heard about him and came to him with many gifts, saying, ‘Everything here will be yours if you cure me.’
The boy replied, ‘I do not cure anyone. It is only Allah who cures. If you believe in Allah, I will supplicate to Allah and He will cure you.’
The man believed in Allah, and Allah restored his sight. He then returned to the king and sat with him as he used to.
The king asked, ‘Who restored your sight?’
He replied, ‘My Lord.’
The king said, ‘Do you have a lord other than me?’
He replied, ‘My Lord and your Lord is Allah.’
The king seized him and continued torturing him until he revealed the boy.
The boy was brought, and the king said, ‘My son, your magic has reached such a level that you cure those born blind and those suffering from leprosy, and you do such-and-such.’
The boy replied, ‘I do not cure anyone. It is only Allah who cures.’
The king seized him and continued torturing him until he revealed the monk.
The monk was brought and told, ‘Abandon your religion.’ He refused. So the king called for a saw. It was placed at the parting of his head, and he was sawn until the two halves of his body fell apart.
Then the king’s companion was brought and told, ‘Abandon your religion.’ He refused. The saw was placed at the parting of his head, and he was sawn until his two halves fell apart.
Then the boy was brought and told, ‘Abandon your religion.’ He refused. The king handed him over to a group of his men and said, ‘Take him to such-and-such mountain. Climb to its summit with him. When you reach the top, if he abandons his religion, leave him; otherwise throw him down.’
They took him up the mountain, and he said, ‘O Allah, suffice me against them however You will.’ The mountain shook beneath them and they fell, while the boy came walking back to the king.
The king asked, ‘What happened to your companions?’
He replied, ‘Allah sufficed me against them.’
The king then handed him to another group and said, ‘Take him away, place him in a small boat, and take him to the middle of the sea. If he abandons his religion, leave him; otherwise throw him into the sea.’
They took him away, and he said, ‘O Allah, suffice me against them however You will.’ The boat overturned with them and they drowned, while the boy came walking back to the king.
The king asked, ‘What happened to your companions?’
He replied, ‘Allah sufficed me against them.’
The boy then said to the king, ‘You will never be able to kill me until you do what I command you.’
The king asked, ‘What is that?’
He said, ‘Gather the people together in one open place and crucify me upon a tree trunk. Then take an arrow from my quiver, place it in the center of the bow, and say, “In the Name of Allah, the Lord of the boy.” Then shoot me. If you do that, you will kill me.’
The king gathered the people in one place, crucified the boy upon a tree trunk, took an arrow from his quiver, placed it in the center of the bow, and said, ‘In the Name of Allah, the Lord of the boy.’ Then he shot him.
The arrow struck the boy in his temple. He placed his hand on the place where the arrow had struck and died.
The people said, ‘We believe in the Lord of the boy! We believe in the Lord of the boy! We believe in the Lord of the boy!’
The king was told, ‘Do you see what you feared? By Allah, what you feared has now happened to you. The people have believed.’
So he ordered trenches to be dug at the entrances of the roads. Fires were lit in them, and he said, ‘Whoever does not renounce his religion, throw him into them,’ or it was said to each person, ‘Enter the fire yourself.’
They did so until a woman came carrying her child. She hesitated to fall into the fire, so the child said to her, ‘Mother, be patient, for you are upon the truth.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3005).
Aḥmad (23931) also narrated it through his chain, and in his version:
“A woman came with an infant whom she was nursing, and it was as though she hesitated to fall into the Fire.”
The author says:
This infant would therefore be the fourth child to have spoken in the cradle.
Accordingly, the hadith of Abū Hurayrah in which the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Only three spoke in the cradle,”
as previously mentioned in the accounts of earlier peoples, may be understood as having been stated earlier, after which further revelation informed the Prophet ﷺ of this fourth child.
Thus the numerical restriction in the hadith of Abū Hurayrah would no longer remain absolute.
The author considers this interpretation preferable to the view of those who regard the wording:
“with an infant whom she was nursing”
as anomalous.
Regarding the word:
“qarqūr,”
it has been said that it means a small boat.
Regarding:
“the boat overturned with them,”
it means that it capsized.
And regarding:
“throw him into it,”
it means: force him into the fire.

13702
Jābir narrated:
Muʿādh led the Maghrib prayer and recited Sūrat al-Baqarah and Sūrat al-Nisāʾ.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Are you putting people to trial, O Muʿādh? Would it not have been sufficient for you to recite ‘By the sky and the night-comer’ and ‘By the sun and its brightness’?”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11600).
Al-Bukhārī (705) also narrated it in a lengthy hadith, in which the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Why did you not pray with ‘Glorify the Name of your Lord, the Most High,’ ‘By the sun and its brightness,’ and ‘By the night when it covers’…”
The hadith continues.

13703
Al-Barāʾ narrated:
The first of the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ to come to us were Muṣʿab ibn ʿUmayr and Ibn Umm Maktūm, and they began teaching us the Qur’an.
Then came ʿAmmār, Bilāl, and Saʿd. After that ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb came with twenty men, and then the Prophet ﷺ arrived.
I never saw the people of Madinah rejoice over anything as much as they rejoiced over his arrival, to the extent that I saw young girls and children saying:
“The Messenger of Allah has arrived!”
By the time he came, I had already recited:
“Glorify the Name of your Lord, the Most High,”
along with other sūrahs similar to it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4941).

13704
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
Muʿādh ibn Jabal used to pray with the Prophet ﷺ and then return to his people and lead them in prayer.
On one occasion he recited Sūrat al-Baqarah while leading them.
A man then left the congregation and performed a shorter prayer by himself.
When Muʿādh heard about this, he said:
“He is a hypocrite.”
The statement reached the man, so he came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we are people who work with our hands and draw water using our camels. Last night Muʿādh led us in prayer and recited Sūrat al-Baqarah. So I left and prayed separately, and he claimed that I am a hypocrite.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“O Muʿādh, are you putting people to trial?”
He said it three times, and then said:
“Recite ‘By the sun and its brightness,’ ‘Glorify the Name of your Lord, the Most High,’ and similar sūrahs.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6106) and Muslim (465). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13705
Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to recite in the two ʿĪd prayers and in the Friday prayer:
“Glorify the Name of your Lord, the Most High,”
and:
“Has the report of the Overwhelming Event reached you?”
He said:
“If ʿĪd and Friday occurred on the same day, he would recite these two sūrahs in both prayers.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (878).

13706
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to perform Witr by reciting:
“Glorify the Name of your Lord, the Most High,”
then:
“Say: O disbelievers,”
and:
“Say: He is Allah, One.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1423), Ibn Mājah (1171), and Aḥmad (21141). Ibn Ḥibbān authenticated it (2436).

13707
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to pray three rakʿahs of Witr.
In the first he would recite:
“Glorify the Name of your Lord, the Most High.”
In the second:
“Say: O disbelievers.”
And in the third:
“Say: He is Allah, One.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (1702) and al-Dārimī (1596).
There are other hadiths with this meaning mentioned in the Book of Prayer.

13708
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“As for the people of the Fire who truly belong to it, they will neither die therein nor live. But there will be people whom the Fire afflicts because of their sins—or he said: because of their offenses—and it will cause them to die a kind of death.
When they become like charcoal, permission will be given for intercession. They will be brought out in groups upon groups and scattered along the rivers of Paradise.
Then it will be said: ‘O people of Paradise, pour water over them.’ They will then grow like a seed that grows in the debris carried by a flood.”
A man among those present said:
“It is as though the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had lived in the desert.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (185).
Regarding the word:
“ḍabāʾir,”
it is the plural of ḍibārah, with either kasrah or fatḥah on the ḍād, and means separate groups or batches.
And the words:
“They were scattered”
mean that they were dispersed.

13709
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever pursues this world harms his Hereafter, and whoever pursues the Hereafter diminishes his worldly interests.”
Then I heard him say:
“So sacrifice what is temporary for what is everlasting.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Zuhd (161).

13710
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ slept upon a woven mat. When he rose, the mat had left marks on his side.
We said:
“O Messenger of Allah, if only we could prepare some bedding for you.”
He replied:
“What have I to do with this world? I am in this world only like a rider who seeks shade beneath a tree, then moves on and leaves it behind.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2377), Ibn Mājah (4109), and Aḥmad (4208). Al-Ḥākim authenticated it (4/310).
The word:
“wiṭāʾ”
means bedding or a mattress.

13711
ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
Al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays wrote to al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr asking him:
“What did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recite on Friday besides Sūrat al-Jumuʿah?”
He replied:
“He used to recite: ‘Has the report of the Overwhelming Event reached you?’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (878:63).

13712
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
We had been forbidden from asking the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about anything, so we used to like it when an intelligent Bedouin would come and ask him while we listened.
A man from the desert came and said:
“O Muḥammad, your messenger came to us and claimed that you claim Allah has sent you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“He spoke the truth.”
The man asked:
“Who created the sky?”
He replied:
“Allah.”
He asked:
“Who created the earth?”
He replied:
“Allah.”
He asked:
“Who set up these mountains and placed within them what He placed?”
He replied:
“Allah.”
The man said:
“By the One who created the sky, created the earth, and set up these mountains—did Allah send you?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The man said:
“Your messenger also claimed that five prayers are obligatory upon us during our day and night.”
He replied:
“He spoke the truth.”
The man asked:
“By the One who sent you, did Allah command you to do this?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The man continued:
“Your messenger also claimed that zakāh is due from our wealth.”
He replied:
“He spoke the truth.”
The man asked:
“By the One who sent you, did Allah command you to do this?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The man said:
“Your messenger also claimed that we must fast the month of Ramaḍān each year.”
He replied:
“He spoke the truth.”
The man asked:
“By the One who sent you, did Allah command you to do this?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The man said:
“Your messenger also claimed that whoever among us is able to make the journey must perform pilgrimage to the House.”
He replied:
“He spoke the truth.”
Then the man turned to leave and said:
“By the One who sent you with the truth, I will neither add anything to these nor take anything away from them.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“If he is truthful, he will certainly enter Paradise.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (12).

13713
Jābir narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I have been commanded to fight the people until they say: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah.’ When they say, ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,’ they protect from me their lives and their wealth except where there is a lawful right against them, and their reckoning is with Allah.”
Then he recited:
“So remind; you are only a reminder. You are not a controller over them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (21).

13714
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“All of my Ummah will enter Paradise except those who refuse.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, who would refuse?”
He replied:
“Whoever obeys me will enter Paradise, and whoever disobeys me has refused.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7280).

13715
ʿAlī ibn Khālid narrated that Abū Umāmah al-Bāhilī passed by Khālid ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiyah, who asked him about the gentlest statement he had heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Abū Umāmah replied:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Indeed, all of you will enter Paradise except one who bolts away from Allah like a camel fleeing from its owners.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22226) and al-Ḥākim (1/55–56).

13716
Jābir narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The ten are the ten days of al-Aḍḥā, the odd is the Day of ʿArafah, and the even is the Day of Sacrifice.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (14511), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2286), and al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11608).
The statement:
“The ten are the ten days of al-Aḍḥā”
is the view of many scholars.
A number of hadiths have been narrated concerning their virtue, including:

13717
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There are no deeds performed during any days that are better than deeds performed during these ten days.”
They asked:
“Not even jihād?”
He replied:
“Not even jihād, except for a man who goes out risking both his life and his wealth and returns with neither.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (969).

13718
Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There are no days greater in the sight of Allah, nor are deeds performed during any days more beloved to Allah than during these days. So increase during them your saying of ‘lā ilāha illa Allāh’ and your praise of Allah.”
Meaning the ten days.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū ʿAwānah in his Musnad (3024).

13719
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated:
I was with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when deeds were mentioned.
He said:
“There are no days in which deeds are better than during these ten days.”
They asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, not even jihād in the cause of Allah?”
He regarded that as a great matter and then said:
“Not even jihād, except for a man who goes out with his life and his wealth in the cause of Allah, and then loses his life therein.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (6559), Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Jihād (157), and al-Ṭayālisī (2397).

13720
Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I and the one who sponsors an orphan will be in Paradise like this.”
He gestured with his index and middle fingers.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6005).

13721
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Hell will be brought forth on that Day with seventy thousand reins, and with every rein there will be seventy thousand angels dragging it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2842).

13722
ʿUtbah ibn ʿAbd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“If a man were to fall upon his face in worship from the day he was born until the day he died of old age, seeking the pleasure of Allah, he would consider even that insignificant on the Day of Resurrection.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (17649), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (17/122–123), and al-Bayhaqī in Shuʿab al-Īmān (751).
This is supported by a mawqūf report from Muḥammad ibn Abī ʿUmayrah, who was one of the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ. He said:
“If a servant were to fall upon his face in obedience to Allah from the day he was born until he died of old age, he would regard it as insignificant on that Day and would wish that he could be returned to the world so that he might increase in reward and recompense.”
Narrated by Aḥmad (17650), Ibn al-Mubārak in al-Zuhd (34), and Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Āḥād wa al-Mathānī (1124).

13723
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Any man who frees a Muslim slave, Allah will save one of his own limbs from the Fire for every limb of the slave he freed.”
Saʿīd ibn Marjānah said:
I went with this hadith to ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn. ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn then went to a slave of his for whom ʿAbdullāh ibn Jaʿfar had offered ten thousand dirhams, or one thousand dinars, and he set him free.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2517) and Muslim (1509:24).

13724
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
A man came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I am suffering from severe hunger.”
The Prophet ﷺ sent to his wives, but nothing was found with them.
So the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Is there any man who will host him tonight? May Allah have mercy on him.”
A man from the Anṣār stood and said:
“I will, O Messenger of Allah.”
He went home and said to his wife:
“This is the guest of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Do not hold anything back from him.”
She replied:
“By Allah, I have nothing except the food of the children.”
He said:
“When the children want their evening meal, put them to sleep. Then come and extinguish the lamp, and tonight we will endure hunger ourselves.”
She did so.
The next morning the man went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he said:
“Allah, Mighty and Majestic, was amazed—or laughed—at what so-and-so and his wife did.”
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“They give others preference over themselves even though they themselves are in need.” [Sūrat al-Ḥashr 59:9]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4889) and Muslim (2054).
In a narration of Muslim:
“A man from the Anṣār stood up, who was called Abū Ṭalḥah.”

13725
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, whenever I see you, my soul becomes content and my eyes are comforted. Tell me about everything.”
He said:
“Everything was created from water.”
I then said:
“Tell me of an action which, if I hold fast to it, will cause me to enter Paradise.”
He said:
“Spread the greeting of peace, feed people, maintain ties of kinship, and stand in prayer at night while the people are asleep; then enter Paradise in peace.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (7932), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (508) and al-Ḥākim (4/160).

13726
Umm Kulthūm bint ʿUqbah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The best charity is that given to a relative who harbors hostility toward you.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (1/406), and from him by al-Bayhaqī (7/27).
There are also hadiths concerning the general virtue of sponsoring an orphan, including the following authentic report:

13727
Sahl ibn Saʿd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I and the one who sponsors an orphan will be in Paradise like this.”
He gestured with his index finger and middle finger.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6005).

13728
Jarīr ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever does not show mercy to people, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will not show mercy to him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7376) and Muslim (2319). The wording is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.

13729
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
Muʿādh ibn Jabal used to pray with the Prophet ﷺ and then return to his people and lead them in prayer.
On one occasion he recited Sūrat al-Baqarah while leading them. A man then left and performed a shorter prayer by himself.
When Muʿādh heard of this, he said:
“He is a hypocrite.”
When this reached the man, he came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we are people who work with our hands and draw water with our camels. Muʿādh led us in prayer last night and recited Sūrat al-Baqarah, so I left and prayed separately, and he claimed that I am a hypocrite.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“O Muʿādh, are you putting people to trial?”
He said this three times, then said:
“Recite ‘By the sun and its brightness,’ ‘Glorify the Name of your Lord, the Most High,’ and similar sūrahs.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6106) and Muslim (465). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13730
ʿIyāḍ ibn Ḥimār al-Mujāshiʿī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ related from his Lord, Blessed and Exalted, that He said:
“I created all My servants as ḥunafāʾ, upright upon pure monotheism. Then the devils came to them and turned them away from their religion, forbade for them what I had made lawful for them, and commanded them to associate with Me that for which I had sent down no authority…”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2865).

13731
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Every newborn is born upon the natural disposition. Then his parents make him a Jew, a Christian, or a Magian, just as an animal gives birth to a complete and sound animal. Do you perceive any mutilation in it?”
Then Abū Hurayrah would say:
“Recite, if you wish: ‘So direct your face toward the religion, inclining to truth—the natural disposition of Allah upon which He has created mankind. There is no altering the creation of Allah. That is the upright religion, but most people do not know.’” [Sūrat al-Rūm 30:30]
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1359) and Muslim (2658).
Regarding the words:
“Then inspired it with its wickedness and its righteousness,”
this means that He made clear to it good and evil, obedience and disobedience, what it should pursue of goodness and righteousness, and what it should leave of evil and corruption.
He has decreed these matters concerning them and facilitated for each what he will do.
This is stated in the authentic hadith:

13732
Abū al-Aswad al-Duʾalī narrated:
ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn said to me:
“What do you think about what people do today and strive in? Is it something already decreed for them and passed upon them from a prior decree, or is it something they encounter anew through what their Prophet has brought them and through which the proof has been established against them?”
I replied:
“Rather, it is something already decreed for them and passed upon them.”
He asked:
“Would that not then be injustice?”
I became extremely alarmed by this and said:
“Everything is Allah’s creation and belongs to Him. He is not questioned about what He does, but they will be questioned.”
He said:
“May Allah have mercy on you. I only asked you in order to test the soundness of your understanding. Two men from Muzaynah came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked:
‘O Messenger of Allah, what do you think about what people do today and strive in? Is it something that has already been decreed for them and passed upon them from a previous decree, or is it something they encounter anew through what their Prophet has brought them and by which the proof has been established against them?’”
He replied:
“No. Rather, it is something already decreed for them and passed upon them.”
And the confirmation of this is in the Book of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:
“And by the soul and He who proportioned it, then inspired it with its wickedness and its righteousness.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2650).

13733
ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn narrated:
A man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, are the people of Paradise already distinguishable from the people of the Fire?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
The man asked:
“Then why do people perform deeds?”
He replied:
“Everyone acts according to that for which he was created, or according to that which has been made easy for him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6596) and Muslim (2649).

13734
Zayd ibn Arqam said:
I will say to you only what the Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to say. He would say:
“O Allah, I seek refuge in You from incapacity, laziness, cowardice, miserliness, decrepit old age, and the punishment of the grave.
O Allah, grant my soul its righteousness and purify it; You are the best to purify it. You are its Guardian and its Master.
O Allah, I seek refuge in You from knowledge that does not benefit, from a heart that does not humble itself, from a soul that is never satisfied, and from a supplication that is not answered.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2722).

13735
ʿAbdullāh ibn Zamʿah narrated that he heard the Prophet ﷺ delivering a sermon and mentioning the she-camel and the man who hamstrung her.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“A powerful, wicked man, well protected among his people, rose up against her, a man like Abū Zamʿah.”
The hadith continues.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4942) and Muslim (2855). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13736
ʿAlqamah narrated:
I entered Syria with a group of the companions of ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd.
Abū al-Dardāʾ heard that we had arrived and came to us. He asked:
“Is there anyone among you who recites the Qur’an?”
We said:
“Yes.”
He asked:
“Which of you is the most proficient reciter?”
They pointed to me.
He said:
“Recite.”
So I recited:
“By the night when it covers, and by the day when it shines forth, and the male and the female.”
He asked:
“Did you hear this directly from the mouth of your companion?”
I replied:
“Yes.”
He said:
“And I heard it directly from the mouth of the Prophet ﷺ, yet these people reject our reading.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Tafsīr (4943) and Muslim in the Book of the Prayer of Travelers and Its Shortening (824), both through al-Aʿmash, from Ibrāhīm, from ʿAlqamah. The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar. Muslim adds: “But these people want me to recite: ‘And what He created,’ and I will not follow them.” In another al-Bukhārī narration (4944): “These people want me to recite: ‘And [by] He who created the male and the female.’ By Allah, I will not follow them.”

13737
ʿAlqamah narrated:
I came to Syria and prayed two rakʿahs. Then I said:
“O Allah, make a righteous companion easy for me.”
I came upon a group of people and sat with them. Then an elderly man came and sat beside me.
I asked:
“Who is this?”
They replied:
“Abū al-Dardāʾ.”
I said to him:
“I supplicated to Allah to make a righteous companion easy for me, and He has made you easy for me.”
He asked:
“Where are you from?”
I replied:
“From the people of Kūfah.”
He said:
“Do you not have among you Ibn Umm ʿAbd, the keeper of the Prophet’s sandals, cushion, and water vessel? And among you is the one whom Allah protected from Satan—meaning through the words of His Prophet ﷺ. And is not among you the keeper of the secret of the Prophet ﷺ, which no one else knew?”
Then he asked:
“How does ʿAbdullāh recite: ‘By the night when it covers’?”
So I recited to him:
“By the night when it covers, and by the day when it shines forth, and the male and the female.”
He said:
“By Allah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ taught it to me directly from his mouth to mine.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3742) and Muslim (823:283). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13738
ʿAlqamah narrated:
I met Abū al-Dardāʾ, and he asked me:
“Where are you from?”
I replied:
“From Iraq.”
He asked:
“From which part?”
I replied:
“From Kūfah.”
He asked:
“Do you recite according to the recitation of ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd?”
I said:
“Yes.”
He said:
“Then recite: ‘By the night when it covers.’”
So I recited:
“By the night when it covers, and by the day when it shines forth, and the male and the female.”
He laughed and then said:
“This is how I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recite it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (823:284).
The recitation of Ibn Masʿūd mentioned in these narrations is a non-canonical recitation transmitted through an authentic chain.
It was recited during the early period among the Companions and Tābiʿīn. Later, consensus was established upon restricting Qur’anic recitation to the well-known mutawātir recitations in order to prevent disagreement concerning the Qur’an.
Therefore, according to the author, it is not permissible now for anyone to recite this variant in prayer as an act of worship, nor to write it in the muṣḥaf.

13739
ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr narrated:
Abū Quḥāfah said to Abū Bakr:
“My son, I see you freeing weak slaves. If you are going to do this, why do you not free strong men who could protect you and stand in your defense?”
Abū Bakr replied:
“Father, I only seek through what I am doing what is with Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”
It was then commonly said that these verses were revealed concerning him and concerning what his father had said to him:
“As for the one who gives and fears Allah, and affirms the best reward…”
through the words:
“And no one has any favor with him that is being repaid, but only seeking the Face of his Lord, the Most High. And he will surely be pleased.” [Sūrat al-Layl 92:5–21]
Good: Narrated by ʿAbdullāh ibn Aḥmad in his additions to his father’s Faḍāʾil al-Ṣaḥābah (66), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/525).

13740
ʿAlī narrated:
We were attending a funeral at Baqīʿ al-Gharqad when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came to us and sat down, and we sat around him.
He had a small stick with him. He lowered his head and began scratching the ground with it, then said:
“There is none among you—no living soul—except that Allah has already written his place in Paradise or the Fire, and it has already been written whether he will be wretched or blessed.”
A man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, should we then simply rely upon what has been written for us and abandon deeds?”
He replied:
“Whoever is among the people of happiness will proceed toward the deeds of the people of happiness, and whoever is among the people of wretchedness will proceed toward the deeds of the people of wretchedness.”
Then he said:
“Act, for everyone will be facilitated. As for the people of happiness, they will be facilitated toward the deeds of the people of happiness; and as for the people of wretchedness, they will be facilitated toward the deeds of the people of wretchedness.”
Then he recited:
“As for the one who gives and fears Allah, and affirms the best reward, We will make easy for him the path of ease. But as for the one who is miserly and considers himself self-sufficient, and denies the best reward, We will make easy for him the path of hardship.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4978) and Muslim (2647). The wording is that of Muslim.

13741
Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I warn you of the Fire! I warn you of the Fire!”
He raised his voice to such an extent that a man at the farthest end of the marketplace could have heard him, and the people in the marketplace could hear his voice while he was upon the pulpit.
In another narration:
“A striped cloak that had been upon his shoulder fell down beside his feet.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (18360, 18398, 18399), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (644, 667) and al-Ḥākim (1/287).

13742
Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr narrated:
I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“The person receiving the lightest punishment among the people of the Fire on the Day of Resurrection will be a man beneath whose soles a burning ember will be placed, causing his brain to boil.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6561) and Muslim (213). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13743
Jundub ibn Sufyān narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ became ill and did not stand for night prayer for two or three nights.
A woman came to him and said:
“O Muḥammad, I hope your devil has abandoned you. I have not seen him come near you for two or three nights.”
Then Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“By the morning brightness, and by the night when it grows still, your Lord has neither forsaken you nor detested you.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4950) and Muslim (1797:115).
The author identifies this woman as the wife of Abū Lahab, the carrier of firewood. Her name was al-ʿAwrāʾ bint Ḥarb, the sister of Abū Sufyān, and her kunyah was Umm Jamīl.

13744
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ recited the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic, concerning Ibrāhīm:
“My Lord, indeed they have led many people astray. So whoever follows me is truly of me.” [Sūrat Ibrāhīm 14:36]
And he recited the words of ʿĪsā, peace be upon him:
“If You punish them, they are Your servants; and if You forgive them, then indeed You are the Almighty, the All-Wise.” [Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:118]
Then he raised his hands and said:
“O Allah, my Ummah, my Ummah!”
And he wept.
Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said:
“O Jibrīl, go to Muḥammad—and your Lord knows best—and ask him what makes him weep.”
Jibrīl, peace be upon him came to him and asked him, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ informed him of what he had said, though Allah knew it best.
Then Allah said:
“O Jibrīl, go to Muḥammad and say: ‘We will surely please you concerning your Ummah, and We will not distress you.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (202).

13745
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was shown the conquests that would be granted to his Ummah after him, and he was delighted by this.
Then Allah revealed:
“By the morning brightness, and by the night when it grows still…”
through His words:
“And your Lord will surely give you until you are pleased.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“He was given one thousand palaces in Paradise made of pearls, whose soil is musk, and in every palace is everything appropriate for him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (24/487–488), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (10/337), al-Ḥākim (2/526), whose wording is given here, al-Bayhaqī in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (7/61), and al-Wāḥidī in Asbāb al-Nuzūl (pp. 490–491), all through routes from al-Awzāʿī, from Ismāʿīl ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Abī al-Muhājir al-Makhzūmī, from ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās, from his father.

13746
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
Jibrīl came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was playing with the boys.
He took hold of him, laid him down, opened his chest, and removed his heart. Then he removed a clot from it and said:
“This was Satan’s share of you.”
He then washed the heart in a golden basin with Zamzam water, put it back together, and returned it to its place.
The boys ran to his foster mother saying:
“Muḥammad has been killed!”
They met him while his color had changed.
Anas said:
“I used to see the trace of that stitching on his chest.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (162:261).

13747
Anas narrated that Abū Dharr used to report that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The roof of my house was opened while I was in Makkah, and Jibrīl descended. He opened my chest and washed it with Zamzam water. Then he brought a golden vessel filled with wisdom and faith, poured it into my chest, and then closed it…”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (349) and Muslim (163).

13748
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I asked my Lord for something and afterward wished that I had not asked Him. I said, ‘My Lord, there were messengers before me. Among them was one for whom the winds were subjected, and among them was one who brought the dead to life.’
He said: ‘Did I not find you an orphan and give you shelter? Did I not find you unaware and guide you? Did I not expand your chest for you and remove your burden from you?’
I said: ‘Yes, my Lord.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī Ḥātim, as cited in Tafsīr Ibn Kathīr (8/430), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (3664), and in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (11/455), and al-Ḥākim (2/526).
Regarding the words:
“And We removed from you your burden,”
the author explains this as meaning:
We lightened for you the burdens of prophethood, made it easier for you to fulfill its responsibilities, and relieved some of your intense concern and deep sorrow over the polytheists turning away from Islam, refusing to follow you, rejecting the truth and guidance you brought, and persisting upon the religion of their forefathers—the religion of shirk and idolatry.
Other interpretations have also been given, but the author considers this interpretation more correct, preferable, and more appropriate to the station of prophethood.
Regarding the words:
“For indeed, with hardship comes ease. Indeed, with hardship comes ease.”
Ibn ʿUyaynah said:
This means that alongside that one hardship there comes another ease.
It is similar to the words of Allah:
“Say: Are you waiting for anything to happen to us except one of the two best outcomes?” [Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:52]
And:
“One hardship will never overcome two eases.”
Al-Bukhārī mentioned this in the Book of Tafsīr.

13749
Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib narrated that the Prophet ﷺ was on a journey and, in one of the two rakʿahs of the ʿIshāʾ prayer, recited Sūrat al-Tīn.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4952) and Muslim (464). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.
Al-Bukhārī (769) and Muslim also narrate through another route from him the additional words:
“I never heard anyone with a more beautiful voice than him.”
Regarding the words:
“By the fig,”
the fig is the well-known fruit of that name. It has many benefits, and thus Allah swears by it as a reminder of His favor upon His servants in having created it for their enjoyment.
Regarding:
“and the olive,”
the olive is the well-known fruit of that name from which olive oil is extracted, and its oil is among the finest kinds of oil.
There is no disagreement among the earlier or later commentators that these two names can be understood according to their apparent meaning.
However, because Allah mentions alongside them:
“Mount Sinai, and this secure city,”
some commentators adopted another interpretation more closely corresponding to the mention of Mount Sinai and the secure city.
According to this interpretation, “the fig and the olive” refer to the blessed land in which these two trees grow abundantly, namely the lands of al-Shām.
Some even said that they are names of two mosques in al-Shām.
Muḥammad ibn Kaʿb said:
“The fig is the mosque of the Companions of the Cave, and the olive is the mosque of Īliyāʾ.”
Regarding:
“Mount Sinai,”
this is the mountain known as Mount Sinai.
Al-Ṭūr means “mountain” in the language of the Canaanites, and it is the mountain upon which Allah spoke to Mūsā, peace be upon him.
Regarding:
“and this secure city,”
this refers to Makkah without disagreement, because Allah made it a secure city, as He says:
“And He made them safe from fear.”
Some scholars said that these verses contain an allusion to three of the Messengers of Firm Resolve and to the places where they were sent and received revelation.
Thus the words:
“By the fig and the olive”
allude to Bayt al-Maqdis and its surrounding region, where ʿĪsā, peace be upon him was sent, for that land is well known for its abundance of figs and olives.
The words:
“Mount Sinai”
refer to Mount Sinai, upon which Allah spoke to Mūsā, peace be upon him.
And:
“this secure city”
means Makkah, the city in which whoever enters is safe, and the place in which our Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ was commissioned as a prophet.
The author says:
The view I consider strongest is to understand these expressions according to their apparent meanings.
All of these are oaths, and the statement upon which the oaths are sworn is:
“We have certainly created man in the best form.”
That is, with the most balanced stature and finest appearance.
Allah created other animals bent forward toward the ground, while He created the human being upright in stature, able to take his food with his hand, together with intellect and discernment.
The words:
“in the best form”
may also be understood as referring to the human being attaining the ultimate purpose for which he was created, as Allah says:
“I did not create the jinn and mankind except to worship Me.” [Sūrat al-Dhāriyāt 51:56]
For the words:
“Then We return him to the lowest of the low” [Sūrat al-Tīn 95:5]
indicate that if he misses this path, he will become among the lowest of the low—that is, among the people of the Fire.
Regarding the words:
“Except those who believe and do righteous deeds, for they will have a never-ending reward,”
those who did not miss the path are excluded from the preceding judgment.
They understood the purpose of their life and creation, followed the Messengers, and acted according to their revealed laws.
They will have with Allah a tremendous reward that will never cease.
Regarding:
“Is Allah not the wisest of judges?”
the meaning is that He is the supreme Judge, and in every one of His decrees there is wisdom and benefit.
It has been narrated from Abū Hurayrah that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever recites ‘By the fig and the olive,’ then let him say: ‘Yes indeed, and I am among those who testify to that.’”
Narrated by Aḥmad (7391), al-Tirmidhī (3347), and Abū Dāwūd (887).
Its chain of transmission is weak because of an unnamed man.
Al-Tirmidhī said:
“This hadith is only narrated through this chain from this Bedouin, from Abū Hurayrah, and he is not named.”

13750
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The first thing with which revelation began for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was true dreams during sleep. He would not see any dream except that it came to pass as clearly as the breaking of dawn.
Then solitude was made beloved to him. He would go to the cave of Ḥirāʾ and devote himself to worship there for a number of nights before returning to his family.
He would take provisions for that, then return to Khadījah and take provisions for another similar period, until the truth came upon him suddenly while he was in the cave of Ḥirāʾ.
The angel came to him and said:
“Read.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“I am not one who can read.”
He said:
“Then he seized me and pressed me tightly until I reached the limit of my endurance. Then he released me and said, ‘Read.’ I said, ‘I am not one who can read.’ He seized me and pressed me a second time until I reached the limit of my endurance, then released me and said, ‘Read.’ I said, ‘I am not one who can read.’ Then he seized me and pressed me a third time until I reached the limit of my endurance, and then released me.”
Then he said:
“Read in the Name of your Lord who created; created man from a clinging substance. Read, and your Lord is the Most Generous, who taught by the pen…”
through the words:
“taught man what he did not know.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned with these verses, the muscles between his neck and shoulders trembling, until he entered upon Khadījah and said:
“Cover me! Cover me!”
They covered him until the fear left him.
He then said to Khadījah:
“O Khadījah, what is happening to me? I feared for myself.”
He told her what had happened.
Khadījah said:
“No! Be glad. By Allah, Allah will never disgrace you. By Allah, you maintain ties of kinship, speak truthfully, bear the burdens of those who are unable, provide for the destitute, honor guests, and assist people when calamities strike in the cause of truth.”
Khadījah then took him to Waraqah ibn Nawfal, who was the son of her paternal uncle.
He had become a Christian during the period of Jāhiliyyah. He used to write in Arabic and would write from the Gospel in Arabic whatever Allah willed him to write. He was an elderly man who had become blind.
Khadījah said:
“O son of my uncle, listen to your nephew.”
Waraqah asked:
“My nephew, what have you seen?”
The Prophet ﷺ told him what he had seen.
Waraqah said:
“This is the same Nāmūs—the bearer of divine revelation—whom Allah sent to Mūsā. If only I were young when your mission begins! If only I were alive when your people drive you out!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Will they really drive me out?”
Waraqah replied:
“Yes. No man has ever brought what you have brought except that he was opposed. If I remain alive until your day comes, I will support you with powerful support.”
Not long afterward, Waraqah died, and revelation ceased for a period, causing the Messenger of Allah ﷺ grief.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4953) and Muslim (160). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.

13751
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
Abū Jahl asked:
“Does Muḥammad place his face in the dust while he is among you?”
They replied:
“Yes.”
He said:
“By al-Lāt and al-ʿUzzā, if I see him doing that, I will certainly step on his neck or rub his face into the dust.”
He then came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was praying, intending, as he claimed, to step upon his neck.
But suddenly they saw him retreating backward on his heels while shielding himself with his hands.
He was asked:
“What is wrong with you?”
He replied:
“Between me and him is a trench of fire, terror, and wings.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Had he come near me, the angels would have torn him apart limb by limb.”
Then Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed—we do not know whether this was part of Abū Hurayrah’s narration or something that reached him:
“No! Indeed, man surely transgresses because he sees himself as self-sufficient. Indeed, to your Lord is the return. Have you seen the one who forbids a servant when he prays? Have you considered if he is upon guidance, or commands righteousness? Have you considered if he denies and turns away? Does he not know that Allah sees? No! If he does not stop, We will surely drag him by the forelock—a lying, sinful forelock. So let him call his assembly; We will call the guards of Hell. No! Do not obey him.”
ʿUbaydullāh added in his narration:
“And He commanded him with what He commanded him.”
Ibn ʿAbd al-Aʿlā added regarding:
“So let him call his assembly,”
meaning:
“Let him call his people.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2797).

13752
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Abū Jahl said:
“If I see Muḥammad praying by the Kaʿbah, I will certainly step upon his neck.”
When this reached the Prophet ﷺ, he said:
“Had he done it, the angels would have seized him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4958).

13753
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ was praying when Abū Jahl came and said:
“Did I not forbid you from doing this? Did I not forbid you from doing this? Did I not forbid you from doing this?”
The Prophet ﷺ finished and rebuked him sternly.
Abū Jahl said:
“You know very well that no one in this valley has a larger assembly than I do.”
So Allah revealed:
“Then let him call his assembly. We will call the guards of Hell.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“By Allah, had he called his assembly, the guards of Allah’s punishment would have seized him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3349).
Regarding:
“a lying, sinful forelock,”
this refers to the forelock of Abū Jahl: lying in what he said and sinful in what he did.
Regarding:
“Then let him call his assembly,”
this means his people and his clan.
Regarding:
“We will call the Zabāniyah,”
al-zabāniyah is the plural of zabnī, derived from al-zabn, meaning pushing or driving forcefully.
They are the stern and mighty angels who will drive him into the punishment.

13754
Abū Salamah said:
I asked Abū Saʿīd, who was a friend of mine, and he said:
We observed iʿtikāf with the Prophet ﷺ during the middle ten nights of Ramaḍān.
On the morning of the twentieth, he came out and addressed us, saying:
“I was shown Laylat al-Qadr, but then I was made to forget it—or I forgot it—so seek it during the last ten nights, on the odd-numbered nights. I also saw in a dream that I was prostrating in water and mud.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2016) and Muslim (1167:216).

13755
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever stands in prayer on Laylat al-Qadr with faith and seeking reward from Allah will have his previous sins forgiven.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1901) and Muslim (760).

13756
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to Ubayy ibn Kaʿb:
“Allah has commanded me to recite to you: ‘Those who disbelieved were not going to cease…’”
Ubayy asked:
“Did He mention me by name to you?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Yes.”
So Ubayy wept.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4959) and Muslim (799:246). The wording is that of Muslim.

13757
Anas ibn Mālik narrated that the Prophet of Allah ﷺ said to Ubayy ibn Kaʿb:
“Allah has commanded me to recite the Qur’an to you.”
Ubayy asked:
“Did Allah mention me by name to you?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
Ubayy asked:
“Was I mentioned before the Lord of the worlds?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
Then Ubayy’s eyes overflowed with tears.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4961).

13758
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah has commanded me to recite the Qur’an to you.”
He then recited:
“Those who disbelieved from among the People of the Book…”
Ubayy said that within the recitation he also recited:
“If the son of Ādam were to ask for a valley full of wealth and were given it, he would ask for a second. And if he were given a second, he would ask for a third. Nothing fills the belly of the son of Ādam except dust. Allah accepts the repentance of whoever repents. And indeed, the religion with Allah is pure monotheism, neither polytheism, Judaism, nor Christianity. Whoever does good will never have it denied.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3793) and Aḥmad (21202), whose wording is given here, and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/224), all through Shuʿbah ibn al-Ḥajjāj, from ʿĀṣim ibn Bahdalah, from Zirr ibn Ḥubaysh, from Ubayy ibn Kaʿb.
Regarding:
“munfakkīn,”
it means ceasing from their disbelief, shirk, and misguidance.
Regarding:
“until the clear proof came to them,”
this means the clear evidence and manifest proof, namely the Qur’an.
Regarding:
“A Messenger from Allah reciting purified pages,”
this means Muḥammad ﷺ, whom Allah sent to them to recite purified pages to them, namely the Qur’an.
It is described as purified pages because it is written in the highest assembly and protected from the approach of devils; none touch it except the purified.
Regarding:
“Within them are upright writings,”
this means that within them are writings, namely the sūrahs and verses.
Qayyimah means truthful reports and just, upright commands.
Once this clear proof comes, it becomes evident who genuinely seeks the truth and therefore follows it, and who does not seek the truth and is therefore ruined, just as occurred among those who were given the Scripture.
Allah says:
“Those who were given the Scripture did not divide until after the clear proof had come to them.”
So you, to whom this noble Messenger was sent and upon whom this magnificent Qur’an was revealed, must not be like those people.
Allah says in Sūrat Āl ʿImrān [3:105]:
“Do not be like those who became divided and differed after the clear proofs had come to them. Those will have a tremendous punishment.”
Yet they had been commanded only to worship Allah alone and not associate anyone with Him.
This is expressed in the words of Allah:
“They were commanded only to worship Allah, devoting the religion sincerely to Him as people of pure monotheism, to establish prayer, and to give zakāh. That is the upright religion.”
Regarding:
“ḥunafāʾ,”
this means turning away from all religions that oppose the religion of Islam.
Regarding:
“to establish prayer and give zakāh,”
among the greatest manifestations of sincerity toward Allah is maintaining the prayers, for they are among the noblest acts of worship.
Zakāh involves providing support to the poor and needy.
A religion composed of these elements is a just religion founded upon truth and benevolence.
As for the recompense of those to whom the clear proof came but who refused to accept it, their fate is as Allah says:
“Indeed, those who disbelieve among the People of the Book and the polytheists will be in the Fire of Hell, remaining there forever. They are the worst of creation.”
That is because they recognized the truth but refused to accept it.
Thus they will have the Fire of Hell, remaining there without being removed from it.
They are described as:
“They are the worst of creation,”
meaning the worst of created beings.
As for those who accepted the truth, they are the righteous:
“Indeed, those who believe and do righteous deeds are the best of creation. Their reward with their Lord is Gardens of everlasting residence beneath which rivers flow, remaining there forever. Allah is pleased with them, and they are pleased with Him.”
That is, Allah is pleased with them and has prepared Paradise and its delights for them, while they are pleased with these blessings and the tremendous bounty Allah, the Exalted, has granted them.
Regarding:
“That is for whoever fears his Lord,”
this reward is for the one who fears Allah, observes true taqwā of Him, and worships Him as He deserves to be worshipped.

13759
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr narrated:
A man came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“Teach me something to recite, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Recite three sūrahs from those beginning with ‘Alif Lām Rā.’”
The man replied:
“I have grown old, my heart has become hard, and my tongue has become heavy.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Then recite from the sūrahs beginning with ‘Ḥā Mīm.’”
The man repeated the same complaint.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Then recite three of the sūrahs that begin with glorification of Allah.”
The man repeated the same complaint and said:
“Rather, O Messenger of Allah, teach me one comprehensive sūrah.”
So the Prophet ﷺ taught him:
“When the earth is shaken with its final earthquake…”
When he finished it, the man said:
“By the One who sent you with the truth, I will never add anything beyond it.”
Then the man turned away.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The little man has succeeded! The little man has succeeded!”
Then he said:
“Bring him back to me.”
The man returned, and the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I have been commanded concerning the Day of Sacrifice, which Allah has made a festival for this Ummah.”
The man asked:
“What if I possess nothing except a milk-animal lent to me by my son? Should I sacrifice it?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“No. Rather, trim your hair, clip your nails, shorten your moustache, and shave your pubic hair. That will complete your sacrifice before Allah.”
