Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (6575), whose wording is given here, Abū Dāwūd (1399, 2789), and al-Nasāʾī (4365), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (5914) and al-Ḥākim (2/532), all through ʿAyyāsh ibn ʿAbbās al-Qitbānī, from ʿĪsā ibn Hilāl al-Ṣadafī, from ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ.
Regarding the expression:
“from those beginning with Alif Lām Rā,”
this means the sūrahs beginning with these three disconnected letters: Alif, Lām, Rā.
There are five such sūrahs in the Qur’an: Yūnus, Hūd, Yūsuf, Ibrāhīm, and al-Ḥijr.
Regarding:
“from those beginning with Ḥā Mīm,”
this means the sūrahs beginning with the two letters Ḥā, Mīm.
There are seven such sūrahs in the Qur’an: Ghāfir, Fuṣṣilat, al-Shūrā, al-Zukhruf, al-Dukhān, al-Jāthiyah, and al-Aḥqāf.
Regarding:
“the Musabbiḥāt,”
these are the sūrahs whose openings contain sabbaḥa, yusabbiḥu, or sabbiḥ.
They are: al-Ḥadīd, al-Ḥashr, al-Ṣaff, al-Jumuʿah, al-Taghābun, and al-Aʿlā.

13760
Muʿādh ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Juhanī narrated that a man from Juhaynah informed him that he heard the Prophet ﷺ recite:
“When the earth is shaken with its final earthquake”
in both rakʿahs of the Fajr prayer.
He said:
“I do not know whether the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had forgotten that he had already recited it, or whether he deliberately recited it in both rakʿahs.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (816).

13761
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The earth will vomit forth pieces of its liver like columns of gold and silver. The murderer will come and say, ‘It was for this that I killed.’ The one who severed ties of kinship will come and say, ‘It was for this that I severed my ties.’ The thief will come and say, ‘It was for this that my hand was cut off.’ Then they will leave it and take nothing from it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1013).
Regarding:
“On that Day it will recount its news,”
the earth will speak about what people did upon its surface.
Regarding:
“Because your Lord will have commanded it,”
the expressions awḥā lahā, awḥā ilayhā, waḥā lahā, and waḥā ilayhā all carry the same meaning, as al-Bukhārī stated.
Here, waḥy means command: Allah commands the earth to split open and release them.
Regarding:
“On that Day people will depart in scattered groups,”
ashtāt is the plural of shatt, meaning separate and divided.
People will leave the place of reckoning in differing categories and groups, some wretched and some blessed.
They will depart from the standing in separate groups toward their respective abodes in Paradise or the Fire.
Those on the right will enter Paradise, while those on the left will enter the Fire.

13762
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was asked concerning donkeys.
He replied:
“Nothing has been revealed to me specifically concerning them except this unique and comprehensive verse: ‘Whoever does an atom’s weight of good will see it, and whoever does an atom’s weight of evil will see it.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4962) and Muslim (987).
It is also narrated from ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd that he said:
“This is the most comprehensive and decisive verse in the Qur’an.”

13763
Ṣaʿṣaʿah ibn Muʿāwiyah, the uncle of al-Aḥnaf, narrated:
I came to the Prophet ﷺ and heard him reciting this verse:
“Whoever does an atom’s weight of good will see it, and whoever does an atom’s weight of evil will see it.”
I said:
“By Allah, I do not care if I never hear anything else besides this. It is enough for me, enough for me.”
Authentic: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (20594), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11630), and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (8/90).

13764
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The fire that the children of Ādam kindle is one part out of seventy parts of the Fire of Hell.”
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, this worldly fire would have been sufficient!”
He replied:
“The Fire of Hell has been made sixty-nine parts hotter than it, each part being equal to its heat.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3265) and Muslim (2843).

13765
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb said:
“We used to regard this as being from the Qur’an until ‘Competition for more has distracted you’ was revealed.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6440).
Ubayy’s statement:
“We used to regard this as being from the Qur’an”
refers to the hadith of Anas ibn Mālik in which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“If the son of Ādam had a valley full of gold, he would love to have two valleys, and nothing will fill his mouth except dust. Allah accepts the repentance of whoever repents.”
Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6439) and Muslim (1048).
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb al-Khazrajī al-Anṣārī was one of the prominent Qur’an reciters among the Companions.
Thus his statement:
“Until ‘Competition for more has distracted you’ was revealed”
apparently indicates that it was revealed in Madinah.
Al-Qurṭubī’s statement that:
“Al-Bukhārī regarded it as Madinan”
is understood in light of al-Bukhārī’s inclusion of this report from Ubayy ibn Kaʿb al-Khazrajī al-Anṣārī.

13766
ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Shikhkhīr narrated:
I came to the Prophet ﷺ while he was reciting:
“Competition for more has distracted you.”
He said:
“The son of Ādam says, ‘My wealth! My wealth!’”
Then he said:
“O son of Ādam, do you truly possess anything from your wealth except what you ate and consumed, what you wore and wore out, or what you gave in charity and thereby sent ahead?”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2958).

13767
Al-Zubayr narrated:
When the verse was revealed:
“Then, on the Day of Resurrection, you will dispute before your Lord.” [Sūrat al-Zumar 39:31]
al-Zubayr asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, even after our disputes in this world?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
And when the verse was revealed:
“Then, on that Day, you will surely be questioned about the blessings,”
al-Zubayr asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what blessings will we be questioned about when all we have are the two dark things—dates and water?”
He replied:
“Indeed, that will certainly come to pass.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (1405), al-Tirmidhī (3356), and Ibn Mājah (4158).
Al-Tirmidhī (3357) also narrated through his chain from Abū Hurayrah:
When the verse:
“Then, on that Day, you will surely be questioned about the blessings”
was revealed, the people said:
“O Messenger of Allah, what blessings will we be questioned about? All we have are the two dark things, the enemy is present, and our swords are upon our shoulders.”
He replied:
“Indeed, that will come to pass.”
The words:
“Indeed, that will come to pass”
contain a prophecy that wealth would become abundant in the future, and that indeed occurred.

13768
Maḥmūd ibn Labīd narrated:
When:
“Competition for more has distracted you”
was revealed, the Prophet ﷺ recited it until he reached:
“You will surely be questioned on that Day about the blessings.”
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, what blessings will we be questioned about? We have only the two dark things—water and dates—our swords are hanging from our necks, and the enemy is present. What blessings could we possibly be questioned about?”
He replied:
“Indeed, that will come to pass.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (23640) through Yazīd ibn Hārūn, Ibn Abī Shaybah (35486) through Muḥammad ibn Bishr, Hannād ibn al-Sarī in al-Zuhd (768) through ʿAbdah ibn Sulaymān, and al-Bayhaqī in Shuʿab al-Īmān (4278) through Abū Usāmah Ḥammād ibn Usāmah; all four narrated from Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAlqamah, from Ṣafwān ibn Sulaym, from Maḥmūd ibn Labīd.

13769
Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh narrated:
My father was killed on the Day of Uḥud, leaving behind two gardens, while he owed dates to a Jewish man. The Jewish man’s claim threatened to consume everything in both gardens.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to him:
“Would you accept part of it this year and postpone part until next year?”
He refused.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“When the date harvest arrives, inform me.”
Jābir said:
I informed him when the harvest came.
The Prophet ﷺ arrived with Abū Bakr and ʿUmar. We began harvesting, while the dates were measured for the creditor from beneath the palm trees, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ supplicated for blessing.
We eventually paid him everything he was owed from the smaller of the two gardens alone—as ʿAmmār understood it.
Then we brought the Prophet ﷺ and those with him fresh dates and water. They ate and drank.
Then he said:
“This is among the blessings about which you will be questioned.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (3639), Aḥmad (15206), whose wording is given here, Abū Yaʿlā (2161), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (3411); all through Ḥammād ibn Salamah, from ʿAmmār ibn Abī ʿAmmār, from Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh. Its isnād is authentic.

13770
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out one day—or one night—and encountered Abū Bakr and ʿUmar.
He asked:
“What has brought you out of your homes at this hour?”
They replied:
“Hunger, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“By the One in whose hand is my soul, the same thing that brought you out brought me out as well. Get up.”
So they went with him to the home of a man from the Anṣār, but the man was not at home.
When his wife saw the Prophet ﷺ, she said:
“Welcome! You are most welcome.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Where is so-and-so?”
She replied:
“He has gone to fetch us some fresh water.”
Then the Anṣārī man arrived. When he saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his two Companions, he said:
“All praise belongs to Allah! No one today has more honored guests than I do.”
He went and brought them a cluster of dates containing unripe dates, ripe dates, and fresh dates, and said:
“Eat from this.”
Then he took a knife.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do not slaughter a milk-producing animal.”
He slaughtered an animal for them, and they ate from the meat and from the dates and drank water.
When they had eaten their fill and quenched their thirst, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to Abū Bakr and ʿUmar:
“By the One in whose hand is my soul, you will surely be questioned about this blessing on the Day of Resurrection. Hunger drove you from your homes, and you did not return until you had received this blessing.”
In another narration:
“Do not slaughter an animal that gives milk.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2038:140).

13771
Abū ʿUsayb narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out one night and passed by me. He called me, so I went with him.
Then he passed by Abū Bakr and called him, and he joined us.
Then he passed by ʿUmar and called him, and he joined us.
They continued until they entered a garden belonging to one of the Anṣār.
The Prophet ﷺ said to its owner:
“Feed us some unripe dates.”
The man brought a cluster and placed it before them. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ and his Companions ate from it.
Then he called for cold water and drank.
He said:
“You will surely be questioned about this on the Day of Resurrection.”
ʿUmar took the date cluster and struck it against the ground until the unripe dates scattered in front of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Then he asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, will we really be questioned about this on the Day of Resurrection?”
He replied:
“Yes, except for three things: a piece of cloth with which a man covers his nakedness, a morsel with which he satisfies his hunger, or a shelter into which he enters to protect himself from heat and cold.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (20678) through Surayj ibn al-Nuʿmān, from Ḥashraj, from Abū Nuṣayrah—whose name was Muslim ibn ʿUbayd—from Abū ʿAsīb, the freedman of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
The hadith contains an indication that ordinary human life cannot properly continue without three basic necessities: clothing, food, and shelter.

13772
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
Abū Bakr went out to the mosque during the intense midday heat.
When ʿUmar heard of this, he came and asked:
“O Abū Bakr, what brought you out at this hour?”
He replied:
“Nothing brought me out except the severe hunger I am experiencing.”
ʿUmar said:
“By Allah, nothing brought me out except the same.”
While they were together, the Prophet ﷺ came out to them and asked:
“What brought you both out at this hour?”
They replied:
“By Allah, nothing brought us out except the severe hunger we feel in our stomachs.”
He said:
“By the One in whose hand is my soul, nothing brought me out except the same. Get up.”
They went together until they reached the door of Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī.
Abū Ayyūb used to save food or milk for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. That day the Prophet ﷺ had been delayed and did not arrive at his usual time, so Abū Ayyūb gave it to his family and went to work among his palm trees.
When they reached the door, his wife came out and said:
“Welcome to the Prophet of Allah ﷺ and those with him!”
The Prophet of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Where is Abū Ayyūb?”
Abū Ayyūb heard him while working among his palm trees and came running.
He said:
“Welcome to the Prophet of Allah ﷺ and those with him. O Prophet of Allah, this is not the time at which you usually come.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“You are right.”
Abū Ayyūb went and cut down a cluster containing dates at different stages—ripe dates, fresh dates, and unripe dates.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Why did you cut down the whole cluster? Why did you not simply pick some ripe dates for us?”
Abū Ayyūb replied:
“O Prophet of Allah, I wanted you to eat from its ripe dates, fresh dates, and unripe dates, and I will also slaughter something for you.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“If you slaughter, do not slaughter a milk-producing animal.”
So Abū Ayyūb took a young female goat or young goat and slaughtered it.
He told his wife:
“Knead dough and bake bread for us, for you know bread-making better.”
He cooked some of the goat and roasted half of it.
When the food was ready, he placed it before the Prophet ﷺ and his Companions.
The Prophet ﷺ took some meat from the goat, put it into a piece of bread, and said:
“O Abū Ayyūb, take this to Fāṭimah, for she has not had food like this for several days.”
Abū Ayyūb took it to Fāṭimah.
When they had eaten and become full, the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Bread, meat, ripe dates, unripe dates, and fresh dates.”
His eyes filled with tears, and he said:
“By the One in whose hand is my soul, this is indeed the blessing about which you will be questioned. Allah, Majestic and Exalted, says: ‘Then, on that Day, you will surely be questioned about the blessings.’ This is the blessing about which you will be questioned on the Day of Resurrection.”
This weighed heavily upon his Companions.
So he said:
“Rather, whenever you receive something like this and stretch out your hands toward it, say: ‘In the Name of Allah.’ And when you have eaten your fill, say: ‘All praise belongs to Allah who satisfied us, bestowed blessings upon us, and favored us.’ This will suffice for it.”
When the Prophet ﷺ stood to leave, he told Abū Ayyūb:
“Come to us tomorrow.”
Whenever someone showed the Prophet ﷺ kindness, he liked to repay him.
Abū Ayyūb had not heard the Prophet’s instruction, so ʿUmar told him:
“The Prophet ﷺ has commanded you to come to him tomorrow.”
The next day Abū Ayyūb came to him, and the Prophet ﷺ gave him a young female slave and said:
“O Abū Ayyūb, treat her well, for we have seen nothing but good from her for as long as she has been with us.”
When Abū Ayyūb returned from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ with her, he said:
“I can find no better way to fulfill the instruction of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ concerning her than to set her free.”
So he freed her.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (5216) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Ṣaghīr (185). Some of its wording is unusual, and Ibn Ḥibbān himself acknowledged at the beginning of its chain that it is a rare report.

13773
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Indeed, Allah restrained the elephant from Makkah, and later gave His Messenger ﷺ and the believers authority over it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (112) and Muslim (1355:448).
Regarding:
“And He sent against them birds in flocks,”
abābīl is a collective noun that has no singular form derived from the same word.
It means groups or successive flocks.
Regarding:
“Pelting them with stones of sijjīl,”
sijjīl refers to something intensely hardened. It is stone whose original substance was clay.
This is like the words of Allah in Sūrat Hūd:
“And We rained upon it stones of layered baked clay.” [Sūrat Hūd 11:82]
That is, according to the author, these were not ordinary rocky stones, but hardened clay created specifically for their punishment.
He states that whenever one of these stones struck a person, smallpox appeared on him, while smallpox had not previously been known in Makkah.
An ordinary rock, he argues, would not cause the disease of smallpox.
Regarding:
“And made them like chewed-up straw,”
al-ʿaṣf is the leaf or stalk of crops.
When grazing animals enter crops, eat them, trample them beneath their feet, and leave them scattered upon the ground after they had been green and flourishing, this resembles the condition of the People of the Elephant—how they ended up fallen and scattered upon the earth after having been strong.
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“Al-ʿaṣf is the husk surrounding a grain, like the covering around a kernel of wheat.”

13774
Al-Zubayr narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah distinguished Quraysh with seven qualities: He favored them in that they worshipped Allah for ten years when none worshipped Him except a Qurashī; He favored them by granting them victory on the Day of the Elephant while they were still polytheists; He favored them in that a sūrah of the Qur’an was revealed specifically concerning them, with no one else included with them: ‘For the security of Quraysh’; and He favored them in that among them are prophethood, the caliphate, guardianship of the Kaʿbah, and providing water to the pilgrims.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (9169) through Muṣʿab—i.e., Ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Ḥamzah al-Zubayrī—from his father, from ʿAbdullāh ibn Muṣʿab ibn Thābit ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr, from Hishām ibn ʿUrwah, from his father, from al-Zubayr.
Regarding his words:
“He favored them in that they worshipped Allah for ten years when none worshipped Him except a Qurashī,”
this means the ten years from the beginning of the mission of the Prophet ﷺ until the Pledge of al-ʿAqabah that followed.
That is, most of those who entered Islam during this period were from the tribe of Quraysh.
Regarding:
“For the īlāf of Quraysh,”
this refers to their harmony, familiarity, and gathering together securely in their land.
The prepositional phrase is placed first to emphasize it.
Quraysh is, according to the majority, the title of one of the higher ancestors of the Prophet ﷺ: Fihr ibn Mālik ibn al-Naḍr ibn Kinānah.
Accordingly, the overall construction of the sūrah is:
Let Quraysh worship the Lord of this House, who fed them against hunger and made them safe from fear, because of the security He granted them in their winter and summer journeys.
Quraysh had two regular trading journeys:
One was to Yemen, which was their winter journey.
The other was to al-Shām, which was their summer journey.
That is, during these two journeys you did not fear destruction or attack because you were inhabitants of the Sacred City, and people respected you because of the reverence they held for it.
Therefore, it was incumbent upon you to worship Allah alone, thank Him, and associate no one with Him.

13775
Asmāʾ bint Yazīd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“‘For the security of Quraysh, their security in the journey of winter and summer.’ Woe to you, O Quraysh! Worship the Lord of this House, who fed you against hunger and made you safe from fear.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (27607), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (24/177–178), Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (24/647), and al-Ḥākim (2/256), all through routes from Shahr ibn Ḥawshab, from Asmāʾ bint Yazīd.

13776
Anas narrated:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“That is the prayer of the hypocrite: he sits watching the sun until, when it is between the two horns of Satan, he rises and pecks out four rakʿahs, remembering Allah in them only a little.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (622).
Allah says:
“Those who are heedless of their prayer,”
and He did not say:
“Those who are heedless within their prayer.”
ʿAṭāʾ ibn Dīnār said:
“All praise belongs to Allah who said, ‘They are heedless of their prayer,’ and did not say, ‘They are heedless within their prayer.’”
Regarding:
“Those who show off,”
this means that they perform deeds in order to be seen by people, rather than sincerely for Allah, Mighty and Majestic.
This is a general characteristic of the hypocrites, as Allah says in Sūrat al-Nisāʾ:
“When they stand for prayer, they stand lazily, showing themselves off to the people.” [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:142]
That is, when he does pray, he prays to show off; and if he misses the prayer, he feels no regret.
Regarding:
“And they withhold al-māʿūn,”
this includes withholding charity, zakāh, and items lent for temporary use, because al-māʿūn can refer to assisting others with one’s property.
The word al-māʿūn is also used for small, ordinary necessities such as water, salt, and fire.
This is another description of the hypocrites: they are so miserly in helping others that they withhold even water, salt, and fire.

13777
Anas narrated that when the Prophet ﷺ was taken up to heaven, he said:
“I came upon a river whose two banks were domes of hollow pearls. I asked, ‘What is this, O Jibrīl?’ He replied, ‘This is al-Kawthar.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4964).

13778
Anas narrated:
One day, while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was sitting among us, he briefly dozed off. Then he raised his head smiling.
We asked:
“What has made you smile, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“A sūrah was just revealed to me.”
Then he recited:
“Indeed, We have granted you al-Kawthar. So pray to your Lord and sacrifice. Indeed, the one who hates you—he is the one cut off.”
Then he asked:
“Do you know what al-Kawthar is?”
We replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“It is a river my Lord, Mighty and Majestic, has promised me. It contains abundant goodness. It is also a reservoir to which my Ummah will come on the Day of Resurrection. Its vessels are as numerous as the stars. Then a servant from among them will be pulled away. I will say, ‘My Lord, he is from my Ummah.’ He will say, ‘You do not know what he introduced after you.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (400).

13779
Abū ʿUbaydah narrated:
I asked ʿĀʾishah about the words of Allah:
“Indeed, We have granted you al-Kawthar.”
She replied:
“It is a river given to your Prophet ﷺ. Along its two banks are hollow pearls, and its drinking vessels are as numerous as the stars.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4965).

13780
Ibn ʿAbbās said regarding al-Kawthar:
“It is the abundant goodness that Allah granted him.”
Abū Bishr said:
I asked Saʿīd ibn Jubayr:
“But people say that it is a river in Paradise?”
Saʿīd replied:
“The river in Paradise is part of the abundant goodness that Allah granted him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4966).
Regarding:
“So pray to your Lord and sacrifice,”
this means: as gratitude to Allah who granted you al-Kawthar, pray to Him.
And “sacrifice” means slaughter sacrificial camels so that you and your family may eat from them and distribute portions among the poor and needy.
What is intended is the sacrificial animals offered on the Day of Sacrifice.
Some jurists inferred from this that the prayer intended here is the ʿĪd prayer, and the sacrifice refers to the uḍḥiyah.
Thus the sacrifice is performed after the prayer.
Regarding:
“Indeed, the one who hates you—he is the one cut off,”
al-abtar originally refers to something from which a part has been cut off, particularly an animal whose tail has been severed.
The expression was later used for a person who had sons and daughters, but whose sons died while his daughters remained.
Regarding:
“The one who hates you,”
shāniʾ is derived from shanāʾah, meaning hatred and enmity.
Thus, whoever hates you, O Muḥammad, and hates the guidance, truth, and clear proof you have brought, is the one who is truly cut off—humiliated and deprived of lasting honorable mention.
It was revealed concerning a group of the disbelievers of Makkah who said regarding the Prophet ﷺ:
“Leave him. He is a man who is cut off and has no surviving male offspring. When he dies, his mention will come to an end.”
Allah, the Exalted, answered them with His words:
“Indeed, the one who hates you—he is the one cut off.”
And matters came to pass exactly as Allah said.
The remembrance of Abū Lahab, Abū Jahl, and other leading men of Quraysh was cut off in disgrace, while Allah preserved the honorable mention of the Prophet ﷺ in heaven:
“Indeed, Allah and His angels send blessings upon the Prophet.”
And He preserved his mention upon the earth:
“O you who believe, send blessings upon him and greet him with complete peace.”
Thus the name of the Prophet ﷺ is proclaimed openly before mankind. Hardly a moment passes without his name being called aloud somewhere throughout the inhabited world:
“And I bear witness that Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah.”
Allah has also made his revealed law remain until the Day of Resurrection and final return.

13781
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When Kaʿb ibn al-Ashraf came to Makkah, Quraysh said to him:
“Are you the best of the people of Madinah and their leader?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
They said:
“Do you not see this man cut off from his people? He claims that he is better than us, while we are the people who serve the pilgrims and the custodians of the Sacred House.”
Kaʿb replied:
“You are better than him.”
Then Allah revealed:
“Indeed, the one who hates you—he is the one cut off.”
And He also revealed:
“Have you not seen those who were given a portion of the Scripture? They believe in false objects of worship and false authorities…”
through His words:
“You will never find for him any helper.” [Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:51–52]
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11643), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2293), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6572).

13782
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recited:
“Say: O disbelievers”
and:
“Say: He is Allah, One”
in the two rakʿahs before Fajr.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (726).

13783
Ibn ʿUmar narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ recited in the two rakʿahs before Fajr and the two rakʿahs after Maghrib, on twenty-some occasions or ten-some occasions:
“Say: O disbelievers”
and:
“Say: He is Allah, One.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (4763).

13784
A man from among the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ narrated:
I was traveling with the Prophet ﷺ when he heard a man reciting:
“Say: O disbelievers”
until he completed the sūrah.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“This man has declared himself free from shirk.”
Then we continued traveling, and he heard another man reciting:
“Say: He is Allah, One.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“As for this man, he has been forgiven.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (16605, 16617), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (7974), and al-Dārimī in his Musnad (3469).

13785
Farwah ibn Nawfal al-Ashjaʿī narrated from his father:
The Prophet ﷺ entrusted to me the daughter of Umm Salamah and said:
“You are only acting as her foster caretaker.”
I remained as long as Allah willed, then I later came to the Prophet ﷺ.
He asked:
“What happened to the girl—or the little girl?”
I replied:
“She is with her mother.”
He asked:
“Then what has brought you here?”
I said:
“Teach me something to say when I go to sleep.”
He said:
“When you go to bed, recite: ‘Say: O disbelievers.’ Then sleep after completing it, for it is a declaration of freedom from shirk.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (23807) and al-Tirmidhī (3403 m), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (1/565), through routes from Isrāʾīl, from Abū Isḥāq, from Farwah ibn Nawfal al-Ashjaʿī, from his father. The wording is that of Aḥmad and al-Ḥākim, while al-Tirmidhī referred to the preceding hadith. It was also narrated by Abū Dāwūd (5055), authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (790), and al-Ḥākim (2/538), through routes from Zuhayr ibn Muʿāwiyah, from Abū Isḥāq, with similar wording. Its isnād is good because of Farwah ibn Nawfal, whose hadith is good; discussion of it is given in detail in the Book of Adhkār.
It is said concerning the reason for the revelation of this sūrah that the disbelievers of Makkah proposed to the Prophet ﷺ that he worship their idols for one year and they would worship his Lord for one year.
So Allah revealed this sūrah.
Allah commanded His Messenger to completely disassociate himself from their religion.
Thus He said:
“I do not worship what you worship, nor are you worshippers of what I worship,”
concerning the present and past state,
and:
“Nor will I be a worshipper of what you have worshipped, nor will you be worshippers of what I worship,”
concerning the future.
Therefore Allah said:
“For you is your religion, and for me is my religion.”
This describes the present state, and upon this separation they would continue—except for one whose heart Allah opens to Islam, who then embraces Islam, worships what the Prophet ﷺ worships, and abandons their idols.

13786
ʿAmr ibn Salamah narrated in a lengthy hadith:
“The Arabs were waiting for the Conquest before embracing Islam. They would say: ‘Leave him and his people. If he prevails over them, then he is a truthful Prophet.’ When the Conquest took place, every tribe hastened to embrace Islam, and my father hastened on behalf of my people to embrace Islam.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4302).

13787
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
After the verse:
“When the help of Allah comes and the conquest”
was revealed to the Prophet ﷺ, he did not perform a prayer except that he would say in it:
“Glory be to You, our Lord, and all praise is Yours. O Allah, forgive me.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4967) and Muslim (484:219). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī, while Muslim’s wording is similar.

13788
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ frequently used to say in his bowing and prostration:
“Glory be to You, O Allah, our Lord, and all praise is Yours. O Allah, forgive me.”
He was putting the Qur’an into practice.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4968) and Muslim (484).

13789
ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ frequently used to say:
“Glory and praise be to Allah. I seek Allah’s forgiveness and repent to Him.”
She said:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, I see that you frequently say, ‘Glory and praise be to Allah. I seek Allah’s forgiveness and repent to Him.’”
He replied:
“My Lord informed me that I would see a sign in my Ummah, and when I saw it I was to frequently say: ‘Glory and praise be to Allah. I seek Allah’s forgiveness and repent to Him.’ I have now seen it: ‘When the help of Allah comes and the conquest’—the Conquest of Makkah—‘and you see the people entering the religion of Allah in crowds, then glorify your Lord with praise and seek His forgiveness. Indeed, He is ever accepting of repentance.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (484:220).

13790
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
ʿUmar used to admit me into the gatherings of the senior veterans of Badr.
Some of them said:
“Why do you bring this young man in with us when we have sons of his age?”
ʿUmar replied:
“He is someone whose standing you already know.”
One day, ʿUmar summoned them and also summoned me.
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
I understood that on that day he had only invited me in order to demonstrate something about me to them.
ʿUmar asked:
“What do you say concerning: ‘When the help of Allah comes and the conquest, and you see the people entering the religion of Allah in crowds…’?”
He recited the sūrah to its end.
Some of them said:
“We were commanded to praise Allah and seek His forgiveness when He grants us victory and conquest.”
Others said:
“We do not know.”
Or some of them said nothing.
ʿUmar then asked me:
“O Ibn ʿAbbās, is that what you say?”
I replied:
“No.”
He asked:
“Then what do you say?”
I replied:
“It refers to the approaching death of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, which Allah informed him of. ‘When the help of Allah comes and the conquest’—the Conquest of Makkah—then that is the sign that your appointed term is near. ‘Then glorify your Lord with praise and seek His forgiveness. Indeed, He is ever accepting of repentance.’”
ʿUmar said:
“I know nothing from it except what you know.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4294).

13791
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When:
“When the help of Allah comes and the conquest”
was revealed, the Prophet ﷺ understood that his own death had been announced to him.
That is, the entire sūrah:
“When the help of Allah comes…”
was revealed as an indication of this.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (3201) and Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (24/709).

13792
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When:
“When the help of Allah comes and the conquest”
through the end of the sūrah was revealed, the death of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was announced to him.
From that time, he became more intense than ever in striving for the affairs of the Hereafter.
Afterward, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The conquest has come, the help of Allah has come, and the people of Yemen have come.”
A man asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, what are the people of Yemen like?”
He replied:
“They are a people whose hearts are tender and soft. Faith is Yemeni, wisdom is Yemeni, and understanding of religion is Yemeni.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11648) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (11/328).

13793
ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUtbah narrated:
Ibn ʿAbbās asked me:
“Do you know the last sūrah of the Qur’an to be revealed in its entirety?”
I replied:
“Yes: ‘When the help of Allah comes and the conquest.’”
He said:
“You are correct.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3024).
Regarding:
“Then glorify your Lord with praise and seek His forgiveness. Indeed, He is ever accepting of repentance,”
the author states that this contains encouragement for everyone who recognizes that his death is approaching, whether through illness or old age, to increase in seeking forgiveness.
For this reason, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ put the Qur’an into practice and frequently said in his bowing and prostration:
“Glory be to You, O Allah, our Lord, and all praise is Yours. O Allah, forgive me,”
as ʿĀʾishah, may Allah be pleased with her, narrated.
Regarding:
“Indeed, He is ever Tawwāb,”
Tawwāb is an intensive form indicating that Allah repeatedly accepts the repentance of His servants.

13794
Abū al-Zinād ʿAbdullāh ibn Dhakwān narrated:
A man named Rabīʿah ibn ʿAbbād from Banū al-Dīl, who had lived during the Jāhiliyyah, informed me:
I saw the Prophet ﷺ during the Jāhiliyyah in the marketplace of Dhū al-Majāz saying:
“O people, say: ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,’ and you will succeed.”
The people were gathered around him.
Behind him was a bright-faced man with a squint and two braids, saying:
“He is a Ṣābiʾ and a liar.”
He followed him wherever he went.
I asked who that man was, and they told me the lineage of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“This is his uncle, Abū Lahab.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (19004) and al-Ḥākim (1/15).

13795
Ibn ʿAbbās narrated:
When the verse:
“And warn your closest relatives” [Sūrat al-Shuʿarāʾ 26:214]
was revealed, together with the wording concerning the sincere members of his clan, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out until he climbed al-Ṣafā and cried out:
“O people, beware of the morning attack!”
They asked:
“Who is calling out?”
They said:
“Muḥammad.”
So they gathered around him.
He called:
“O Banū so-and-so! O Banū so-and-so! O Banū so-and-so! O Banū ʿAbd Manāf! O Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib!”
They gathered before him.
He said:
“If I were to tell you that cavalry were about to emerge from the foot of this mountain, would you believe me?”
They replied:
“We have never known you to lie.”
He said:
“Then I am a warner to you before a severe punishment.”
Abū Lahab said:
“May you perish! Is this all you gathered us for?”
Then he got up and left.
So this sūrah was revealed:
“May the hands of Abū Lahab perish, and perish he did…”
Al-Aʿmash recited it in this form, through the end of the sūrah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4971) and Muslim (208). The wording is that of Muslim, while al-Bukhārī’s wording is similar.
Regarding:
“May the hands of Abū Lahab perish, and perish he did,”
the first phrase is a supplication against him, meaning:
“May he suffer loss and destruction.”
The second is a statement about him:
“And he did perish,”
meaning that his loss and destruction were certainly realized.
The attribution of destruction specifically to his two hands is connected to a report that Abū Lahab once picked up a stone with his hand intending to throw it at the Prophet ﷺ.
Regarding:
“Neither his wealth nor what he earned will benefit him,”
among the Arabs, the word wealth was often used particularly for camels and sheep.
Regarding:
“and what he earned,”
this means what he accumulated of money, gold, silver, weapons, and other possessions.
It is also narrated from ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās and others that:
“What he earned”
means his children, because a child is regarded as being from the earnings of his father.
It is narrated from Ibn Masʿūd that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ called his people to faith, Abū Lahab said:
“If what my nephew says is true, then on the Day of Resurrection I will ransom myself from punishment with my wealth and my children.”
So Allah, the Exalted, revealed:
“Neither his wealth nor what he earned will benefit him.”
Regarding:
“He will burn in a flaming Fire,”
yaṣlā nāran means that he will be roasted and burned by it.
The Arabs say:
“He exposed him to the fire,”
when he roasted him in it.
And:
“a Fire of flame”
means a Fire containing sparks, blazing flame, and severe burning.
The particle indicating futurity here also conveys certainty of fulfillment, like the words of Allah:
“He said, ‘I will seek forgiveness for you from my Lord.’” [Sūrat Yūsuf 12:98]
This verse was revealed while Abū Lahab was still alive, yet he later died as a disbeliever.
Thus the verse announced beforehand that he would not embrace Islam and would not escape the blazing Fire.
The author therefore counts this among the proofs of the Prophethood of Muḥammad ﷺ.
Regarding:
“And his wife, the carrier of firewood,”
she was his wife, Arwā bint Ḥarb ibn Umayyah, the sister of Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb.
She assisted her husband in his disbelief, denial, and stubborn opposition.
Thus, on the Day of Resurrection, she will assist in increasing his punishment in the Fire of Hell by carrying firewood and throwing it upon him, adding to his torment.
It has also been said that she used to place thorns in the path of the Prophet ﷺ between his house and the Kaʿbah.
The phrase:
“And his wife”
is grammatically coordinated with the hidden pronoun in:
“He will burn,”
meaning:
“And his wife too will burn in the Fire.”
Regarding:
“Around her neck is a rope of masad,”
this is a second description of this wicked woman.
That is, a rope will be placed around her neck by which she carries firewood in Hell in order to intensify the Fire upon her husband.
Al-jīd means the neck, especially the neck of a woman where a necklace is worn, often used in descriptions of beauty.
This woman, however, is described with these humiliating characteristics as recompense for what she did in this world.
This woman also died upon disbelief and shirk.
Thus, according to the author, the prophetic indication contained in the sūrah was fulfilled concerning her just as it was fulfilled concerning her husband: both died as disbelievers and will enter the Fire.
This woman was also foul-mouthed and abusive, as stated in the following report:

13796
Asmāʾ bint Abī Bakr narrated:
When:
“May the hands of Abū Lahab perish”
was revealed, the one-eyed Umm Jamīl bint Ḥarb came crying out loudly, carrying a stone pestle in her hand and saying:
“We reject Mudhammam, we detest his religion, and we disobey his command.”
The Prophet ﷺ was sitting in the mosque with Abū Bakr, reciting Qur’an.
When Abū Bakr saw her approaching, he said:
“O Messenger of Allah, she is coming, and I fear that she may see you.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“She will not see me.”
He recited Qur’an and sought protection through it, as Allah says:
“When you recite the Qur’an, We place between you and those who do not believe in the Hereafter a concealed barrier.” [Sūrat al-Isrāʾ 17:45]
She approached until she stood before Abū Bakr, yet she did not see the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
She said:
“O Abū Bakr, I have been informed that your companion has satirized me.”
He replied:
“No, by the Lord of this House, he has not satirized you.”
She turned away saying:
“Quraysh knows that I am the daughter of its leader.”
Al-Walīd said in his narration—or someone else said—that Umm Jamīl once stumbled in her cloak while circumambulating the House and said:
“May Mudhammam perish!”
Umm Ḥakīm bint ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib replied with words meaning:
“I am a chaste woman and will not answer with obscenity; I am sharp-witted and know what I say. Both of us are daughters of cousins, and Quraysh knows best.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥumaydī (323), and from him by al-Ḥākim (2/361).
A similar report is narrated from Ibn ʿAbbās:
When:
“May the hands of Abū Lahab perish, and perish he did”
was revealed, the wife of Abū Lahab came toward the Prophet ﷺ while Abū Bakr was with him.
When Abū Bakr saw her, he said:
“O Messenger of Allah, she is a foul-mouthed woman, and I fear that she may harm you. Perhaps you should stand up and leave.”
He replied:
“She will not see me.”
She came and said:
“O Abū Bakr, your companion has satirized me.”
He replied:
“He does not compose poetry.”
She said:
“I believe you.”
Then she left.
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
I asked:
“O Messenger of Allah, did she not see you?”
He replied:
“An angel continued shielding me from her with his wings.”
Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (25, 2358), and from him by Ibn Ḥibbān (6511), and by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2294).
Its chain is weak because of ʿAṭāʾ ibn al-Sāʾib. His memory became confused later in life, and ʿAbd al-Salām ibn Ḥarb narrated from him after this confusion had occurred.
Al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar explicitly stated in Hady al-Sārī:
“Whoever narrated from him after his memory became confused, his hadith is weak.”
Nevertheless, Ibn Ḥajar graded it good in Fatḥ al-Bārī (8/738), perhaps because of supporting evidence.
It also has another supporting report from Zayd ibn Arqam recorded by al-Ḥākim (2/526), although it contains a defect that al-Ḥākim himself pointed out.
The author considers this sūrah one of the great miraculous signs of the Noble Qur’an.
Allah revealed it while Abū Lahab and his wife were both still alive. Allah, the Exalted, declared that they would enter the Fire and be punished therein, which meant that they would never embrace Islam.
Events then occurred exactly as Allah, the Knower of the unseen and the seen, had foretold.

13797
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb narrated:
The polytheists said:
“O Muḥammad, describe to us the lineage of your Lord.”
So Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed:
“Say: He is Allah, One. Allah, the Self-Sufficient Master.”
He said:
“Al-Ṣamad is the One who neither begets nor is born, and there is none comparable to Him. For anything that is born will eventually die, and anything that dies will leave inheritance, whereas Allah does not die and is not inherited.”
Regarding:
“He neither begets nor is born,”
he said:
“He has no likeness and no equal, and there is nothing like Him.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3364), Aḥmad (21219), and al-Ḥākim (2/540), all through Abū Jaʿfar al-Rāzī, from al-Rabīʿ ibn Anas, from Abū al-ʿĀliyah, from Ubayy ibn Kaʿb. The wording is that of al-Ḥākim, who said: “Its isnād is authentic.”

13798
ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated:
The Prophet ﷺ sent a man as commander of a military expedition. When he led his companions in prayer, he would conclude his recitation with:
“Say: He is Allah, One.”
When they returned, they mentioned this to the Prophet ﷺ.
He said:
“Ask him why he does that.”
They asked him, and he replied:
“Because it describes the Most Merciful, and I love to recite it.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Tell him that Allah loves him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in Kitāb al-Tawḥīd (7375) and Muslim in Ṣalāt al-Musāfirīn (813), both through ʿAbdullāh ibn Wahb, from ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith, from Saʿīd ibn Abī Hilāl, who reported from Abū al-Rijāl Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from his mother ʿAmrah bint ʿAbd al-Raḥmān—who was under the guardianship of ʿĀʾishah—from ʿĀʾishah.

13799
Abū Hurayrah narrated:
I was coming with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when he heard a man reciting:
“Say: He is Allah, One.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“It has become obligatory.”
I asked:
“What has become obligatory, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“Paradise.”
Abū Hurayrah said:
I wanted to go to the man and give him the glad tidings, but I feared that I would miss lunch with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, so I preferred to have lunch with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Then I went to the man, but found that he had already left.
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik in Kitāb al-Qurʾān (18), from ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from ʿUbayd ibn Ḥunayn, the freedman of the family of Zayd ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, who said that he heard Abū Hurayrah narrate it.

13800
Anas ibn Mālik narrated:
A man from the Anṣār used to lead the people in prayer at the mosque of Qubāʾ.
Whenever he began a sūrah in the prayer, he would first recite:
“Say: He is Allah, One”
until he completed it, and then he would recite another sūrah along with it.
He did this in every rakʿah.
His companions spoke to him and said:
“You recite this sūrah, yet you do not consider it sufficient until you recite another sūrah with it. Either recite this one alone, or leave it and recite another sūrah.”
He replied:
“I will not abandon it. If you want me to continue leading you while doing this, I will do so. If you dislike it, I will stop leading you.”
They considered him the best among them and disliked having anyone else lead them.
When the Prophet ﷺ came to them, they informed him of what had happened.
He said:
“O so-and-so, what prevents you from doing what your companions ask of you, and what makes you recite this sūrah in every rakʿah?”
The man replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, I love it.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Your love for it has admitted you into Paradise.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2901), from Muḥammad ibn Ismāʿīl al-Bukhārī, from Ismāʿīl ibn Abī Uways, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Muḥammad, from ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿUmar, from Thābit al-Bunānī, from Anas ibn Mālik.

13801
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated:
A man heard another man repeatedly reciting:
“Say: He is Allah, One.”
When morning came, he went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and mentioned this to him, apparently considering the sūrah too little.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By the One in whose hand is my soul, it is equivalent to one-third of the Qur’an.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in Faḍāʾil al-Qurʾān (5013), from ʿAbdullāh ibn Yūsuf, from Mālik, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Ṣaʿṣaʿah, from his father, from Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī.

13802
Qatādah ibn al-Nuʿmān narrated:
During the time of the Prophet ﷺ, a man stood in prayer in the pre-dawn hours reciting:
“Say: He is Allah, One. Allah, the Self-Sufficient Master. He neither begets nor is born. And there is none comparable to Him,”
repeating it without adding anything else.
When morning came, another man went to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, so-and-so stood in prayer last night during the pre-dawn hours and recited Sūrat al-Ikhlāṣ—‘Say: He is Allah, One. Allah, the Self-Sufficient Master. He neither begets nor is born. And there is none comparable to Him’—repeatedly without adding anything to it.”
It was as though the man considered this too little.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“By the One in whose hand is my soul, it is equivalent to one-third of the Qur’an.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (1548), from Abū Maʿmar al-Hudhalī Ismāʿīl ibn Ibrāhīm, from Ismāʿīl ibn Jaʿfar, from Mālik ibn Anas, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿah, from his father, from Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī, who said: “My brother Qatādah ibn al-Nuʿmān informed me,” and then narrated it.

13803
Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said to his Companions:
“Is any one of you unable to recite one-third of the Qur’an in a single night?”
This seemed difficult for them, and they said:
“Which of us is capable of doing that, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“‘Allah, the One, the Self-Sufficient Master’ is one-third of the Qur’an.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in Faḍāʾil al-Qurʾān (5015), from ʿUmar ibn Ḥafṣ, from his father, from al-Aʿmash, from al-Ḍaḥḥāk al-Mashriqī, from Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī.

13804
Abū al-Dardāʾ narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Is any one of you unable to recite one-third of the Qur’an during a night?”
They asked:
“How could one recite one-third of the Qur’an?”
He replied:
“‘Say: He is Allah, One’ is equivalent to one-third of the Qur’an.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in Faḍāʾil al-Qurʾān (811:259), through routes from Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd, from Shuʿbah, from Qatādah, from Sālim ibn Abī al-Jaʿd, from Maʿdān ibn Abī Ṭalḥah, from Abū al-Dardāʾ.
Muslim also narrated it through another route from Qatādah with the same chain (811:260), with the wording:
“Allah divided the Qur’an into three parts, and He made ‘Say: He is Allah, One’ one of the parts of the Qur’an.”

13805
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Gather together, for I am going to recite one-third of the Qur’an to you.”
Those who gathered, gathered.
Then the Prophet of Allah ﷺ came out and recited:
“Say: He is Allah, One.”
Then he went back inside.
Some of us said to one another:
“I think some news has come to him from heaven, and that is why he went inside.”
Then the Prophet of Allah ﷺ came out and said:
“I told you that I would recite one-third of the Qur’an to you. Indeed, it is equivalent to one-third of the Qur’an.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in Faḍāʾil al-Qurʾān (812:261), through routes from Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd, from Yazīd ibn Kaysān, from Abū Ḥāzim, from Abū Hurayrah.
Regarding the word:
“Gather together,”
it means: assemble.
Al-ḥashd means a gathered group of people.

13806
Abū Masʿūd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Would it be too difficult for any one of you to recite one-third of the Qur’an every night?”
We said:
“Who is capable of that, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“‘Say: He is Allah, One’ is one-third of the Qur’an.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭayālisī (651), Ibn Mājah (3789), and Aḥmad (17106), all through Abū Qays, from ʿAmr ibn Maymūn, from Abū Masʿūd.

13807
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Is any one of you unable to recite one-third of the Qur’an every night?”
They asked:
“Who can manage that, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“‘Say: He is Allah, One.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (10511), al-Bazzār (1866), and Ibn Ḥibbān (2576), all through Shuʿbah, from ʿAlī ibn Mudrik, from Ibrāhīm al-Nakhaʿī, from al-Rabīʿ ibn Khuthaym, from Ibn Masʿūd. Its isnād is authentic.

13808
Anas narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“‘Say: He is Allah, One’ is equivalent to one-third of the Qur’an.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (3788), from al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn al-Khallāl, from Yazīd ibn Hārūn, from Jarīr ibn Ḥāzim, from Qatādah, from Anas. Its isnād is authentic.

13809
Ubayy ibn Kaʿb—or a man from the Anṣār—narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever recites ‘Say: He is Allah, One,’ it is as though he has recited one-third of the Qur’an.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (21275) and Abū ʿUbayd in Faḍāʾil al-Qurʾān (p. 268), both from Hushaym, from Ḥuṣayn, from Hilāl ibn Yasāf, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Laylā, from Ubayy ibn Kaʿb—or from a man of the Anṣār. Its isnād is authentic.
The wording:
“Or a man from the Anṣār”
does not harm the report because that man from the Anṣār would also have been a Companion.
Moreover, the report has come through other routes directly from Ubayy ibn Kaʿb without uncertainty, and this is more likely because Ubayy ibn Kaʿb was among the foremost Qur’an reciters of the Companions.
The meaning of the statement that reciting:
“Say: He is Allah, One”
is equivalent to one-third of the Qur’an is that the Qur’an contains three principal subjects: Tawḥīd, the Message, and the Day of Resurrection together with its details.
Sūrat al-Ikhlāṣ is entirely devoted to explaining Tawḥīd in the most beautiful expressions and briefest words.
Thus, whoever recites it has, as it were, grasped the meaning of Tawḥīd, which is one of these three great subjects, and receives a reward like that of reciting one-third of the Qur’an.
However, this does not mean that such a person is equal to someone who spends the entire night reciting the Qur’an, because the rewards of Qur’an recitation have differing degrees with Allah, the Exalted.
The scholars differed greatly in explaining and interpreting this hadith, to an extent difficult to enumerate.
Ibn ʿAbd al-Barr even said:
“Remaining silent concerning this issue is better than speaking about it.”

13810
Muʿādh ibn Anas al-Juhanī, a Companion of the Prophet ﷺ, narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever recites ‘Say: He is Allah, One’ completely ten times, Allah will build for him a palace in Paradise.”
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said:
“Then we will increase greatly in doing so, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Allah has even more, and what He has is better.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (15610): Ḥasan narrated from Ibn Lahīʿah; and Yaḥyā ibn Ghaylān narrated from Rushdīn; both narrated from Zabbān ibn Fāʾid al-Ḥamrāwī, from Sahl ibn Muʿādh ibn Anas al-Juhanī, from his father Muʿādh ibn Anas al-Juhanī. Ibn Lahīʿah has been criticized, but Rushdīn ibn Saʿd corroborated him in this chain. Their teacher Zabbān ibn Fāʾid al-Baṣrī al-Ḥamrāwī was disputed: Aḥmad ibn Ḥanbal said, “His hadiths are objectionable”; Ibn Maʿīn said, “A weak shaykh”; and Abū Ḥātim said, “Ṣāliḥ.”
Al-Dārimī (3472) also narrated it as a mursal report from Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib, with the wording:
“Whoever recites ‘Say: He is Allah, One’ ten times will have a palace built for him in Paradise. Whoever recites it twenty times will have two palaces built for him in Paradise. Whoever recites it thirty times will have three palaces built for him in Paradise.”
ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said:
“By Allah, O Messenger of Allah, then our palaces will become very numerous!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Allah’s bounty is more expansive than that.”
Through these two chains, the hadith rises to the level of Good, except for the additional wording:
“Whoever recites it twenty times will have two palaces built for him, and whoever recites it thirty times will have three palaces built for him.”
Perhaps one of the narrators calculated this by analogy with the reward for ten recitations and then mistakenly attributed it directly to the Prophet ﷺ.

13811
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated:
I met the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he said to me:
“O ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir, shall I teach you sūrahs the like of which were not revealed in the Torah, the Psalms, the Gospel, or the Qur’an? Do not let a single night pass without reciting them: ‘Say: He is Allah, One,’ ‘Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of daybreak,’ and ‘Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of mankind.’”
ʿUqbah said:
“Thereafter, no night passed over me except that I recited them. It was only right that I should never abandon them, since the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had commanded me to recite them.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17542), from Ḥusayn ibn Muḥammad, from Ibn ʿAyyāsh—Ismāʿīl—from Usayd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Khathʿamī al-Ramlī, from Farwah ibn Mujāhid al-Lakhmī, from ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir.
Regarding the words:
“Allah, al-Ṣamad,”
this is a construction of restriction, indicating that al-Ṣamadiyyah belongs to Allah alone.
He is the Master to whom all creatures turn. Every created being turns to Him and cannot do without Him, while He is completely free of need from them.
This also nullifies what the people of shirk practiced during the Jāhiliyyah when they called upon their idols to fulfill their needs and turned to them in times of calamity.
Al-Ṣamad is the Master to whom people turn in all affairs.
It has also been said that al-Ṣamad is the One who has no hollow interior, and therefore does not eat or drink, neither begets nor is born, because whatever is born will eventually die.
This is stated in the authentic hadith:

13812
Abū Hurayrah narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Allah said: ‘The son of Ādam has denied Me, though he had no right to do so, and he has insulted Me, though he had no right to do so. As for his denial of Me, it is his saying: “He will not recreate me as He created me the first time,” although the first creation is no easier for Me than recreating him. As for his insulting Me, it is his saying: “Allah has taken a son,” while I am the One, al-Ṣamad. I neither beget nor am born, and there is none comparable to Me.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4974).
Regarding the words:
“He neither begets nor is born,”
this means that He has no child, contrary to what the Jews and Christians claimed, nor does He have a father or a consort.
The disbelievers of Makkah also made such a claim.
Allah says:
“They say: ‘The Most Merciful has taken a son.’” [Sūrat Maryam 19:88]
And He says:
“They say: ‘The Most Merciful has taken a son.’ Glory be to Him! Rather, they are honored servants.” [Sūrat al-Anbiyāʾ 21:26]
This is also stated in the authentic hadith:

13813
Abū Mūsā narrated that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“No one—or nothing—is more patient with offensive things he hears than Allah. They attribute a child to Him, yet He continues to grant them well-being and provide for them.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Adab (6099) and Muslim in Ṣifat al-Qiyāmah (2804), both through al-Aʿmash, from Saʿīd ibn Jubayr, from Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Sulamī, from Abū Mūsā. The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
Regarding the words:
“And there is none comparable to Him,”
al-kufʾ means one who is equal or similar in attributes.
This also refutes the claim of the polytheists that the angels are the daughters of Allah, the claim of the Jews that ʿUzayr is the son of Allah, and the claim of the Christians that the Messiah is the son of Allah.
Allah, the Exalted, declared their claims false and denied that birth or likeness can be attributed to Him.

13814
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ became ill, he would recite the Muʿawwidhāt over himself and blow lightly.
When his illness became severe, I would recite over him and wipe him with his own hand, hoping for its blessing.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in Faḍāʾil al-Qurʾān (5016) and Muslim in al-Salām (2192:51), both through Mālik, from Ibn Shihāb, from ʿUrwah, from ʿĀʾishah.

13815
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
Every night when the Prophet ﷺ went to bed, he would bring his palms together, blow lightly into them, and recite into them:
“Say: He is Allah, One,”
 “Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of daybreak,”
 and “Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of mankind.”
Then he would wipe with his hands as much of his body as he could, beginning with his head, face, and the front of his body.
He would do this three times.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in Faḍāʾil al-Qurʾān (5017), from Qutaybah ibn Saʿīd, from al-Mufaḍḍal, from ʿUqayl, from Ibn Shihāb, from ʿUrwah, from ʿĀʾishah.

13816
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Have you not seen verses that were revealed tonight, the like of which has never been seen? ‘Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of daybreak,’ and ‘Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of mankind.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in Ṣalāt al-Musāfirīn (814:264), from Qutaybah ibn Saʿīd, from Jarīr, from Bayān, from Qays ibn Abī Ḥāzim, from ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir.

13817
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated:
I was walking with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when he said:
“O ʿUqbah, recite.”
I asked:
“What should I recite, O Messenger of Allah?”
He remained silent.
Then he said again:
“O ʿUqbah, recite.”
I asked:
“What should I recite, O Messenger of Allah?”
He again remained silent.
So I said:
“O Allah, cause him to answer me.”
He then said:
“O ʿUqbah, recite.”
I asked:
“What should I recite, O Messenger of Allah?”
He said:
“‘Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of daybreak.’”
I recited it to the end.
Then he said:
“Recite.”
I asked:
“What should I recite, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“‘Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of mankind.’”
I recited it to the end.
Then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“No one has ever asked with anything like these two, nor has anyone seeking refuge ever sought refuge with anything like these two.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (5438), from Qutaybah, from al-Layth, from Ibn ʿAjlān, from Saʿīd al-Maqburī, from ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir.

13818
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated:
I took hold of the foot of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, teach me Sūrat Hūd and Sūrat Yūsuf.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me:
“O ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir, you will not recite a sūrah more beloved to Allah or more far-reaching in His sight than ‘Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of daybreak.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (17418), al-Ṭabarānī in al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (17/312), and al-Dārimī (3482), all through ʿAbdullāh ibn Yazīd al-Muqriʾ, from Ḥaywah and Ibn Lahīʿah, who both heard Yazīd ibn Abī Ḥabīb say: Abū ʿImrān narrated to me that he heard ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrate it.

13819
Yazīd ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Shikhkhīr narrated from a man of his people that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed by him and said:
“Recite these two in your prayer.”
Meaning the two Muʿawwidhatayn: Sūrat al-Falaq and Sūrat al-Nās.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (20744), from ʿAffān, from Shuʿbah, from al-Jurayrī, from Yazīd ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Shikhkhīr.

13820
Zirr narrated:
I asked Ubayy ibn Kaʿb:
“O Abū al-Mundhir, your brother Ibn Masʿūd says such-and-such.”
Ubayy replied:
“I asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ about it, and he said to me:
‘I was told to say it, so I said it.’
Therefore, we say exactly as the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in Faḍāʾil al-Qurʾān (4977), from ʿAlī ibn ʿAbdullāh, from Sufyān, from ʿAbdah ibn Abī Lubābah, from Zirr ibn Ḥubaysh.
The author says:
It is well known among many Qur’an reciters and jurists that Ibn Masʿūd did not write the two Muʿawwidhatayn in his personal muṣḥaf. It is even reported that he would erase them from a muṣḥaf and say:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was only commanded to seek refuge by means of them.”
The correct view is that held by the majority of the Companions, who memorized both sūrahs, wrote them in the official muṣḥafs, and sent those copies throughout the Muslim lands.
As for ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd, perhaps he did not hear directly from the Prophet ﷺ that these two sūrahs were part of the Qur’an, or perhaps he forgot it.
It is authentically established that the Prophet ﷺ recited both of them in prayer:

13821
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated:
While I was leading the mount of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ through one of the mountain passes, he said to me:
“O ʿUqbah, will you not ride?”
I felt too much reverence for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to ride his mount.
Then he again said:
“O ʿUqbah, will you not ride?”
I became concerned that refusing might amount to disobedience.
So the Messenger of Allah ﷺ dismounted and I rode for a short while. Then he mounted again and said:
“O ʿUqbah, shall I teach you two of the best sūrahs that people recite?”
I replied:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
He then taught me:
“Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of daybreak,”
and:
“Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of mankind.”
Then the prayer was established. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went forward and recited both of them in the prayer.
Afterward, when he passed by me, he said:
“What do you think now, O ʿUqbah? Recite them whenever you go to sleep and whenever you wake.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (5427) and Aḥmad (17296), and authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (534), all through al-Walīd ibn Muslim, from Ibn Jābir, from al-Qāsim Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir. The wording is that of Aḥmad, and its isnād is authentic. Ibn Jābir is ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Yazīd ibn Jābir al-Azdī, Abū ʿUtbah al-Shāmī; he is trustworthy, and Ibn Maʿīn, Ibn Saʿd, and others declared him trustworthy.

13822
ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir narrated:
I was leading the she-camel of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when he said to me:
“Shall I teach you two sūrahs, the like of which has never been recited?”
I replied:
“Yes.”
He taught me:
“Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of daybreak,”
and:
“Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of mankind.”
He did not see me showing great amazement at them.
When morning came, he recited both of them.
Then he said to me:
“What do you think now, O ʿUqbah?”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (1462), al-Nasāʾī (5436), Aḥmad (17350), and Ibn Khuzaymah (535), all through Muʿāwiyah ibn Ṣāliḥ, from al-ʿAlāʾ ibn al-Ḥārith, from al-Qāsim ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, the freedman of Muʿāwiyah ibn Abī Sufyān, from ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir.
These are some of the routes of transmission from ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir. I have omitted the rest to avoid excessive length.
From these reports, it is very clear that the two Muʿawwidhatayn are part of the Qur’an. This was the position of the majority of the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ and the Tābiʿīn.
Perhaps ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd did not hear this directly from the Prophet ﷺ, or, if he had heard it, he later forgot.
The author states that Ibn Masʿūd eventually returned to the official muṣḥaf prepared by order of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān and burned his own copy by order of the Commander of the Believers, as explained in the introduction to the commentary.
Regarding:
“Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of al-Falaq,”
al-Falaq means the dawn.
The basic meaning of falaq is the splitting or bursting forth of something from within another thing, as dawn breaks forth after the darkness of night.
The:
“Lord of al-Falaq”
is Allah, the Exalted, because He is the One who alternates night and day.
Regarding:
“From the evil of what He created,”
this means seeking refuge from the evil found among all created things, particularly from Iblīs and his offspring.
Regarding:
“And from the evil of darkness when it settles,”
al-ghāsiq is a description of the night when its darkness becomes intense.
The Arabs say:
“The night became dark,”
using the verb ghasaqa.
From this is the verse:
“Until the darkness of the night.” [Sūrat al-Isrāʾ 17:78]
Regarding:
“when it settles,”
waqaba means to disappear or enter into darkness.
Thus it is said:
“The sun waqabat,”
meaning that it set.
What is meant is the intensification of the darkness of night.
Seeking refuge is specifically mentioned at the darkest time of night because harmful creatures, frightening beings, and the offspring of devils spread more widely during it.

13823
ʿĀʾishah narrated:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ took me by the hand and showed me the moon as it rose.
He said:
“Seek refuge in Allah from the evil of this ghāsiq when it enters.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (24323), al-Tirmidhī (3366), al-Ḥākim (2/540), al-Bayhaqī in al-Daʿawāt (314), and Ibn Jarīr in his Tafsīr (24/748), all through Ibn Abī Dhiʾb, from al-Ḥārith, from Abū Salamah, from ʿĀʾishah. Its isnād is good because of al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Qurashī al-ʿĀmirī, the maternal uncle of Ibn Abī Dhiʾb; his hadith is good, although Ibn al-Madīnī declared him unknown, which is understood as due to the small number of his narrations.
Regarding:
“And from the evil of those who blow upon knots,”
al-nafth means blowing lightly.
Sorcerers do this when they place their magic into something, tie knots over it, and then blow upon those knots.
Al-naffāthāt refers to female sorcerers.
The author states that the wording of the noble verse suggests that women frequently practiced sorcery, because much of their sorcery was aimed at separating a man from his wife.
He further states that after completing household responsibilities, some women would turn toward sorcery for that purpose, and that female sorcerers were known in the society of the Jāhiliyyah.
Regarding:
“And from the evil of an envier when he envies,”
envy means wishing for another person’s blessing to disappear.
The author states that this occurs more frequently among men than women, because an envier may actively seek to destroy the causes of another person’s blessing.
It is stated in the authentic hadith:

13824
Abū Saʿīd narrated:
Jibrīl came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked:
“O Muḥammad, are you suffering from illness?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
Jibrīl said:
“In the Name of Allah, I perform ruqyah for you, from everything that harms you, from the evil of every soul or the eye of an envier. May Allah heal you. In the Name of Allah, I perform ruqyah for you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in al-Salām (2186), from Bishr ibn Hilāl al-Ṣawwāf, from ʿAbd al-Wārith, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Ṣuhayb, from Abū Naḍrah, from Abū Saʿīd.
The author states that this occurred when the Prophet ﷺ was affected by magic from envious Jewish sorcerers, after which Allah granted him well-being and healed him.
The verse, however, is general in seeking refuge from the evil of every envier when he acts upon his envy.

13825
ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrated that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There is none among you except that a companion from among the jinn has been assigned to him.”
They asked:
“Even you, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“Even me, except that Allah helped me against him, so he submitted, and he commands me only with good.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in Ṣifat al-Qiyāmah (2814), through routes from Jarīr, from Manṣūr, from Sālim ibn Abī al-Jaʿd, from his father, from ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd.

13826
ʿĀṣim narrated:
I heard Abū Tamīmah narrating from a man who rode behind the Prophet ﷺ that the Prophet’s donkey stumbled.
The man said:
“May Satan perish!”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not say: ‘May Satan perish.’ For when you say, ‘May Satan perish,’ he grows in stature and says, ‘By my power, I caused him to fall.’ But when you say, ‘In the Name of Allah,’ he shrinks until he becomes like a fly.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (20592), from Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar, from Shuʿbah, from ʿĀṣim.
Regarding:
“From among the jinn and mankind,”
al-jinnah is a collective noun referring to the jinn.
The stronger view is that this verse explains the preceding words:
“Who whispers into the hearts of mankind.”
Thus, the whisperers include devils from among both humans and jinn.
Allah says:
“And thus We have made for every prophet enemies—devils from mankind and jinn—inspiring one another with decorative speech as deception.” [Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:112]
The Commentary on the Noble Qur’an Has Been Completed by the Help of Allah, the Exalted, and His Excellent Granting of Success
My methodology in this tafsīr was:
Interpreting the Qur’an by means of the Qur’an itself in important places.
Interpreting the Qur’an by authentic hadiths.
Interpreting the Qur’an by what has been authentically established from the noble Companions where the verse can reasonably bear that interpretation.
Interpreting the Qur’an according to what is understood from the Arabic language during the era of the Qur’an and afterward.
As for what is mentioned in books of tafsīr from the statements of some scholars concerning verses related to matters of the unseen—such as exact dimensions of length and width, degrees of severity and lightness, abundance and scarcity, comparisons with things in this world or assertions that there is no resemblance, and similar matters—I have refrained from including them.
This is because such claims amount to speculation concerning the unseen and have no evidence from the Qur’an or Sunnah.
Likewise, concerning the histories of past nations, whatever is established in the Qur’an, the Sunnah, or reliable historical documents, I have mentioned.
Whatever has no foundation in the Qur’an, Sunnah, or reliable documentation, I have omitted.
I did not expand extensively upon the verses dealing with legal rulings, because those matters were discussed in their appropriate places elsewhere in the book.
After the primary hadith collections with connected chains, I relied upon the major foundational works of tafsīr, namely:
Tafsīr ʿAbd al-Razzāq — died 210 AH.
Tafsīr Ibn Jarīr al-Ṭabarī — died 310 AH.
Tafsīr Ibn Abī Ḥātim — died 327 AH.
Tafsīr al-Baghawī — died 516 AH.
Tafsīr al-Qurṭubī — died 671 AH.
Tafsīr Ibn Kathīr — died 774 AH.
Tafsīr al-Saʿdī — died 1376 AH.
Aḍwāʾ al-Bayān by al-Shinqīṭī — died 1393 AH.
A mention of other tafsīr works from which commentators and hadith commentators have quoted includes the following:
1. Tafsīr of Muḥammad ibn al-Sāʾib al-Kalbī — died 146 AH
He was accused of unreliability.
It is reported that during his final illness he said to his companions:
“Everything I narrated to you from Abū Ṣāliḥ—from Ibn ʿAbbās—is false.”
2. Tafsīr of al-Suddī al-Ṣaghīr — died 146 AH
He narrated from al-Kalbī.
His name was Muḥammad ibn Marwān al-Suddī, and he too was accused of lying, like his teacher al-Kalbī.
3. Tafsīr of al-Suddī al-Kabīr
He was Ismāʿīl ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Suddī, who died around 137 AH.
He was considered ṣadūq—generally truthful—and I have quoted some tafsīr reports from him.
Al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Ḥajar said:
“He was a Kūfan and generally truthful, but he compiled his tafsīr from multiple routes, including from Abū Ṣāliḥ from Ibn ʿAbbās, from Murrah ibn Sharāḥīl from Ibn Masʿūd, and from a number of the Companions and others. He combined all of their narrations together, so the reports of reliable narrators could not be distinguished from those of weak narrators. Al-Suddī met no Companion except Anas ibn Mālik. He is also sometimes confused with al-Suddī al-Ṣaghīr, who was mentioned previously.”
See: Muqaddimat al-ʿUjāb fī Bayān al-Asbāb (1/211).
4. Tafsīr of al-Rabīʿ ibn Anas ibn Ziyād al-Bakrī — died 140 AH
Part of it is narrated from Abū al-ʿĀliyah with a disconnected chain, while in other places he does not name his source.
5. Tafsīr of Zayd ibn Aslam — died 136 AH
It is narrated from him by his son ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, who is considered weak by hadith scholars.
6. Tafsīr of Muqātil ibn Sulaymān — died 150 AH
Hadith scholars accused him of lying and abandoned his narrations, and he was also accused of anthropomorphism.
7. Tafsīr of Ismāʿīl ibn Abī Ziyād al-Shāmī, Judge of Mosul
I did not find the year of his death.
In al-Taqrīb, he is listed among the eighth generation, whose members died before 200 AH.
He is considered abandoned in hadith, and scholars accused him of lying.
8. Tafsīr of Yaḥyā ibn Sallām al-Maghribī — died 200 AH
It contains many objectionable and anomalous reports.
9. Tafsīr of Mūsā ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Thaqafī
I did not find the year of his death.
He was accused of lying.
Ibn Ḥibbān said in al-Majrūḥīn (2/175):
“He fabricated a book of tafsīr upon Ibn Jurayj from ʿAṭāʾ from Ibn ʿAbbās. He compiled it from the words of al-Kalbī and Muqātil ibn Sulaymān, then falsely attached it to Ibn Jurayj from ʿAṭāʾ from Ibn ʿAbbās. Ibn ʿAbbās never narrated it, ʿAṭāʾ never heard it from Ibn ʿAbbās, and Ibn Jurayj never heard it from ʿAṭāʾ.”
10. Tafsīr of Sunayd, al-Ḥusayn ibn Dāwūd — died 226 AH
His tafsīr is acceptable and contains no major problem.
I did not quote from these tafsīr works because they contain extremely weak and objectionable hadiths, as well as Isrāʾīliyyāt narratives, with the exception of the tafsīr of al-Suddī al-Kabīr and the tafsīr of Sunayd in certain places.
And success is from Allah alone.
